2018
Roger Coombs passed away in his sleep at Good Hope Hospital on the 6th February 2018 aged 83; he had not been well during the last 2 years of his life and his Obituary by Peter follows below: -
Obituary to Roger Coombs – Life Member
An Outstanding Wicket-keeper
Roger Coombs was born on the 23rd September 1934 in Sutton Coldfield and went to Hanley Castle a boarding school for boys in Worcestershire.  During his school’s summer holidays he would be seen sitting on a bench between the two grounds in Rectory Park watching cricket with his cricket kit in its bag beside him; in the hope that a player would be late arriving.  Lateness was something the 1st team captain, Pat Bowerbank, would not tolerate.  Like in all story books, one day a player failed to arrive on time to take the field, so Pat enquired whether Roger had his kit with him and, with his positive response, Roger was told “Get you kit on you are playing”.  So, Roger made his cricket debit for Sutton in the 1st team at the age of 16 – 17 years in 1950 or 51.
Roger Coombs
Having played cricket at school, he had learned the hard way, from his school sports master about always turning out with clean and well-prepared kit.  He was once dropped from the school team as they were about to take the field, because he had not whitened his pads.  From then on Roger was an extremely smartly turned-out cricketer throughout his playing career.
In 1954 Roger received his National Service call up papers to join the army and was sent to learn to drive a 10-ton truck before being posted to Germany and based in the Berlin area.  During the next two years he not only drove around Germany, he also became a very important player in both the regiment’s cricket and hockey teams.  Back in “civvy street” in September 1956, Roger resumed his association with the Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club for the start of the 1957 season, playing most of his cricket in the 2nd team.
For the past fifty years the club had possessed wicket-keepers of the highest class in club cricket, and this period was no exception.  Roger would have gained a place in almost any club's 1st team, but with Sutton Coldfield he was unfortunate in that he had to play "second fiddle" first to Geoff Pritchett and then to Peter Lowe.  It was only when the Warwickshire County Cricket Club were trying to persuade Peter to sign professional terms that Roger was allowed frequent opportunities to wear the pads and gloves.  Peter was moved by his employer, ICI, to a job in the Bristol area and in 1966 Roger became the regular 1st team wicket keeper.
Through the early 1960’s Roger had featured as a very capable batsman finishing in 1960 & 61 seasons with the 2nd team highest aggregate batting runs; totaling 657 and 619 runs respectively.  As the 2nd team’s wicket keeper Roger had established a club’s record in 1961 when claiming 76 victims behind the stumps during the season, a record that still stands today in 2018.  However, with Peter’s departure, Roger became the 1st team wicket keeper for the next 10 season.  In 1971 when he was elected 1st team captain, he could not be persuaded to relinquish the gloves to James McDowall a Cambridge Wicket-keeper Blue who had joined the club.
Roger was an outstanding wicket-keeper for Sutton Coldfield for over 30 years and regularly had 50 victims a season and in his career had close on a total of 900 victims.  He would have clearly made the 1,000 mark if the umpires standing at square leg had been as quick with their eye as Roger was with his reaction to the batsman’s back foot being lifted.
Roger regularly toured with both the Warwickshire Pilgrims and the Warwickshire Imps to Eastbourne and Sidmouth respectively.  He also tours with the Mayflies to the Gliffaes Hotel playing clubs around the Abergavenny area.  The highlight of Roger’s playing career was to play at the famous Wanderers Stadium in Johannesburg in 1975 when he was selected by the Midland Club Cricket Conference to tour South Africa as the team’s wicket-keeper batsman.  Because of Apartheid the tour went under the Kingfisher name.
He was also the ground secretary from 1974 until to about 2000 and, retiring from playing around 1980, and took on the white coat and umpired with the second team for several seasons.  Roger was elected a Life Member in 1989
One can’t conclude without referring to the fact that Roger joined the Sutton Coldfield Hockey Club in September 1956 playing in the 1st team until 1974.  He rejoined the Sutton club after a short spell playing for Kings Heath HC, but the drink and drive law became too much of a challenge so he returned to Sutton playing in the social 4th Xl until he retired in the early 1980’s.
To sum up Roger sporting prowess; he was a hard but honest competitor, played to the best of his ability and in the right sprit, and was the most amicable man in the bar after a game.
Roger passed away in his sleep at Good Hope Hospital on the 6th February 2018 aged 83, leaving two devoted daughters, Laura and Helen.
By Peter Bell
Annual Dinner –
The Club's Annual Dinner was held on Friday the 9th February 2018 at the Moor Hall Golf Club and was attended by a 100 members and guests with 10 letters of congratulation received by the President expressing enjoyment of the event.
The proceedings were compered by our President, James Ross, who ask all to stand for a minute silence in respect of a long serving and life member, Roger Coombs who died a few days earlier.  After an excellent dinner the £200 'stand-up-bingo' prize was won by    Richard Abell who graciously donated it back to the Club
Michael Mackey won the “Clubman of the Year Award” with Guran Ballagan receiving the “Carol Hansford Award” and the “Terry Matthews Award” going to Mustafa Ali.  The “Junior Player of the Year” was won by James Lunn.
The formal event was concluded by Paul Greetham, Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club & Warwickshire C.C.C’s Development Manager interviewing the County Cricket Club’s Sport Director, Ashley Giles MBE, which was followed by a comedian, Adger Brown, from the South of England, who entertain us royally.
John Howard
John was elected a Life Member of the SC Cricket Club on Thursday 17th May 2018, during a party attended by some 90 members and friends celebrating John’s sixtieth birthday, when he was presented with a citation by the President, James Ross in recognition of his work for the Club.
Sutton Coldfield CC Vets Xl
The Club under the management of Mike Hepburn and the captaincy of Andy Biddle enter the Elford Vets T20 Cricket League with evening games starting at 6 pm, with teams from Aston Unity, Four Oaks Saints, Elford, Nuneaton, Sutton Coldfield and Walmley CCs’ for players over the age of 40 years.
Two Centurions
A record for the Club when two players both scored centuries in a 4th team game against Walmley 5th team on the lower ground on Saturday 19th May.  Silva Jayahanthan opened the batting and scored 104 before being LBW, Sergel Weerasuriya batting at No. 3 scored 140 not out when the 4th team declared on 321 runs for 5 wickets after 42 overs. Walmley in their reply scored 252 runs for 4 wickets at the end of a 48 over game.  Sutton had a winning draw with 15 points against Walmley’s 10 points.
Obituary to Andy Biddle
An Outstanding Sportsman
1965 - 2018
Andrew Robert Charles Biddle, 'Bidds', joined Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club (SCCC) as a junior member in 1978. Initially as a scorer for senior teams before going on to play regularly. His openness, passion and lack of shyness soon endeared him to his team mates and earned the respect of opponents. He had a winning mentality but always competed within the spirit of the game. In his mid-twenties he became a regular member of Sutton 1st X1 as a wicket keeper batsman. Andrew's prowess as a wicket keeper led to him being asked to play for Worcestershire CCC 2nd X1. An opportunity he turned down to his regret in later life.
The highlight of Andrew's playing career was in 1993 when SCCC were crowned league champions. He was planning a 25th anniversary celebration later this year.
Andy Biddle
Very sadly he will be the one person unable to attend an event he would have greatly cherished.
As a batsman Andrew's great strength was his skill at working the ball into gaps and using his speed between the wickets to keep the scoreboard ticking over. Being quicker than his team mates this inevitably resulted from time to time in run outs. In his penultimate match three weeks ago for the newly formed SCCC Veterans his eagerness to scamper a single resulted in one of his closest friends, sixty-four-year-old Michael Hepburn, complete with new knee, being run out by seven yards. Given his good nature grudges were never held. In his later career with the gauntlets discarded Andrew revealed himself to be an outstanding fielder with a very safe pair of hands.
Apart from playing for Sutton other clubs represented were, Warwickshire Imps, The Civil Service and Mayflies. In 1983 along with his close friend Richard Abell, Andrew organised the SCCC tour to Cambridge which ran successfully for several years.
The grind of the working week was a bit of anathema to Andrew and apart from playing sport his real vocation lay in the coaching of youngsters at cricket and football. He was very supportive of youth sport and it gave him the opportunity to give back with interest what he had taken out as a player. His natural enthusiasm and connectivity rubbed off on his charges. He took on the role of coaching SCCC Under-10s and saw them through to fourteen. To his great credit many of them are now playing in SCCC senior teams. His football coaching skills were equally adept and took him to Australia to coach youngsters.
Cricket wasn't Andrew's only passion. He played football with great distinction for Sutton Town, Paget Rangers, Boldmere St Michael's, Parkfield, Sutton United, Halfway FC, Brookvale and The Civil Service. The pinnacle of his career came whilst appearing in the FA Cup for Sutton Town v Bolton Wanderers and laying on the Sutton goal in a 2-1 defeat shown on the Match of the Day programme. Had work not been so intrusive he would have had time to be a very capable golfer.
At Bishop Vesey's Grammar School, he was a formidable hockey player, and this sporting talent resulted in him being recognised as Sutton Coldfield Sports Personality of the Year in the 1990s.
Life off the pitch progressed at the same pace with Andrew always at the forefront of any gathering, however informal, with team mates past and present. A love of Carling or Argentinian Malbec ensured these meetings were always convivial and full of banter. Nobody however close was spared a lighted hearted quip, and if the boot was on the other foot the response was always swift and inevitably amusing. That quickness of thought was shown in another understated talent, that of the written word.
My thoughts and sentiments about Andrew's passing will be shared by so many. Andrew had a very caring nature and giving to charity played an important part in his life. But above all else, we offer deepest condolences to his family. Mother Pat, sister Nicola, brother Richard, three children that he adored and thought the world of, Vicki, Lauren and Saul, and to Lyn, Diane and Suzanne.
Andrew you will sorely missed and will always be in our hearts. It is difficult coming to terms at your sudden passing at such a young age. Life will never be quite the same for your family, so many friends, acquaintances and colleagues. Rest assured we won't let you down and we will give you the very best of send offs which you so thoroughly deserve.
Goodbye for now and God bless you.
James Ross, President
Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club
www.suttoncoldfieldobserver.co.uk    Friday 29th June 2018
An emotional tribute to a Sutton `legend'
By NICK HORNER News Reporter
AN EMOTIONAL tribute has been paid to a Sutton Coldfield sporting 'legend' ahead of his funeral next week by one of his closest friends.  Jez Dale, who describes himself as one of Andy Biddle's 'thousands of friends; urged the town to attend Bids' funeral at Holy Trinity Parish Church next Friday, July 6.
Andy, who played more than 500 times for Sutton Coldfield Town FC and for Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club, died on Friday, June 8, following a heart attack while playing an over-40s cricket match two days before.
Jez, for whom Andy was Best Man at his wedding, said: "Next Friday, the town of Sutton Coldfield has the chance to say a fond farewell to one of its sporting legends.
'Andy Biddle, or 'Bids' as he was affectionately known, was a loyal servant to both the football club and cricket club that bears the town's name. He played nearly 500 times for Sutton Town Football Club and even more than that for Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club.  "He loved both clubs and showed them huge loyalty when he could have chosen to play at an even higher standard.  "That longevity and loyalty has meant he has become affectionately known to so many as 'Mr. Sutton Coldfield:  "He was an exciting and naturally talented player, whether that was on the left wing of the football pitch, or keeping wicket or as a batsman on the cricket pitch. He could turn a game of either in his team's favour with a flash of brilliance. You always wanted Bids on your team.  "However, it wasn't just his loyalty and his skill that made Bids special; he was a huge asset in the dressing room. His witty, intelligent banter was renowned and he built the camaraderie and team spirit that is essential in successful teams.
"He was a heartbeat of all the teams he played for and, having played alongside him, I often felt he loved being in the dressing room just as much as he did being on the pitch.  "He was mischievous, charming and there was rarely a dull moment when you were in Bids' company. He had an infectious personality and that is what drew people to him. He was the connector that brought different friend groups together and he was the glue that kept us all together.  "It was his love of sport and his love for his friends that caused him several weeks ago to enter a Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club veterans' team into a midweek league. He brought together many ex-team-mates, who had long hung up their cricket boots, and convinced many of them to come out of retirement.
"He set up a WhatsApp group with over 50 ex-players and for several weeks It 'pinged' constantly as he bantered with the group and convinced people to play, against their better judgement. Many people only agreed to turn out, as they put it, 'because it was for Bids.'  "It was fun to hear that constant 'ping, ping, ping' and to see him once more in his element, picking the team, joking with everyone and bringing old friends together.
"Then, a few games into the season, I was working late and the phone's pings continued incessantly for an hour or so. It was the night of a game and I expected it to be news of another batting collapse or a huge score being put together by the opposition.  "Sadly, those were not the reason. Bids had suffered a heart attack trying to turn a single into a quickly-run two. He never regained consciousness, despite valiant efforts from players and paramedics, and he passed away 48 hours later in hospital surrounded by family and close friends.
"He was just 52 years old, but we take comfort from the fact that his last waking moment was in a place he loved, doing what he loved."  Jez added: "Bids lived life to the full and we are going to ensure he leaves us in a fitting way and we will be at the club late into the evening. His children have asked if those who attend have a photo of them with Bids, please would they bring a copy of it with a message or their favourite story written on the back.  "The word 'legend' is used too frequently, in my opinion, but it is so justified in this circumstance. He was a great footballer, a hugely talented cricketer, a fabulous team-mate, a good bloke and a great friend to so many of us.
"Our lives were enriched for having spent time with him and, while we are all hurting at present, we know we will come to appreciate how lucky we were to have known him."
The funeral starts at 11am at Holy Trinity Parish Church, and was followed by a wake at his beloved Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club.
A tribute to Bids by Richard Able
Mr. Magnificent
Teenage friend a very special mate
Friendship began at the old school gate
He’d often start a line and say
“I’m not being funny Abe”, that was his way
I was calm ….he was not
He was sparkling, fizzy, a bottle of pop
A son, a brother and a father to three
Unwavering friend, outspoken honesty
From the early days of our youth
Our friendship was forged on respect and truth
Sometimes he’d wobble with personal doubt
As friends we'd sit and work it out
And if ever I lent him something of mine
I’d never see it again…but that was fine
To his very core he was a Sutton Man
But let’s not forget his beloved West Ham
At school he was of diminutive stature
Countered by his kaleidoscopic character
For his popularity was abundantly clear
Voted Sutton Coldfield Sports Personality of the year
With his sporting passions of football and cricket
He had amazing pace on the turn and at the wicket
He was many things to many folks
Player, teammate and a coach
A passionate and competitive nature
Losing simply did not feature
Annual highlights in days of yore
Were the golden times on cricket tour
He played with heart and soul in every football game
And when he considered the referee was to blame
He’d protest his grievances loud and clear
…and the inevitable red card would appear.  (again)…
So many stories deserve a mention
Not least the tale of his police detention
His spontaneity and joi de vivre
Could find trouble…but then reprieve
For he was fleet of foot and quick of wit
Trouble? He’d always wriggle out of it
Except that time on our Greek vacation
And his 24 hours of incarceration
Innocent beers in a local bar
A fracas broke out from afar
Cops swooped in as we looked on
Then it all went very wrong
He was warned to cease his Z cars humming
Woodsy and I didn’t see what was coming
Impressed with his own jaunty wit
He carried on humming it
And so my friends I can attest
To the crime that lead to his Greek arrest
This became a legendary tale
Of our Kavos vacation and his 24 hours in jail
He lived his life in technicolour
Dramatic funny, there was no other
Cricket, chats, mirth and beers
A memory box of over 40 years
And I will forever lift the lid
On Mr Magnificent …the unforgettable…Bids.

All-rounder Aaron Thomason has signed a new contract with Warwickshire County Cricket Club.
Aaron, now aged 21, has agreed a new two-year deal and will remain at Edgbaston for the 2019 campaign.  He has made 16 List A appearances for Warwickshire and played 28 T20 games for Birmingham Bears since his debut in 2014.
"Aaron has been a consistent performer in white-ball cricket for the last two years and he can make a serious push to play in the Championship side," said sport director Ashley Giles.

With the Birmingham & District Premier Cricket League reverting to only two leagues, a Premier and 1st Division for the 2019 season, all the Second & Third Division teams plus all 2nd Xl teams will be moved back into their respective county league structures.
Harborne who won the 2nd Division will replace Wellington who were relegated from the 1st Division and will play in the Shropshire League.  C & R Hawkins, although runners up in Division 2, their ground did not pass the B&DPCL ground assessment which allowed Brockhampton to remain in the 1st Division.
Lee Thomason finish 6th in the Second Division averages with an average of 43.5 and a total of 739 runs and a highest score of 116 not out.  Tom Eustace finished the season with an average of 35.0 and a total of 525 runs and a highest score of 80.  Dinushka Nawarathnage, our overseas player from Sri Lanka, scored 521 runs with an average of 34.7 and a top score of 117 runs.  He was also the Club’s leading wicket taker with 36 wickets from 221 overs at an average of 36.9 and with a best performance of 5 for 14 runs.
Jack Smith finished in joint 14 place in the fielding award with 10 catches and 1 run out.

Defibrillator
Since the tragic death due to a heart attack of Andy Biddle while playing cricket last summer the Club have acquired a defibrillator which is available to all in the Rectory Park and is affixed to the outside of the Club’s pavilion.
Annual Dinner – 2019
The Club's Annual Dinner was held on Friday the 2nd February 2019 at the Moor Hall Golf Club and was attended by about 100 members and their guests.
The proceedings were well compered by our President, James Ross and after an excellent dinner the £200 'stand-up-bingo' prize, hosted by Mark Hoath was won by Reg Brassington for the 2nd time in 3 years
Jim Hobbis, the retiring treasurer after 4 years in office was awarded the “Clubman of the Year Award”.  Henry Roper receiving the “Carol Hansford Award” and the “Terry Matthews Award” going to Felix Haines.  The “Junior Player of the Year was presented to Jacob Squire-Wood.
A new trophy “10 Not Out” which was initially awarded to Peter Bell at the 2017 dinner, but was not subsequently awarded at the 2018 dinner.  However, at this year’s dinner it had been mounted into a handmade Oak Stand by Phil Sunderland, (The father of our senior barmaid, Hannah) commissioned by Peter Bell and presented posthumously and jointly to Roger Coombs and Andy Biddle.  Both being long serving members of the Club who both sadly passed away last season.
The formal event was an interview between Paul Greetham, Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club & Warwickshire C.C.C’s Development Manager and the recently retired former England and Nottinghamshire County Cricket Club’s wicket keeper and the new Repton School Sports Director and England selector, Chris Read.
Grace before dinner was said by Michael Dean, who captained the 1st team during the 1990’s

2019 Playing Season
The 2019 season got off to a bad start with our 1st team having great difficulty in getting their strongest side out, and then into June the weather was atrocious with many games being cancelled and we had a group of travelers (Gypsies) arriving for a short stay in our car park so we had a lock down while the park management and police moved them on, although it would have been too wet for any cricket to be played.  During that weekend our Ground Chairman, Mike Hepburn was rushed into the Good Hope Hospital, under surveillance with a possible septic arthritis to a knee that he had surgery recently.  The President James Ross took over the responsibilities for the ground, which included driving the tractor. James sent out the following report as the Club’s acting groundsman.
The President, James Ross
Learning to drive the tractor & cut the outfield
“I said yesterday that I would go off piste (jokingly).  My intention being I would cut into every last corner and slightly beyond being as you know I’m a stickler for attention to detail.
I arrived this morning to be told by Jolly John (Howard)who had read the email not with a good morning but a not to go off piste.  Off to training by Colin (Greetham) and blow me down the tractor would not start.  A dead battery, I’m sure Heppers (Mike Hepburn) had organised that so he could not be usurped.  So, I went to Halfords on Tyburn Road for a replacement.  An hour and a half later after coaching I was about to set sail when a chap who had been talking to Lee Thomason on the square approached me and said do you remember who I am?  I said yes, you’re Ian Nuttall (A fast bowler in the 1970’s- 80’s) and we had a great catch up.  He sends his best to you, Neil (Houghton) and Belly (Peter Bell) whilst ruminating about those no more.  Colin by now getting frustrated wanting to get me away.
Conversation over I was off and covered every corner of both grounds, some of it twice for 2.5 hours.  Incredibly therapeutic and off piste.
I wore Audrey’s prescribed headgear for the occasion, i.e., hoodie because of the flying detritus from the mowers.  Unfortunately driving at pace, it kept blowing off.  Net result hopefully after a good shower most of the flying dog shit has been removed from the back of my head and inside my ears. I think next time out I need Biggles goggles and Guy Gibson headgear.
Amusingly getting into a state of the art of power steered of my Mercedes driving down the drive my steering wasn’t great. Bouncing all over the place!
Looking forward to doing it again on Friday.  First cut of the gardens tomorrow since fall.  Gosh they have grown. Mundane after today.
James (Club President)

Ex Junior Member of Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club - Tom Banton.

Banton was named PCA Young Player of the Year last week after hitting 533 runs in the County Championship, 454 in the Royal London One-Day Cup and 549 in the Vitality Blast.
Tom Banton, Ben Stokes and Sophie Ecclestone picked up the premier prizes at the 50th PCA Awards and Tom told the Heat's official website: "If you had asked me at the start of the summer would I imagine it going like this, I would have probably had a laugh and suggested you weren't serious”.  Now Somerset’s wicketkeeper-batsman Tom Banton will play for Brisbane Heat during the upcoming Big Bash League campaign.  Tom has also been named in England's T20 squad for their upcoming tour of New Zealand.
Tom Banton's sensational summer has earned him an England call-up and selection for The Hundred with Welsh Fire. Ahead of the live draft, we spoke exclusively to the Somerset star.
The 20-year-old was one of the emerging talents from the 2019 season - scoring 549 runs in the Vitality Blast at an average of 42.23 and a stunning strike-rate of 161.43.
He also scored a pivotal half-century in Somerset's Royal London One-Day Cup triumph at Lord's in May and is now reaping the rewards for his prodigious performances.

I can't wait to be. - honest!”
Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club
Annual General Meeting
Held on Tuesday 15th October 2019 at 7.30pm
Apologies: Rob Haines, Jim Hobbis, Tracey Lawrence (TR), Matt Appleby, Stuart Russell (STR), Paul Heron, Andy Neal, Tim Cave, Simon Matthews
Attendees: James Ross (JR), Steve Russell (SR), Roy Hopwood (REH), John Howard (JH), Colin Greetham (CG) Dave Hands, Nick Baker (NB), Peter Bell, Mike Hepburn, Tom Finn, Graham Clark (CLG), Lee Thomason (LT), Jack Smith (JAS), Harry Williams-Lucas, Neil Houghton (NH), John Robinson, Michael Mackey, Geoff Gray, Naser Ali, Tom Eustace.
Before the commencement of the meeting JR stated that, as Matt Appleby is unable to attend, there will be no presentation (Item 14).
Approval of 2018 Minutes
These were approved. Proposed by Nick Baker and Seconded by Jack Smith
Matters arising from the previous AGM minutes
There were no outstanding matters.
Chairman’s Report
Looking back at senior cricket in 2019, it is fair to say the season has been disappointing. With the re-structure of the Birmingham League both 1sts and 2nds found themselves in the Warwickshire League. We began the season hopeful that the 1’s would compete at the top end of the table. However, difficulties with availability and several key players struggling for form meant we spent most of the season in the bottom half finishing eventually 8th. For the 2’s the season was worse. Losing their first 6 Win/Lose/Draw games would mean that even a spirited performance in the second half of the season could not prevent relegation. The 3’s & 4’s performed admirably given major problems at times with availability with the 3’s finishing 3rd and the 4’s 5th in their respective leagues. We will investigate with the league the possibility of the 3’s moving up a league with the reshuffle that will follow several teams dropping out of the league.
John will, I am sure, have his thoughts about the season, but it appears to me that 2 factors had a major impact and are both in need of addressing by the new committee. The first of these I have mentioned previously, and this is availability. This proved a constant headache to the selection committee. Cricket takes up much of a players Saturday and Sunday and the reality is players have many other calls on their time and a wide range of other attractions on which to spend time and money. Cricket needs to look at its offer as the sport is being challenged at all levels. Its long period now off terrestrial television has undoubtedly had an effect in lowering its place it in the public consciousness. As a club we may have to accept that the days when players played every Saturday and Sunday are a thing of the past. Neil Houghton told me earlier in the season that when he was captain one season, he only used 14 players. I suspect our captains can only dream of such selection consistency.
This then brings me to my second factor and that is depth of skills within the club. Lack of availability has revealed the unreadiness of many players to make the step up to the team above. At the beginning of the season, we sought to bring in a coach to work with the senior teams but were unsuccessful in our search. Coaching and planning clear player development pathways are a crucial issue I believe for the next committee and in particular looking to utilise the investment the club has made in supporting qualification of our own coaches. Matt will cover this more in his presentation. We are not a club who wants or is able to bring in paid players so growing the skills of our own is crucial. In addition to skills, I believe better game management is required. I watched too many games lost at 2nd XI in particular by either a naïve approach to run chases – too many early wickets lost in an anxiety to score runs and/or not knowing when to stop chasing and take the draw points on offer.
A further challenge for the club is the issue of Sunday cricket where again we have forfeited too many games. I question the future of league cricket on a Sunday and maybe approaches such as the Bash/100 will indeed be the way this goes.
Having focused on a rather negative view of senior cricket, junior cricket at SCCC continues to flourish and my congratulations to all involved in another successful season. I attended the junior presentation in July, and it was great to see so many young players and their parents in attendance. We continue to see young players working their way into our senior teams with players such as Felix Haines and Ollie Nimmo both having the opportunity to play at 1st XI level which is fantastic. Keeping these talented players and others involved and providing them with a clear pathway into the senior teams is I believe the big focus over the next few years.
Last year, I talked about the need for the club to pay attention to its offer to existing and prospective members as we play cricket in a very competitive area of the county with clubs such as Walmley, Streetly and Saints all able to offer potential players a good level of cricket. Each of those clubs is looking at its offer with Walmley in particular looking at and changing the structure of the club, to be a Sports Club rather than just a cricket club. As a committee we agreed that it was important that we too took some time out to look critically at what type of a club we want to be moving forward and how we might remain an attractive proposition to those looking to play cricket here. To that end, in March we commissioned Matt Appleby to undertake a Strategic Review of the club. Matt brings extensive experience as a Sports Consultant to this task and has over the summer devoted many hours to this, interviewing people from across the club and conducting a member’s survey which had an excellent response. Matt presented his findings to the Committee at the beginning of the month and will do the same at the end of this meeting. The Committee were largely supportive of his recommendations but as Dave Hands commented “the devil is in the detail” and this will require an increased involvement of people from across the club if we are to successfully take these ideas forward. I would encourage people to listen to Matts proposals with an open mind as they reflect the view across the club and where possible volunteer to get involved.
A few people put a lot of work into keeping this club going and providing a great place to play cricket. We need more people to get involved. This is not necessarily involvement in the general committee but playing a part in helping identify potential sponsors, helping on specific events etc. I would particularly like to see more players involved in the club not just turning up to play – more give and less take. Don’t just leave it to the few!
Finally, as some of you know I have decided to step down as Chairman. Being Chairman for the last 3 years has been a great honour and something I never thought would be possible when I joined the club 17 years ago. I believe that the club requires fresh energy and leadership to address the challenges ahead and I believe Nick Baker will provide that having done an excellent job with the junior section. Having held both the roles of Chair and Treasurer over the last year, I have volunteered to continue in the Treasurer role, and I am pleased that I have been nominated & seconded. I look forward to continuing to support the club on its future journey. Can I thank all members of the committee for their support during my 3 years and I look forward to supporting Nick.
JR thanked SR for all his hard work and dedication over the past years as Chairman.
Secretary’s Report
One of the most important aspects of our Club is that we are all volunteers and, as Secretary, I’ve once again been able to work with both Senior and Junior sections and seen the hard work and commitment put in by Team Managers/Captains, Coaches, grounds staff, parents and, of course ‘volunteers’ who do so much to help the functioning of the Club.
Administration and ‘paperwork’ are usually regarded as a nuisance and ‘something that has to be done’ but it necessary and, indeed essential, for the functioning of the Club. The new membership forms that were introduced this year have, despite being very detailed, been well received. However, a new, briefer and simplified junior renewal membership form will be introduced at the start of the new season and we will be looking into making all other membership forms in a similar format. I must stress upon everyone the importance of completing the necessary form in order to be a registered member of the Club and also to ensure that up-to-date contact details are held so that all communications are received and in order to comply with the General Data Protection Register.
Communications within the Club to members and to external agencies have improved from last year, but could still be better, we will endeavour to improve them by establishing new procedures. The introduction of a weekly Newsletter providing items such as team results/fixtures and forthcoming events, coupled with dedicated WhatsApp groups has helped enormously in providing everyone with up-to-date information and has allowed them to take an active part in what’s going on within the Club. As the Club is your Club, so too is the Newsletter your Newsletter and your views are a valuable contribution to it. The website is undergoing an update and will be refreshed over the coming week ready for the start of the new season.
The Senior Management Committee has been meeting in order to organise the running of our senior and junior teams and to undertake administrative work, including financial and secretarial matters as well the day to day running and maintenance of the pitches and the Clubhouse. I would like to thank everyone involved for their hard work. I would like to thank Steve for the hard work and commitment he’s put in as Chairman of the Club and for continuing to support the Club by remaining in the role of Treasurer. I’d like to extend a warm welcome to Nick Baker, who from tonight, takes on the role of Chairman and also to Rob Haines as Chair of the Junior Section.  I look forward to supporting them both not just as Secretary but as a member of the Club.
Yet another playing season is over. However, the work behind the scenes continues and from tonight a new Senior Management Committee will be elected bringing new, fresh ideas to the Club. In particular the Committee will continue to work on the proposals formulated through Matt’s Strategic Review and to take any decisions made to the Membership in order to successfully move the Club forward. It’s unfortunate that Matt was not able to attend tonight to present his presentation, but I’m sure the Chairman and Committee will make it available to all as soon as possible.
I know this has been said many times, but a Club like ours really does rely heavily on the goodwill of those who support us - the players, parents, volunteers, team managers and coaches, all of whom undertake many tasks to maintain the Club and its infrastructure. To them I say “Thank you for all your hard work, commitment and for being member of our Club.
Treasurer’s Report
We have had a solid year financially. As you can see from the report, we budgeted for a small surplus of £1300 but have ended up with a surplus of £3355. This has been through a combination of increased income and good cost management.
Income
Major items for comment;
Subscriptions – In senior cricket we have with a lot of effort on the part of captains, John Howard and Stuart Russell ensured that all memberships were paid and match subs collected. It is still a real effort to collect annual subscriptions despite giving players various payment options and this is an approach we need to keep under review
Senior Match Fees are slightly down but there were at least 2 4th XI games where we had to forfeit and the same on Sundays.
Junior – whilst down on an ambitious budget the figure is up £1400 from last year
Donations and Sponsorship are both up though we are slightly down on the fixture card. Sponsorship is a key issue for us moving forward. Lanes have been an excellent supporter, but we cannot rely only on them moving forward. I know Matt will pick this up later.
Gift Aid – this is less than expected but we believe this may have been due to a mistake in our claim
Miscellaneous Income: The main items are reported in the report. Most notable were a £1000 rebate from the GPC and 2 ECB Grants obtained by Mike Hepburn. These are offset on the Expenses side (Ground Equipment & Cooker/Barbecue)
Expenditure
A number of items are below budget including Teas where we had budgeted for an external provider who pulled out. We were able to replace her and manage costs in this area. Thanks to John Howard.
Coaching costs – paid out less than we anticipated. This is however likely to be an area of significant increase next year given our determination to improve senior coaching provision.
Player Payment – often a contentious area but largely off-set by Sponsorships. Slightly above budget as we made an additional payment to Dinushka to allow him to change his flights as we did not need him for the play-offs
Ground Equipment – this includes payment to Mike Hepburn offset by £1000 from ECB
Ground Labour – 3 areas – Lee Thomason (£3,410); Phil Oliver (£3,498); Glendale (£1,100). Will need to look closely at this next season as Lee is stepping down from Groundsman and we may need to employ someone at an increased cost.
Miscellaneous Expenditure – This includes nearly £2,000 as cost for the dinner. The Barbecue & Cooker cost were offset by an ECB Grant & GPC Donation.
Looking ahead, we have a number of potential cost items next year that require us to focus on sources of income beyond Subscriptions and Donations. A focus is required on attracting new sponsors and where we can access Grants. We will incur probably higher Ground Labour costs and Coaching costs. It is important that those social members who joined this year, re-join and that the cricket committee and captains ensure all memberships and subs are collected
GC – Have Lanes agreed to continue with their sponsorship? JH – In contact with about this. SR – we really do need more sponsors.
NH – Was there a surplus on the Annual Dinner? SR – Yes, about £1,000.00p
Welfare Officer’s Report
This has been my first year of being Welfare Officer, so I have undergone training at Edgbaston in safeguarding and related issues. I have undertaken DBS initiation and verification duties for the club through ECB. A major issue this season has been ensuring all relevant staff are DBS checked...next season this will be pursued more vigorously before the season begins. All staff working with juniors have been directed to the ECB Safe hands website to familiarise themselves with ECB policies and procedures. There have been a couple of minor issues this season which have been resolved without difficulty. I have attended Friday night junior training and will continue to do so next season if I am re-elected.
JR – Thanked Tracey for taking on this role and for the hard work involved in getting it up and running.
Cricket Chairman’s Report
In one word, frustrating. The club as a whole has underachieved but suffered mostly at the hands of several playing members whose inconsistent availability affected and reflected in our ability to produce consistent results throughout the teams for a large part of the season. Due to availability in the first few weeks, we had to field weakened teams but also forfeit the first two 4th XI games which didn’t start the season well for us as a whole.
The 1st XI finished 8th but with some notable wins along the way. Having had a slow start they produced a spell during the middle of the season which took them well into the top half of the league only to fall away at the end, mainly due to lack of availability where at present we don’t have strength in depth.
Lee, Dinushka and Jack all performed well with the bat along with Jacob who played a few
mature and patient innings which helped provide the platform for good scores which we lacked in the early part of the season.
Dinny was good with the ball and in general bowled well, but both Jack’s, Reidy and Smith did well, along with Clarky, and Harry showed much potential when given the opportunity.
I thank Lee for his time as captain but with the emergence of Jack at the helm our fortunes seemed to change. It’s good to see some of the younger element perform well, i.e. Jacob and Harry and also debut performances from Olly behind the stumps, Felix and Tom Hurley at the end of the season, which bodes well for the future.
John Griffiths continues to want to support the 1sts with coaching when available and the improved turnout at nets seemed to help the senior teams both with attitude and performances.
Although the 2nd XI was relegated at the end of the season, their efforts in the second half give encouragement for next year. Nasser led by example and tried his heart out. We badly lack players who are prepared to work hard and consistently fail to score sufficient runs for themselves and for the team. When reasonable scores were achieved then the team had some success and can hopefully learn from their mistakes in the first half of the year. We must try and get them a coach and/or mentor who can work with them on match days and
Ensure they adhere to the disciplines that will achieve success.
We have a very good and varied bowling attack with a fair amount of experience. Martin Ball was fairly consistent, and Felix Haines showed his promise along with Nasser and Stuart and Dave Hansford when available showed he had good pace.
Sadly, to say, no top-order batsmen achieved 300 runs and that shows our problem. Everyone appreciates Naser’s commitment to the side and there are noticeable performances by some juniors who have been given their opportunity. We can only support Nasser as much as we can and hopefully selection headaches and form will improve next year
The 3rd XI has had a reasonably good season and finished third in the league. Again, reduced availability and batting disciplines have caused the team to suffer a potential promotion. Availability better as the 2nd half of the season went on But, the consistency in the play, particularly the batting is the main issue. Matt continues to work hard with the team in trying to get them focused. There are now one or two players from this team who are or will be challenging in higher teams very soon.
Again coaching, but also attendance at nets, needs to improve if we are to move this side up the leagues.  Juniors again are beginning to prosper in this side and will hopefully challenge in the coming seasons.
4th XI continued to be on the up and have given some good results in some high scoring games. The season started badly as I’ve already mentioned but when availability became better then performances started to come as well. 5th position was a most creditable position to finish considering the difficult start.
Whether it be Dave Hands, or Ian Hay, or Hemant in charge of the team they seem to have got the right spirit mixed with a good combination of youth and experience. Dave continues to persevere with his team and has done a fantastic job in getting sides out each and every week and just a few more points would’ve seen them finish in the top four. If you took away the penalty points and added just one bonus point from those two missed games half also. The first two matches of the season saw us lose 10 points where we may have picked up some points and that has hampered our position.
The Sunday side have had a mixed season finishing 6th in the league.
Dinny continues to support the team and scored a record 200* in one of the games.
Henry, Harry and Jacob then help make the core of the team with the rest
A very mixed side which gives opportunities to more junior members playing against senior opposition which put them in good stead for the future
To reiterate my comments are as of the last few months. We are now in a position where we really need to decide NOW where we go as a club. With the effort being put in, with more people contributing, but still needs to improve more as how we work as a unit, we ought to want to play at the highest level possible. Certainly, stay in the Warks Prem at least. Going forward that means we need to attract some new blood to the squads in general as well as to continue to fast track our own juniors and not waiting until the start of winter nets. We’ve actually started this at present with John Griffiths at Edgbaston doing an 8-week course with some of the juniors who we believe will play senior cricket next year.
At the end of the day, we agree and can all say we want better coaching etc. but we have to be realistic in that unless we look at bringing in more investment through sponsors our goals will be difficult to achieve.
JR thanked JH for his hard work in this capacity and for staying on in an acting capacity. Also, thanks to LT who will be standing down a Groundsman.
Junior Chairman’s Report
(Awaiting report)
Grounds Chairman’s Report
Following on from Saturday’s working party site screens have been put away, covers wrapped up etc.  The squares have had treatment, have been scarified, low spikes and seeded. There are areas which need further work. The squares will require cutting shortly.
Over the winter the mowers will require servicing together with the tractor and roller.
The nets will require further cleansing, brushing and netting repairs will be required.
Over the past number of years Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club have been extremely fortunate to be able to utilise the services of South African Dan and Lee Thomason. Times of course change as we are now at that junction. For those who turn up, attend and play on a Saturday much work is carried out during the week, unseen, and carried out at different times depending on the weather for example, in anticipating bad weather covers are put on. Watering during the week, cutting the squares, sometimes certainly twice a week. Marking pitches out for not only weekend games but midweek games and after junior games. Who is going to do all of this? The answer to that question is definitely not straight forward.
The outfield has been cut by a Company called Glendale accepted not brilliant, but the alternatives are very limited. There should be projects for the future. A complete re-vamp of the nets, the all-weather wicket on the lower ground is showing signs of distress. There should be a review of the utilisation of pitches so that more wickets are used certainly on the top ground and it is to be noted that only four adult wickets were used for the lower ground during the 2019 season.  There needs to be more flexibility and more junior games played on the top ground. There needs to be in my opinion a complete review of the utilisation of the facilities that we have and a review to cover the management arrangements of the ground completely.  We need to advertise for a groundsman.
By the end of October there will be the normal period of quietness where there is not very much that actually can be done to the ground.  As and when spiking the outfield, general repairs bit and bobs.
Once we come to February, we start all over again. How and that is the management’s predicament.  I would like to thank Jamie and Tiger for all their work this summer.  Colin for his advice and assistance. We need more volunteers.  It would be lovely to have more volunteers so that a system could be organised so that on a Friday for example there was a larger group with tasks allocated.  It will be lovely to see on Saturdays the pitches being marked out by members not players, a roller being available if required.  I see this at other clubs and do not really understand why we do not do this.
I mentioned at the start of this report that further work is required to be carried out on the squares particularly the top square where there are bare patches. This additional work will be carried out by the end of October.

Chapter XlX – 2020 - 2021
The Annual Dinner
The Dinner was held at Moor Hall Golf Club on Friday 7th February 2020 and was attended by around 100 Members, Friends and Guests and the function included the junior awards: -
Junior player of the year - went to Harry Williams- Lucas;
The Terry Matthews trophy- went to Tom Hurley:
The Carol Handsford award went to Luke Bishop
“The 10 - Not Out” trophy now mounted of an Oak Plinth, by Peter Bell was awarded to Mike Hepburn for outstanding services to the club, as an ex-1st team captain and Ground Chairman for over 10 years.
SUTTON COLDFIELD CRICKET CLUB
Grace by Michael Dixon
Lovers of cricket are we all gathered here, Looking back on what was a momentous year World Cup, Ashes and South Africa too, So much to fit into my grace here to you.
The first was the World Cup, could England prevail? No Hales at the top, a small test did he fail The group games were mixed, we finished just third, But in the semi, Edgbaston gave the Aussies 'the bird'
Then to Lords' the best game that there's been If you're a Kiwi, can't believe what you've seen Stokes and Buttler together got us chargin' Super over, and a win by the barest of margin.
The Ashes were next and the boys were all tired, Smith and Warner turned up as they hadn't been fired. Ben was the star, for England that is, The power and the glory that Sunday was his.
All this from a ban just two years ago, He picked himself up from what was such a low His batting and bowling and catches make us think We'd have definitely struggled if he'd gone to the clink!
The urn it was shared, though they keep it down under, Their bowling was great, all pace and some thunder, But England fought back and never did yield And oh how we laughed at Nathan Lyon's misfield!
To Africa finally to end off the year, Members from Sutton were there to give them a cheer, This year of all years shows that cricket's our love, We give thanks to those cricketing gods from above.
And to tonight, gathered here at Moor Hall To members and guests, we say welcome to all Jim is our speaker, a Bear through and through With Ian, our comic with some jokes for us too.
So have a good night with the best club in Sutton When it comes to a dinner we hit the right button.
Lord, we thank you, for good food, good wine and lively chat And keep us playing with a nice straight bat.
The Warwickshire County Cricket Club Head Coach, Jim Trouton, was interview by Paul Greetham which gave the audience a very good incite in how the County Club is looking to the future with a young very talent side but the loss of Ian Bell due to an injury through most of the 2019 season effect the Club’s performance. The return of comedian and Head Rat, Ian Richards was very entertaining and he had his usual dig at members of the club, including the Sutton Club Historian, when Ian expressed his concern when shaking his hand “That it was warmer than he had expected”. All good light-hearted fun and he gave everyone the opportunity for a good laugh.
Cricket 2020 with Covid-19
After a most successful Annual Cricket Club Dinner, we were all very optimistic and positive about the forthcoming cricket season. This was quickly dashed when Covid-19 arrived in the UK; A pneumonia of unknown cause, coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) is an infectious disease caused by severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus. It was first identified in December 2019 in Wuhan, China, and has since spread globally, resulting in an ongoing pandemic and as of 15 May 2020, more than 4.53 million cases have been reported across 188 countries and territories, resulting in more than 307,000 deaths.
More than 1.63 million people have recovered. Common symptoms include fever,  cough, fatigue, shortness of breath, and loss of smell and taste. While the majority of cases result in mild symptoms, some progress to acute respiratory distress syndrome, multi-organ failure, septic shock, and blood clots. The time from exposure to onset of symptoms is typically around five days but may range from two to fourteen days. The virus is primarily spread between people during close contact, most often via small droplets produced by coughing, sneezing, and talking. The droplets usually fall to the ground or onto surfaces rather than travelling through air over long distances. Less commonly, people may become infected by touching a contaminated surface and then touching their face. It is most contagious during the first three days after the onset of symptoms, although spread is possible before symptoms appear, and from people who do not show symptoms.
In early March 2020 the UK Government issued instructions and advice that included preventive measures which include frequent hand washing, maintaining physical distance from others, quarantine covering coughs, and keeping unwashed hands away from the face. In addition, the use of a face covering is recommended for those who suspect they may have the virus.
The Government also issued a stay-at-home policy to protect our NHS and save lives and agreed to pay 80% of all wages for those who laid off, with a request for all to work from home where possible. The advice was work from home, stay at home as much as possible, limit contact with other people, keep your distance if you go out keep 2-metres apart where possible.
Neither our Cricket Committee or the Ground Committee have been idle nor has our President who has been working on behalf of the club with both “Pen & Paint Brush”. No, it’s not a local pub. He wrote to all Life members and Vice Presidents advising on the precarious position we were in, with no cricket being played and no indication from the Government or the ECB of any relaxing in the restrictions or when cricket might be played, the bar and pavilion is in lockdown and no subscriptions coming in, we still have to pay our bills. The President explained, in a very well-constructed letter our situation, that we are eating into our reserves, and his intervention generated over £3,500 in donations.
He also informed them that the traditional fixture card for 2020 season is not being printed. The President also sent a personal letter of thanks to all those that made a financial contribution, via the postal service, expressing the Club’s appreciation of their generous response.
Notwithstanding that, he has managed to spend over some 30 hours painting the fence panels that run between the top ground score box and the edge of the coppice between the two grounds. However, within days of the President completing the painting strong winds in early May broke a big branch off an overhanging tree that broke the fence close to the score-box, so President had to remove the heavy branch, repair the fence and repaint the affected area.
The new fence
Our Chairman, Nick Baker, sent out a detailed circular letter to all members, initially sending condolences on behalf of the Club-to-club members who have lost family members to the virus and the impact the Covid-19 pandemic has had on us all.
He informed us that the Club’s Committee has held several video meetings to discuss how we manage the club and budget in light of a vastly reduced season. As a club, we still have to maintain the pitches and cover our ongoing fixed costs.
He thanked our, President James Ross, for his efforts in securing much valued donations. He went on to refer to the fact that Dave Hands has submitted an application to Sport England for a grant and have received a £7,000 grant. He also reported that the GPC (the Cricket and Hockey Clubs) had secured a £10,000 grant under the Small Businesses and Hospitality Group which will help with the pavilion - clubhouse overheads as we have no income being generated through the bar.
All Club’s committees are keeping a close eye on how restrictions may be eased, but we are guided by the ECB and Government advice as to when and how we can proceed. Until that point, we are trying to ensure the club and its playing facilities are ready to go. If any members are able to any support or ideas then please come forward.
Tragic Deaths
John Howard, The Pavilion Manager and Players Chairman sent his and the Club’s sincere condolence to one of our younger 3rd - 2nd team players, Ben Dunnington, whose Father Keith Dunnington, who worked at Queen Elizabeth Hospital in Tyne and Wear but died on the 19th April. Sadly, and tragically both Ben’s Grandparents, Lillian age 81 and Maurice aged 85 died within days of each other in May, just two weeks after their son Keith, all from Covid-19. Their deaths were wildly publicised in the National News and National Papers, with nurses, firefighters and well-wishers lined the streets of their home town.

Late Autumn 2020 - Working Party
Saturday 28th November 20. A very wet morning for some 40 members participating in a working party, removing the tonnes of leaves from both grounds before the winter set in. The age of the helpful members ranged between 8 to 66 years of age and when it was all done home-made cakes were available. The President & Chairman quoted “it was a great effort”.
Before
The last Leaves being Collected
It was a Great Effort and a Job Well Done!
2021
A New Variant COVID-19 Virus & Lockdown.
Our Prime Minister, Boris Johnson announced a national lockdown and instructed people to stay at home to control the virus, protect the NHS and save lives from Monday 4th January 2021. The decision follows a rapid rise in infections, hospital admissions and case rates across the country, and our hospitals are now under more pressure than they have been at any other point throughout the pandemic. This drastic jump in cases has been attributed to the new variant of COVID-19, which our scientists have now confirmed is between 50 and 70 per cent more transmissible.
On 4 January, there were 26,626 Covid-19 patients in hospital in England, an increase of over 30% in one week, and the April 2020 hospital admissions peak has now been surpassed by 40%.  The case rate in England up to 29 December was 478.5 per 100k, three times higher than on 1 December when the case rate was 151.3.  On 3 Jan, 454 deaths were reported, with 4,228 over the last 7 days – a 24% increase on the previous 7 days.
The four UK Chief Medical Officers have advised that the COVID threat level should move from level four to level five, indicating that if action is not taken NHS capacity may be overwhelmed within 21 days.
The Annual Dinner 2021 -- Cancelled
The new year has usually kicked off with the annual dinner but this year’s event dinner in January or February 21 was not contemplated due to the Covid-19 Pandemic. A lockdown came into force immediately after Christmas 2020 and is to last until the 29th March 21 at the earliest, before any relaxation in Government’s restrictions is contemplated. There is serious concern, as the virus has nutated to a more contagious strain. This is believed that is the first time that the Club’s Annual Dinner has been cancelled since the early period of the Second World War, in 1940 & 41
Two Air Ambulance fly into Rectory Park on to a snow carpeted ground
I believe this is the first occasion that two Air Ambulances have flown into Rectory Park together. Talking to the photographer, Peter Westbrook, Chairman of Sutton Coldfield Hockey Club, who was walking his dog, Bailey, was unable to say if they were delivering patients to Good Hope Hospital, some 100 meters away or collecting patients for transfer into other hospitals on Monday 25th January 2021
Paul Heron leaves the Midlands
Paul Heron, a well-respected member, who has been involved in organizing the Club’s annual dinner for a number of years has left the Midland to set up home in Otley, Yorkshire, so as to be in close proximity of both his son and daughter, who both now reside in that area. Paul also organized the Club’s fixture card and has also run the Club’s 200 Club Monthly Draw for many years still managed to conduct the January 21 draw via Zoom, with our President, James Ross.
Zoom Meetings
Zoom is the new method of communication with visual contact between the Club and its membership, and in fact our last AGM was conducted under this procedure, as public meetings are banned, because of the virus.
Pre-Season Working Parties
Ever hopeful that we will be playing cricket this summer working parties were well supported on Saturdays the 21st, 28th March, 3rd & 10th April 21, with some 40 plus members answering the Chairman, Nick Baker’ call for working parties.

The Ground Secretary, Mike Hepburn had highlighted particular tasks that needed attention from the membership, including the following tasks: -
Pressure washing, to remove material from the pathway in front of the pavilion;
Removing the dead grass in the bowlers run up to the nets, filling the hollow and level up the surface;
Cleaning the sight screens;
Paint the outside of the lower ground pavilion;
Cutting the squares;
Removing the leaves from the area in front of the score box;
Plus, a thoughtful members took the opportunity to plant some late summer flowering bulbs and sow some wild flower seeds.

Friday Evening - Junior Cricket - 7th May 2021
Great evening at SCCC last night. Forty-nine 5-year-olds having their first taste of cricket. Also, 9s and 11s on their normal Friday night training - coaching.
All the parents in attendance tested for Covid-19 by House Manager, John Howard with his usually cordial hospitality skills. The ground looked superb, after considerable work on the ground during most Fridays by the Golden Oldies, with hoes and mowers of the enclosure area and the sowing of cornflower and poppy seeds to add to the Lilly of the Valley seeds planted earlier in the spring.
Parents in a warm evening, enjoying a social drink watching their offspring first entry into cricket.
No Fixture Card in 2020
Due to Covid-19 no fixture card was ever produced for the 2020 season as there was no advice on when the season would have started and then in June 20 a limited Saturday fixture was organized by the Warwickshire Cricket League. Similar, this season 2021, with lockdown preventing the selling of advertising space and the uncertainty of what cricket would be played a simple, fit in your pocket folding A4 card was produced for Saturday fixture, as shown above.
20 All Out
This was one of the lowest scores, if not the lowest, by any visiting side to Rectory Park, on Saturday 29th May 21 when the Old Edwardians 2nd Xl were bowled out by the Club’s 2nd team in 15 overs and 1 ball. This was due to a fine performance by Martin Ball taking 7 wickets in 8 overs for 11 runs. Sutton scored the winning run off 15 balls without loss in a match that lasted less than 2 hours.
The 2021 Fixture Card
FIXTURE CARD - 2021 SEASON
On the Sundays in 2021 the Club only had one side playing league cricket in the Arden Sunday Cricket League and was placed in Division 2 with nine other clubs, it would be a percentage league, as there was no certainty that teams would achieve a complete home and away competition.
An interesting selection of clubs, some were well known to us while others, I have no record of the Club ever having a match on a 1st team basis before. The Clubs in the Division 2 are, Attock, Barnt Green, Fillongley, Kenilworth Wardens, Knowle Village, Nether Whitacre, Solihull Municiple, Wellesbourne, Weoley Hill,
Blank Sunday fixtures occurred when there was no ASCL appointed game, so members were invited to play in, was referred to a Sunday Smash game. the first such game was against Handsworth at 2:00pm on Sunday 13th June 2021. If members were interested in having a game, (including anyone who was unable to have one on Saturday 12th June), would you please contact Simon Matthews, son of Terry.
Climate Changes
There has been concern for a number of years about the Climate Change due mainly to the increases in Carbon Dioxide because of mankind, raising a long-term shift in the planet's weather patterns and average temperatures.
This year, 2021, a number of worldwide conferences have started to show the effects we can expect, including the UK with higher summer ground temperatures and more flash floods. There may be no connection between this statement and our July evening sunsets.
However, climate change is the long-term shift in average weather patterns across the world. Since the mid-1800s, humans have contributed to the release of carbon dioxide and other greenhouse gases, such as methane into the air. This causes global temperatures to rise, resulting in long-term changes to the climate.
How are humans changing the climate?
In the 11,000 years before the Industrial Revolution, the average temperature across the world was stable at around 14°C. The Industrial Revolution began in the mid-1800s when humans began to burn fossil fuels such as coal, oil, and gas for fuel.
Burning fossil fuels produces energy, but also releases greenhouse gases such as carbon dioxide, methane, and nitrous monoxide into the air. Over time, large quantities of these gases have built up in the atmosphere. For example, the level of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere rose by 40% during the 20th and 21st century and is now over 400ppm (parts per million). This level of carbon dioxide is higher than at any time in the past 800,000 years.
The global temperature changes -1850 to 2018, compared to 1961-1990 averages
From the 1st July 2021, Birmingham City Council have introduced a charge for old vehicles entering the City Centre, but the commencement date was put back 2 weeks as the computer monitoring program was not ready.
The Clean Air Zone, which is inside the inner ring road, timing and charges will operate 24 hours a day, 365 days a year. £8-a-day charge for cars, taxis, busses, lorries and vans and a £50 a day charge for lorries and buses.
All drivers need to input their car registration number into the system to see if they are exempt from any charges.

The Silver Salver at Lord’s
With Covid-19 now of serious concern and no activity in sport or social life for a considerable time scale of over 12 months, I looked back on early days of the Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club outstanding history. I noted, that I had not received any response concerning my meeting at Lords in 2007 with Glenys Williams, the MCC archivist, regarding the Silver Salver, referred to on page 8 of the Club’s history. I wrote a Neil Robinson at Lords on the 5th November 2020 concerning the Silver Salver’s history held by Lord’s but I did not receive a reply, the reason being that 95% of the staff were on indefinite furlough, due to Covid-19.
In May 2021 I received a telephone call from Robert Curphey, MCC Archivist & Library Manager regarding my letter of November, advising me what inscription was on the cataloguing record, as follows: -
“In grateful memory of long services and tried friendship this token of esteem is presented to the Rev. W.K.R. Bedford Rector of Sutton Coldfield and sometime secretary of the Free Foresters by his most attached friends & affectionate brethren of the club
March 1863”.
Robert stated that he had read a copy of our Booklet “149 Years of Cricket at Rectory Park” and agreed that it did not give a true picture of the Rev. W. K. Riland Bedford and that he would rewrite the caption to the Salver.
Further to our exchange of e-mails, I agreed that Robert’s proposed rewrite the wording of the information to the MCC’s cataloguing record, and when the item goes on display again, the MCC can include this as part of the caption. – please see below:
In grateful memory of long services and tried friendship this token of esteem is presented to the Rev. W.K. Riland Bedford, Rector of Sutton Coldfield and Founder and President (1847-1892) of the Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club in 1847 and Founder in 1856 of the Free Foresters and sometimes Secretary, by his most attached friends & affectionate brethren of the club, March 1863.
Robert was duly thanked by me on behalf of the Club, for his understanding of a need to correct the original wording on the caption held at Lord’s. - Peter
Royal Sutton Coldfield Chronicle
The 3rd addition of the new newspaper covering the towns activities, something, we all have missed since both the Sutton Coldfield News and the Observer papers closed down. We hope the Chronicle will allow sporting activities to be reported in its weekly circulation. As the following article indicates that they will.
Presidents Day
Saturday 3rd July 2021 – Our visitors were from Stratford upon Avon 1st Xl
Our President, James Ross, made speech of welcome to his guests during the change of innings and announced a number of awards and citations. First creating a new Life Membership to Michael B. Mackey for his long and active service of 21 years, as player, cricket coach and an active supporter.
Vice Presidents were awarded to Nicholas A. Baker; Martin M.I. Ball; Simon T.J. Berry; Graham M.D. Clark; Mark D. Guest; James R. Little; Simon J. Matthews; Stephen G. Russell; Warwick N. Tidy.
I can’t let the event go past without formerly saying “Thank You” for such an excellent day, and for the presentation you made to me, Neil and Tiger of the Charles Sales book “The Covers are Off” including a personal note from Charles. It was very kind of you and most considerate as we all enjoying what goes on behind the seen. The lunch was very appetizing and an inspiration on “How do I feed guest keeping within the Covid-19 restrictions - regulations”. You even took control for the sunshine.
In the first half of the 2021 season, we only had 3 wins and we were languishing close to the bottom of the Warwickshire County Cricket League, Premier Division whereas Stratford were top an expecting a healthy win. Winning the toss, they invited Sutton to bat first, scoring a modest total of 215 runs all out in the 46 over of a 50 over game with Lee Thomason scored 84 and Tom Eustace 46. Stratford made a disastrous start, losing both openers for 16 runs but worst was to follow with two quick run outs by majestic and athletic fielding, first from Jack Reidy and then by Jacob Squire-Wood quickly followed by a catch behind the stumps by Lee and the visitors were 57 runs with their top five batsmen back in the pavilion. Our young off spinner, Harry Williams-Lucas was introduced into the attack and in his one ball short of 8 over had removed four batsmen for 33 runs with the aid of two class stumpings by Lee, for Sutton to round off a perfect day by bowling the visitors out for 162 and a win by 53 runs. Sutton acquiring 20 points with Stratford receiving a consolation 5 points.
Every Sunday morning, I tend to go through the WCCL results. This time I went deeper in the statistics. Lee Thomason is top of the Premier batting averages with 495 runs this season. However, he has scored 12,553 runs in the WCCL at an average of 42.27, with 24 hundreds, at an average of 4 per season. I also note that the 3rd Xl are top of their division at the half way stage.
Sutton 1st Team Conceding 310 runs to Coventry & NW
On one of the hottest days this year, Saturday 17th July 2021, with temperatures in the high twenties, Coventry & NW won the toss and elected to bat first at Rectory Park against our 1st team in a Warwickshire County Cricket League Premier Division 50 overs match.
The Coventry side made a good start scoring 100 runs in the 16th over for the loss of 3 wickets, but with only the loss of two further wickets they had reached 200 runs in the 38th over and then losing their 6th wicket with the score at 220. The Sutton team believed they would remove the lower order batsmen quickly, but they had not anticipated Ankit Kaushik furious attack, in particular on Raul Ram, who concede 30 runs in the 50th over, with 4, 6, 4, 6, 6, 4 boundary hits.
Looking back into the Club’s distinguished historical, I can’t find any reference to a first team match when we have conceded over 300 runs to the visitors at Rectory Park. Although the 1st Team did score 411 for 2 wickets in a B&DPCL 50 over cup competition at Harborne on the 8th July 2017 scored 411 runs for 2 wickets against Harborne, which is referred to in 2017 records.
I can’t find any past record, where a Sutton bowler, in any team, has ever concede 30 runs in an over. The only reference is a 3rd team game against Studley at Rectory Park on the Lower Ground on Saturday 5th August 2006 where Mark Bell was the batsman who was involved in 30 runs being scored in an over against the bowling of a Mr. Bray, although it did involve the wicket keeper. The score book recording the over as; 4, 6, 6, 6, dot, 4. With the dot being 4 byes past the wicket keeper.   The score card of Suttons’ batting is shown earlier in the 2006’s history.

A Star for the future
SUTTON COLDFIELD'S
SHAILAN SRIKANTHAN
PLAYS AT LORDS
Shailan played at Lords for MCC Schools vs the England Schools Cricket Association on Tuesday 7th September 21.
Fielding first the MCCC bowled out ESCA for 253, with Shailan bowling 7 overs, 1 maiden 1 wicket for 30 runs. Shailan also took a catch in the field.
Unfortunately, he didn't get to bat in the middle as MCC chased the total in 48 overs.
A fantastic opportunity fully taken by Shailan, seen here, looking out over the Lord’s Ground from the dressing room balcony. Congratulations Shailan.
Highlights of the 2021 Season
This has to be the last match of the season with the 1st team needing a win over the league leader and our local rivals, Walmley. They brought a very large number of their supporters but the Sutton club were far too good for them winning by 42 runs. It also prevented Walmley, who needed to win the WCC Premier League and so qualify for the B&DPCL Four County Round Robin playoff, with the top 2 teams gaining entry next season into Division 2 of the B&DPCL. Also, the win meant, the Club avoided being relegated out of WCC Premier League with Streetly losing on run rate and going out of the Premier League.
The second Xl had a satisfactory season finishing in among a number of clubs in mid-table. They did have an outstanding performance by Martin Ball who took 7 Wickets in conceding 11 Runs in a spell of 8 Overs against Old Edwardians 2nd who were bowled out for a total of 20 runs in 15.1 overs. Sutton in their reply score 21 runs without loss in 2.3 overs.
Congratulation also goes to the 3rd Xl, who won the North West 8th Division league handsomely by 66 points.
The 4th team were unfortunate in missing out on promotion by 1 point, finishing a very creatable third in the North West 9th Division

Andy Neal.

End of Season Awards & Curry Evening
Around 70 members, including friends & relation sat down to an excellent curry, the service was a little slow but it was worth waiting for.
The Chairman, Nick Baker, commenced a short address by thanking our sponsors, in particular Lane, referring to the continual rise in the running cost that the Club has to bear, but it was essential to provide good ground – playing facilities. He went on to congratulate the gardening club for maintain the gardens area around the pavilion, and also, the Bar chairman, John Howard for maintaining bar services throughout the season, despite constantly having to amend the bar service procedures in line with Government’s Covid-19 changes in legislations. Nick went on to say that the Club membership was 453 which included players, parents and supporters, and that during the season we had field 6 sides on a Saturdays and 2 on Sundays; thanking the coaches and captains for their efforts, with particular reference to junior coaches who were handling various age groups between Under-9 to Under-17, with a reference to a number of young players who played for the County.
Awards, were presented by each captain, with a £25 voucher for spending over the bar, starting with the 4th team. It was interesting to hear that, captain Ian Hay, said he was from time to time overloaded with available players, confirming the Chairman’s comments earlier in the proceedings, with the 3rd team captain expressing a similar good mix of players available at team selections.
The 2nd team captain referred to his side being a candidate for relegation at the half way point in the season but gelled as a team, winning 4 out of their last 5 games. He concluded with a comment about George Dyche, as a youngster of great promise. The 1st team captain, referred to the last game of the season against Walmley, with particular reference to the outstanding bowling performances of Jacob and young Felix.
To record a win over Walmley, preventing them going forward to the B&DPL play off and at the same time getting Streetly relegated was considered by many members as a “Double Whammy”

Also, the Sunday Smash and a new 100-ball format cricket next season.
The evening finished with our President, James Ross, presenting Martin Ball a certificate recognizing his new status as a Vice President in the Club.
