Annual General Meeting looking forward to the 2022 Season
The AGM was held on the 21st October 2021 at 7.30pm in the Club’s pavilion which was attended by 19 members and the chairman, Nick Baker Via videoconference with 3 apologies.
Chairman Reported that the 2020/2021 seasons started with the continued uncertainty surrounding cricket due to the ongoing Covid pandemic and with the country sill in lockdown much of our winter activities were cancelled. This had a knock-on effect on the club’s financial position. Using the paid site Pitchero, we were able to update the website and manage membership, facilitate contactless payments, split membership costs over a period of time, collect match fees and contact the membership base through push emails as well as news on the site.  This reduced cash handling with members to pay on a monthly basis. No overseas player was employing in the 2021 season. In addition to Lanes who continue to support the club as headline sponsors, we were also able to offer sponsorship packages to Saracen Fire and Protections, Little Owls Tuition, SheCo, Tamworth Steel, Harts Furniture, Stokes Chrome, SSH Construction, Veerus and Chosen Estate Agents. Life members playing members and former players also gave good financial support.
Mike Hepburn and his team of volunteers got the grounds into working condition – painting of the picket fence, preparing nets for use, mowing and tending to the area outside of the club and the club’s gardeners Jamie Ross, John Robinson, Peter Bell and Neil Houghton, made sure the pavilion surrounding area was an enjoy to see. The Astro footprint of the nets was increased in the length.
Lee Thomason and Phil Oliver to ensure both grounds were in great condition and were able to support the increased volume of cricket played this year. In particular the increased number of age group county matches supported and the high standard of our grounds was a credit to the grounds team.
It was agreed to adjust the membership fees back to 2019 levels but to maintain to a flat household sum. At the season started, we saw a massive increase in both junior and senior membership.
268 Players – both junior and senior
58 Supporters
131 parents.
The club entered teams into all leagues and competitions available – 1st, 2nd,3rd and 4th Saturday teams, with a Saturday 5ths and 6ths playing on 2 weekends, Sunday 1st XI plus Sunday 100 ball team and a Vets team. In addition to junior teams from U10-U17 and Friday night All Stars…. players representing Sutton Coldfield from 9 through to 58 years old.
Thank to Zoe Mathews as Welfare Officer. Thankfully no incidents required Zoe’s involvement.
Thanks to Andy Neal who put in a tremendous amount of work to ensure the junior teams ran smoothly.
Thanks to the junior coaches and team captains who supported their players.
A special thanks to Jacob Squire-Wood, who secured his coaching award and using the Andy Biddle Foundation grant to secure a job at BVGS. Jacob also coached and help support Mark Turnam and England Blind Cricketer who has been using our nets to get back into form after the enforced Covid break. We will continue to support Mark in 2022 with access to facilities and coaches in his preparation for international cricket.
Due to Covid Restrictions in 2020 would continue in 2021– no teas, no use of changing rooms and stoppages in game were undertaken and managed well by captains, coaches and players. Many thanks to all involved in managing these protocols.
1ST Xl Team Captain’s Reports from Jack Smith.
On the last day of the season, we beat top of the league Walmley preventing them winning the league and more importantly maintaining our Premier League status by the finest of margins.
Lee Thomason amassed 761 runs, an average of 64 (6 X 50, 1 X 100) finishing 17 runs shy of the league’s leading scorer despite several fewer innings & not batting in either game vs bottom of the table Barby.
Tom Eustace had a great season, scoring 513, an average of 43 including 112 from 113 balls vs Walmley.
Raul Ram (in what may turn out to be his last season for the club due to relocating) also had a really strong season in the middle order scoring 351 runs at an average of 39, scoring 3 x 50s.
Jacob Squire-Wood led our attack superbly with 25 wickets at an average of 20, bowling fantastically all season with a best return of 5-56 vs Streetly. Jacob also spearheaded the final day win vs Walmley with a remarkable opening spell of 12 overs for 13 runs with 1 wicket.
Amaan Farooq was excellent at first change with 20 wickets at 22 with a best return of 6-55 against Attock.
Special mentions must also go to Felix Haines who opened the bowling for the majority of the season and has performed excellently culminating in a magnificent opening spell against Walmley, bowling 5 overs for 11 runs and removing Walmley’s 2 key top order batsman (Batters).
Shailan Srikanthan was a real find for the club this season, joining us from Walmley and has settled in brilliantly, bowling with great control. Shailan even managed to represent MCC Schools at Lords in September – a remarkable achievement.
Both Felix & Shailan have been selected for the Warwickshire EPP during this winter. We really hope that these two can go on and be mainstays for the side for many years to come.
Awards    Batting:  Tom Eustace 513 runs with an average of 43.0
Bowling: Jacob Squire Wood 25 wickets with an average of 20.0.
Second XI Captain Report from Nas Ali
Played 20, Won 9, Drawn 1, Lost 10.
After a poor start to the season the team in the second half of the season won 8 out of the last 10 matches.
Batters struggled at times, but Ollie Grey Nimo amassed 410 runs at 34.17. Anil Kumar scored 327 at 46.71. Elliot Needle was not out on 96 against Handsworth was worthy of note.
Martin Ball returned the best figures of 7 for 11, skipper Nas Ali took 26 wickets at an average of 20.81.
The senior bowlers of Finn and Ball were well supported by the youth of George Dyche.
Third XI Captain Report from Dave Hands in Division 8 NW.
Won 11, Lost 3, Draw 3
The 3rd Xl won their league by 66 points above the second placed side to be League Champions of 8 NW
The batting remained steady, if not a little erratic at times with 1 or 2 collapses
Isaac Mackey was the highest run scorer & award winner for 2021 with 412 runs & high score 129 not out.
Regan Beamish finishing in a very close 2nd position on 410 runs
The bowling was often led by the massively experienced Stuart Russell, regularly supported by Dom Lee, Ghulam Rana, Luke Bishop, Mo Khalil & James Dalton – James & Stuart making a few appearances in the 2nd team this season.  Sam Hay, Jack HW, Tom Hurley, Will and Spike all made good bowling contributions when selected. Dom Lee receive the 3rd Xl bowling award.
Like all Club 3rd Teams, players vary from week to week, as the demands on replacements by the 1st & 2nd Xls, take their toll; in particular during the summer holiday period. However, a number of other players including a new member to the club Ghulam Rana – from Catherine-de Barnes & Dom Lee from the 4th Team
Other 4th team players making appearances in the 3rd Xl were Rajveer Chauhan, Harry Hands, Sam Hay, Kevin Cuthbert, Rohit Chopra, and our star youngsters, Will Burford & Spike Whitehouse, all of whom gave great accounts of themselves and contributed well to the team effort.
In all there were 39 different players appearing for the 3rd team during the course of the season
Fourth Xl Captain Report from Ian Hay in Division 9 North West
Won 8, Winning Draw 1, Losing Draw 1, Lost 4, Abandoned Games 4
Position at the end of the Season 3rd with 240 points.
A relatively successful season finishing 3rd just missing out on promotion, with 2nd in the league Coleshill just a point above us on 241.
A total of 40 players playing for the 4th team, with 16 juniors representing the 4th XI during the season.
There were more players potentially to select from taking the potential playing pool to around 45 players.
The team then was then made up of a consistent squad of roughly 15-18 players that contributed to the results that almost got us promoted, with the teams being a mix of roughly 50/50 juniors and senior players and the mix generally worked well.  In particular the junior section did really well and I’m excited about the future talent coming through.
We had nearly 50 players that could have been selected to play for the 4th XI. A large number of these players that we took membership from (in my opinion) were not good enough to gain a regular place in the 4th XI. If we have a similar membership uptake of this nature again next year we need (in my opinion) to offer them some competitive cricket. Walmley for example, run 6 teams with the Walmley 5a and 5b sides playing in Div. 9 NW and Div. Central respectively.
This is something I think we need to seriously consider, that a few people that joined us subsequently left to join other clubs or are considering joining other clubs next season due to lack of playing opportunities.
On the whole though, apart from the above politics and the weekly selection dramas, it was a pleasure to play and skipper a really good bunch this season.
Further Comments by the Chairman
Social
The end of season curry was reasonably well attended. There will be more focus on social events in the coming year.
The Government Covid rules and regulations were constantly being changed but on the whole John Howard, Tom Owens and the bar managed to ensure service went smoothly on the whole – a difficult task to ensure safe service whilst encouraging a social environment.
Planning for 2022
I and Steve Russell, are review the financial plans for 2022.
The Annual Dinner booked for 4th Feb at Moor Hall. This will commence the 175th Anniversary celebrations.
With increased playing numbers we have agreed in principle additional ground use at BVGS to run 5 team fixtures – another captain will be required to take on this responsibility, we have had a couple put their hand up already.
All Star junior cricket will continue on Friday nights and develop on what we achieved this year, we will look for new junior committee members to help support our activities.
A former players lunch plus celebratory fixtures and kit to look forward to….and even a 175th Anniversary Beer has been agreed to be brewed by our local Brewhouse Pub
We are on the look for help – someone to manage the website on a weekly basis and own communications, help with event organisation and new ideas people!! Please come forward pass onto anyone who may be interested.
Treasurer’s Report by Steve Russell
Income
We have had a solid year financially. As you can see from the report, we budgeted for a small surplus of £1,300 and achieved a surplus of £3,355.
Subscriptions – In senior cricket we have with a lot of effort on the part of captains, John Howard and Stuart Russell ensured that all memberships were paid and match subs collected. It is still a real effort to collect annual subscriptions despite giving players various payment options and this is an approach we need to keep under review.
Senior Match Fees are slightly down but there were at least 2 4th XI games where we had to forfeit and the same on Sundays.
Junior – whilst down on an ambitious budget the figure is up £1400 from last year.
Donations and Sponsorship are both up though we are slightly down on the fixture card. Sponsorship is a key issue for us moving forward. Lanes have been an excellent supporter but we cannot rely only on them moving forward. I know Matt will pick this up later.
Gift Aid – this is less than expected but we believe this may have been due to a mistake in our claim.
Miscellaneous Income: The main items are reported in the report. Most notable were a £1000 rebate from the GPC and 2 ECB Grants obtained by Mike Hepburn. These are offset on the Expenses side (Ground Equipment & Cooker/Barbecue)
Expenditure
A number of items are below budget inc. Teas where we had budgeted for an external provider who pulled out. We were able to replace her and manage costs in this area. Thanks to John Howard.
Coaching costs – paid out less than we anticipated. This is however likely to be an area of significant increase next year given our determination to improve senior coaching provision.
Player Payment – often a contentious area but largely off-set by Sponsorships. Slightly above budget as we made an additional payment to Dinushka to allow him to change his flights as we did not need him for the play-offs.
Ground Equipment – this includes payment to Mike Hepburn off-set by £1,000 from ECB.
Ground Labour – 3 areas – Lee Thomason (£3,410); Phil Oliver (£3,498); Glendale (£1,100). Will need to look closely at this next season as Lee is stepping down from Groundsman and we may need to employ someone at an increased cost.
Miscellaneous Expenditure – This includes nearly £2,000 as cost for the dinner. The Barbecue & Cooker cost were offset by an ECB Grant & GPC Donation.
Looking ahead, we have a number of potential cost items next year that require us to focus on sources of income beyond Subscriptions and Donations. A focus is required on attracting new sponsors and where we can access Grants. We will incur probably higher Ground Labour costs and Coaching costs. It is important that those Social Members who joined this year, re-join and that the cricket committee and captains ensure all memberships and subs are collected.
Juniors Chairman’s Report by Andy Neal
Taken from notes and comments made on evening of 21/10/21, this report includes the names of all those involved with supporting the junior section who were thanked at the end of junior season event on Friday 20th August.
An enjoyable successful and nearly full junior season.
As per Nick Baker’s (Chair) report there has been a brilliant growth in youth members and begun to develop a great avenue for adult cricket. We have provided cricket from All Stars age group though under 10s all the way to under 19.
For the junior section household membership also proved a real success with many juniors joining the club often bringing along an adult player who can get involved with the club too.
The section has been extremely successful in organising and completing so many matches this season – a challenge other clubs have struggle to rise to given COVID challenges. We are lucky to have such a committed group of volunteers and supporting youth cricket at the club.
Some of the successes to call out from the juniors.
Under13 Championship winners.
Under13 League and Under12 Area League winners.
Under11 Grizzlies – Area league winners and due to play their finals day at the start of 2022
season
Our All-Stars program was the biggest ever for the club with close to 50, 5–8-year-olds getting involved on Friday nights. Particular thanks to Roy Hopwood, Sue Hurrell, Jacob Squire-Wood, Luke Bishop, Liz Smart, George Dyche, Jack Hurrell-Woodward and Will Burford along with John Howard and the bar/BBQ team for helping to make it a success.
I would like to thank all our junior players parents, coaches (John Griffiths, Jacob Squire Wood, Luke Bishop, Liz Smart), scorers, ground support teams (particularly Mike Hepburn, ground volunteers, special thanks to Lee Thomason for flexibility in getting pitches and access to top pitch for games) and especially our volunteer team managers – Mark Stirch, Simon Matthews, Jim Little, Jon Crawshaw, Ian Hay, Paul Wright, Dave Hands, Nick Baker and Rob Haines. Also thank you to Nick Baker and the Senior Management Committee for their support of junior section.
The club has put forward 20 trialists for Warwickshire trials and sign of our players high level of skill.
Looking forward, the section in my opinion needs to focus on -
Ensuring 2022 is all electronically scored to help with player statistics
Continue to focus on the growth of the section particularly focusing on the under 10 age groups and below where COVID has impacted skill levels.
Consider how the youth section will be involved in 2022 175th celebrations.
Consider our succession plans for 2024 season and beyond – the current makeup of the Junior Committee means that potentially as our kids get older, we will need to ensure the next group of section volunteers come through.
We are lucky and fortunate at this club to have such a committed and enthusiastic group of people keen to see our junior cricket being enjoyable and successful.

Proposal by Peter Bell for a change of name/title
REH - A short while ago PB advised myself and Nick Baker (the Chairman) of changes to the title of various Sutton Coldfield based clubs in that they were now to be adding ‘Royal’ to their title and felt that the Club should do likewise. A proposal for a change of title was put to me by PB and he also wrote to the GPC Chairman in this regard.  For the Club to do this it would firstly have to be referred to the GPC, who are the controlling body, and secondly it should be discussed at the Club’s AGM.
By way of explanation if the Club were to do this, then it can only be done by (a). A name/title change under the Constitution and (b). A change of name/title for the limited company.
As the Limited Company is controlled by the General Purposes Committee (GPC) any proposed alterations to the Club Constitution must be submitted to the GPC for ratification.
I wrote to Tom Owens (TO) (GPC Chairman) explaining PB’s proposal so it could be discussed at their meeting on 15th September, which I was unable to attend. As the originator of the proposal Peter Bell raised the matter at that meeting.
PB suggested that there would be “kudos” to sponsors supporting a ‘Royal’ club and JR asked whether the GPC committee had any objections to this happening. There were no objections, but TO stated it would require a change to the Constitution and Articles of Association at Companies House. It would have to be further discussed at their next AGM.
I have now brought PB’s proposal for discussion to this AGM for the meeting to discuss this proposal.
Discussion outcome.
A robust discussion took place with ‘pros and cons’ for this proposal being mooted.
PB maintained sponsors would be more attracted to a Club that had ‘Royal’ in its title and it would also add kudos to the Club.
However certain concerns were raised, chiefly:
(a). GC/DH What is the value of a change of name to the Club?  JR – This is a valid point.
(b).  JS If this were to take place would the logo etc on player’s kit etc have to be replaced and at what cost?
(c). There could be kudos involved where sponsorship was concerned, but are sponsors really bothered about the ‘Royal’ title?
(d). RH - Could this cause exclusivity rather than inclusivity? It was felt this could appear to be somewhat ‘elitist’ and may turn people away.
(e). GC - The Club has always been known as Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club since its inception so why change it now?
(f). NB - It may add some kudos as far as the junior section is concerned.
After much discussion there was no definite consensus with the matter so was left in abeyance. It was felt by the Chairman that this should be taken up at the next Senior Management Committee Meeting.
Nomination for ’10 Not Out’ Award.
Whilst this is not an item for an AGM, PB Peter Bell raised this matter as he feels the award is not receiving the attention it deserves and this was the best opportunity to bring it to the membership’s attention.

Chapter XX – 2022
175 Anniversary of the Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club 1847 to 2022.
Happy New Year and Congratulations to SCCC on the 175th Anniversary of its foundation from the Chairman Nick Baker to all the Club’s members. A fantastic milestone has been reached in 2022, and this year sees us celebrate 175 years of Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club playing on the same ground and the same pitches since in Rectory Park since 1847 and a few years earlier. There will be several events to mark this achievement throughout the year, a celebratory dinner, former players lunch, golf day, junior events and a Ball to close the season. We will post dates for the diary soon!
Please keep an eye on the website and WhatsApp groups for latest news and if you would like to help with any of these events, please reach out to myself or any of the committee.

Annual Dinner
The Club is celebrating its 175th birthday with our President James Ross and Chairman Nick Baker well advanced in January 22 in the organization of the Club’s annual dinner and President’s Day.  No annual dinner was possible in 2021 due to Covid, which also prevented the original date of this years of the first Friday in February 2022 being cancelled. A new date of Friday 8th April 2022 at Moor Hall Golf Club has been secured. The other good news is that the Government restriction surrounded Covid has lifted on Tuesday 22 February 2022.  Also announced Stratford upon Avon CC are our guests for Presidents Day- the 28th May 22
The Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club require Umpires
There are several Umpiring Courses being held by the WCL.
The Chairman has agreed that the Club will cover the cost of the course so please register.
Please contact register via the article and contact Nick Baker. The course will run over two full day sessions at Solihull Blossomfield CC. The sessions will be held on Saturday 26th March (9.30 am to 3.30 pm) and Sunday 10th April (10.00 am to 4.30 pm at the latest).
There is also a Tutored Online course, which is a mixture of tutored sessions and self-study online. A Stage Two course takes around 9 to 10 hours to complete so for the online version we break it down into 5 sessions which will run for a maximum of two hours each time.
The tutored online course will run over five successive Saturday mornings from Saturday 19th February to Saturday 19th March inclusive. Start times are 10.00 am and we should finish no later than 12 noon.
You do need a decent level of broadband connectivity and access via a PC or a laptop is strongly advised. I Pads can work but trying to access by a mobile phone has proved difficult in the past.
If you are interested in this online course then please book a place for either course.
Both versions of the course cost £30 (if you have a Stage One discount code then the cost can be reduced to £10)
I do need to add that you must take the Stage One course prior to embarking on Stage Two. If you haven’t undertaken a Stage One in the past then please take the free online Stage One course using the link below. It is a self-study course that can be undertaken at your own pace.
Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club 175th Anniversary Update
Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club is one of the oldest clubs in the West Midlands. This year they celebrate their 175th Anniversary. They have been playing at the Rectory Park in the Town since its inception.
It has been evident in the last couple of years how important sport and the social interaction is to us all. The club has a diverse community with teams and activities ranging from U6’s through the junior age groups into senior cricket, with 4 senior Saturday teams, Sunday 1st XI and a Veterans XI.
To recognise the Anniversary milestone, the club will be hosting a range of celebratory events including a charity fixture against the Help for Hero’s veterans’ charity on the 7th August, an over 35’s tournament on the 28th August to support the Andy Biddle Foundation, and an end of season 175th Anniversary Ball on October 8th.
The Cricket Club is also running a season long collaboration with the Brewhouse and Kitchen in Sutton Coldfield where members of the Cricket Club such as its captain, Jack Smith and Chairman Nick Baker will be helping the Brewhouse’s Head Brewer, Iain Birchley produce a 175th Anniversary Ale on March 17th. It will be a Golden Cask Ale and the Brewhouse and Kitchen will host a tapping party in the Brewpub on the Evening of the 20th April and the Anniversary Ale will be served in the Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club Clubhouse for the duration of the Anniversary Season.
Invitation from the Brewhouse
We are really proud to be linking up with Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club to help them celebrate their 175th Anniversary and are brewing an Anniversary Ale on 17th March 17th (St Patrick’s day) and members are invited to come and see the event. The best time to attend the 'Brew Day' would be 3pm to 5pm as the best picture opportunities would be taking place between these times. There would also be the opportunity to interview the Cricket Club Chairman and Captain along with myself and the Head Brewer from the Brewhouse and Kitchen. Alternatively, you may wish to cover the 'Tapping Party' on April 20th when we will hold an event to 'Tap' the beer that was brewed on the 17th March. The same group of people should be available for interviews and photos on the Evening of this event also.
From our President. James Ross.
Gents, thank you so much for your attendance today, greatly appreciated and showed generational support for our club which is so important. Not least in our 175th to emphasise our heritage. The Brew House set an object lesson in customer management and a welcome. Could we learn something from that?
I thought it was superb and how good has been Nick Baker in working up the initiative.
Next step the tapping in April (provisionally Thursday 28th) when we taste the fruits of our labours. It looked like today a load of grunge but I’m sure we’ve transformed in due course to elixir.
Annual Dinner
This year’s annual dinner of 2022 was a first for two years due to Covid restrictions requiring our 2021 dinner to be cancelled and even this year’s dinner had to be rearranged from its original date the of 4th February. The committee decided that a dinner would take place on Friday 8th April 2022 to celebrate our 175 years of existence as Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club at Moor Hall Golf Club and was attended by around 100 members. A considerable amount of effort, in particular by our President and Chairman in the production of an attractive menu card and a broacher of 44 pages, detailing the distinguished history of the Club.
The Menu Card
The formal evening commenced with Grace which was given by Michael Dean, 1st team captain 1996 & 97 and League champions in the 1997 season.
The Grace
Lovers of cricket, we're back, have no fears! Reflecting on what was the strangest 2 years In fact, it's 26 months since I last did this grace Lockdowns and testing we've all had to face
The last time we met, England were top of the tree After the World Cup and Ashes, happy as can be Along came Covid and into the bubble, Since then, it's really been nothing but trouble
With the bowlers approaching a ripe middle age, We needed the batsmen to take centre stage. Apart from the skipper who stood pretty firm, Watching the others, it made us all squirm.
The Ashes were gone in double quick time Tactics, selection, no reason or rhyme The coach was a Bonner and poor Giro as welt Now defeat by the Windies, on that we won't dwell
But our speaker tonight, he used to bowl seam First the Bears, then the Foxes were his first-class team These days, in the comm box is where you'll find Daggers, Being usually far more amusing than Aggers.
Now with us at Sutton it's a big birthday this year 175 is a long time to be here Fine players, great teams have all made their mark Long may there be cricket at Rectory Park
So have a good season, we approach it with glee Even if, these days, you don't get any teal
Lord, we thank you, for good food, good wine and lively chat
And keep us playing with a nice straight bat.
Our President James Ross was the MC and gave a quick resume of how the evening was to proceed with Stand-up Bingo at £10 a head, between the 1st and 2nd course of the dinner and would be run by our Chairman, Nick Baker, on Heads & Tails followed by a comfort break after the dinner had been consumed with a number of speeches to follow. The winner of the Stand-up Bingo was Raul Ram
The president announced two awards, -
First to Mark Guest, who was awarded a VP for years of service – back to his early teenage playing cricket.
The second award was the “10 Not Out Trophy” which went to John Howard for his excellent service is maintaining the Pavilion open throughout the Covid-19 restriction and regulations imposed by the Government, resulting in the Bar’s contribution to the Club’s income.
The Chairman on the Warwickshire County Cricket Club, Mark McCafferty, talked about The Game and how clubs were the source of developing the talent of young boys and girls in the junior cricket so that there was a readable source of young cricketers into the professional game.
In response to Mark’s address our Chairman, Nick replied by talking about The Club and its passage through 175 years.
The highlight of the evening was our Principal Guest Speaker, Charles Dagnall, Cumberland CCC, Warwickshire CCC, Leicestershire CCC, BT Sport, Sky Sports and Test Match Special. Charles commenced his speech with a tour round the room with humorous chats with several members before homing in on the table of our young players. This approach certainly got everyone attention, as no one knew where he might strike again. However, he told us how he got into first class cricket from his Club team and knew now as a radio commentator and pundit, he referred to many humorous and interesting tale along the way through his career.
For the Statisticians of the Future Wikipedia states: - Charles bowelled 1,240 overs and took 164 first class wickets at an average of 28.24 runs per wicket taking 5 wickets on 2 occasions and his best figures were 6 for 50 runs. In his batting he scored 422 first class runs with his top scores were 28 and a 23 not out.
A Cricket shirt donated by a former Club Junior Player, Tom Banton, now an International, Test and County Cricketer playing with Somerset CCC was auctioned by Charles and was won by Ollie Grey-Nimmo who out bid all-comers.
Charles rounded up the evening with a walk about, chatting will many members. He was certainly a cricketer who understands Club Cricket and the importance in putting as much support as possible into the host club’s efforts.
A Great Dinner and considered by the Club’s Historian: - To be from the “Top Draw”.

Aston Unity Cricket Club
During the Dinner, I met up with John Reves, Life Member of AUCC who informed that this season they will be playing at Coppice Lane for the last time as they have a new ground in Erdington. The location is adjacent to where Erdington Rugby Club play in Kingsbury Road. I understood from John that the entrance would be off Spring Lane and that the move from Coppice Lane was necessary as they could not attract members as the local area does not have any inhabitance, whereas, Erdington has an abundance of local schools for both boys and girls to take up the game of cricket.

An article below that was published in the Royal Sutton Coldfield addition of the Chronical for the week of Thursday 28th April 22, regarding Sutton’s home game against Walmley CC of the 23rd April 22. Sutton Coldfield 1st Team in their 2022 White Ball Kit
L to R Back Row: - A. Farooq, Felix Haines, Jacob Squire-Wood, Aaron Thomason, Harry Williams-Lucas. Seated: - Graham Clark, A. Shabir, Lee Thomason, Jack Smith, Raul Ram, N. Lawrence.
It’s worth a comment on the fact that the scoring of 994 runs for the loss of 26 wickets in the two games is a great complement to the Groundsman – Lee Thomason and his assistances in being able to produce a quality wicket in April the 23rd and 30th. The figures give an average of just over 38 runs per wicket against two quality oppositions of Walmley & Attock CC. respectively.
1ST XI SMASH CLUB RECORDS VS ATTOCK
BY the Chairman NICK BAKER
994 runs scored and 26 wickets at Rectory Park over 2 weekends.
2 great results for SCCC 1st XI.
Having lost the toss, Sutton were somewhat surprisingly put into bat on a used surface. After losing a couple of early wickets Nathan Lawrence and Lee Thomason built a solid platform which allowed Raul Ram (95) and Aaron Thomason (114) to come together and bat magnificently for a partnership of 203! Both players timed their innings incredibly well, with Sutton adding 114 runs in the last 9 overs.
In response Sutton’s bowling was too strong for Attock who limped to 103 all out thanks to some excellent bowling from all of the attack. Special mention to Jacob Squire-Wood who returned great figures of 4-44.
Overall, a fairly faultless performance and great to win 2 games on the bounce. We travel to Olton on Saturday and hope to continue this good form.
994 runs scored on the same wicket over 2 weekends at Rectory Park is testament to the hard work Lee Thomason has put into cultivating the wicket.
In addition to the match report - please see the following from Peter Bell, we believe this result to have broken a few club records –
Is 314-6 the highest score ever recorded by SCCC?
Answer - Yes at Rectory Park.
Is winning margin of 211 our highest ever?
Answer - Yes
Is the 5th wicket partnership between Raul Ram and Aaron Thomason of 203 a record for that wicket?
Answer - yes
However, on the factual side of the records I hold, batting at Rectory Park has never produced very high scores. In the pre-WW2 the highest score was a partnership of 200 between N F Prosser 100 not out and E H Walters 87 not out but that was away at Stratford in 1938 by the Club's 1st Xl.
The highest home partnership was against Stratford for a 2nd wicket in 1978 of 217 between M Dixon 101 not out and B K Gardom 105 not out. The highest away game partnership was for a 3rd wicket against Harborne in 2017 of 280 between Lee Thomason 202 not out and Tom Eustas 127 not out.
The highest score by a Sutton team with 411 in a 50 over game.
The previous highest 5th Wicket partnership was in 1938 of 200 runs away at Stratford between N F Prosser100 not out and E H Walters with 87 not out.
To the best of my knowledge 211 is the highest win by the 1st team. The previous best was against Harborne by 192 runs in the above referred to game.
Footnote
It’s worth a comment on the fact that the scoring of 994 runs for the loss of 26 wickets in the two games is a great complement to the Groundsman – Lee Thomason and his assistances in being able to produce a quality wicket in April the 23rd and 30th. The figures give an average of just over 38 runs per wicket against two quality oppositions of Walmley and Attock CCs respectively.
The above response is by the referred Club’s Historian & Live Member Peter Bell.

Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club.
Obituary to Kenneth David Walter Lancaster
Ken, as he was affectionally known, the only child of Vic & Kate Lancaster, born on the 10th April 1930 and lived his childhood in Defford Road, Lozells, Birmingham and attended the local, Anglesey Primary School. That was until Birmingham City Council redeveloped the Lozells area of the City in the late 1950’s and early 1960’s, which resulted in the family being rehoused in a flat on the corner of Kingsbury Road and Bromford Lane. Ken passed the 11-plus exam into the King Edward Vl Handsworth Grammar School before being called up under the Government’s National Service legislation in 1948.
He served his 2-years in the Royal Air Force as a corporal in their Military Police, affectionally know as Snowdrops, as they wore a white hat and belt on top of their tunic. During his service activities Ken represented the RAF in their Table Tennis activities as well as finding time to play cricket for his RAF station. On being demobilised in 1950 he joined Joseph Lucas in Kings Street as a draftsman for a period of about 10 years.
Following the Government’s awareness of a serious shortage of science teachers within the school’s education system; suitable candidates within industry were invited under TVEI (Technical Vocational Educational Initiative) to apply for posts
By kind permission of ASTRA
without the need for a teaching qualification. Ken did just that, and went into the teaching profession.
It is believed that Ken taught at three schools, Selly Oak Boys School, Kings Rise School but mainly at Handsworth Wood Boys School, on Church Lane. It was in September 1967 that Ken, along with eight other new members of staff arrived at Handsworth Wood School; A school that had in previous years gained a low reputation both locally and academically in the Aston area, and was a difficult school to teach in. However, in the following years Ken, along with the other personal were able to effect a considerable change and to turn the school into a very successful place, both academically and particularly in sporting achievements and reggae music. Ken’s initial appointment was as a Math’s and Technical Drawing teacher. In 1970 he became Head of the School’s Soho House and in 1972 Head of Mathematics. He also introduced Table Tennis to the school’s curriculum and ran coaching sessions at two schools, King’s Rise School for Girl as well at Handsworth Wood School for Boys, He also ran ten soccer sides in the boy’s school, and in 1968 he established the Aston Football League, which he organised until he left teaching. However, while still at the Handsworth Wood School Ken taught a number of boys, who had a musical talent and went on to form a very successful reggae band, Steel Pulse, 1975 to the present day. According to Wikipedia their composer was David Hinds (lead vocals, guitar), Basil Gabbidon (lead guitar, vocals), Ronald McQueen (bass) and Colin Gabbidon on drums. Steel Pulse were the first non-Jamaican act to win the Grammy Award for Best Reggae. Their debut album, Handsworth Revolution launched them in an international known band, in particular in the States.
It is believed Ken moved to Kings Rise School, now an Academy to teach Math’s and subsequently became Head of their Technology Department, a position he held until his retirement from fulltime teaching in the late 1990’s.
Ken married a Vera Florence Mullaley, (born 4th July 1934), on the 10 September 1960 and set up home initially in Wheaton Vale, Handsworth, before going to live in Orphanage Road Erdington. However, Vera developed a medical problem that required the Lancaster’s to move in 1993 to a bungalow in Gibbons Road, Mere Green. Even when Vera was wheelchair bound Ken was still finding time to attend to his many cricket commitments accompanied by Vera until her death on 28th August 2004.
During Ken’s school days he enjoyed a number of sporting activities in Burbury Park including football and in particular cricket. He joined Aston Unity Cricket Club as an opening batsman and an off-spin bowler, playing in both their 1st and 2nd Xl in the Birmingham & District Premier Crick League at the Club’s Court Lane ground and continued when the club moved to Coppice Lane, Middleton. However, as the seasons rolled by, Ken was unable to get a regular place in Unity’s 2nd team on Saturdays so in 2000 he moved his allegiance to play his Saturday cricket with the Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club but continued to turn out for Unity on Sundays. This situation lasted for 2 seasons until 2002 when he finally severed his connections with Aston Unity CC after 53 years.
Now at the Sutton club, Ken played mainly in the 3rd team although he did captain their 2nd Xl in the 2001 season and played a number of games in the following seasons when they were short of a spin bowler. Ken continuing enjoying his cricket well into his late 60’s with the Sutton Coldfield Club’s 3rd team and produced many outstanding performances with his bowling taking 6 Solihull wickets for 23 runs in the 1990 season at the age of 60. Ken in his early cricketing career with Aston Unity, also played Sunday cricket and with likeminded friend in the Lozells area and his enthusiasm instigator the formation of Burbury Cricket Club in the early 1950’s. Initially playing in Perry Hall Park in the Birmingham Parks leagues, before the Club decided in the 1960’s to become a nomadic club, with Ken as secretary, managed a full fixture programme for two teams weekly. However, a parcel of land was found in Blackgreaves Farm and a cricket ground was developed with the Club’s being renamed Lea Marston Cricket Club, but disbanded after 15 seasons when the drink & drive laws came into force. This was due to the early members of the club having been rehoused across the breadth of Birmingham. Ken was also a regular tourist with the Mayflies CC in the Abergavenny, Builth Wells & Brecon area.
1993, 3rd Xl League Champions
SCCC 3rd Xl team member in their Willis Corroon Championship side
Ken was also a keen and capable footballer, and organised sides from the Burbury Cricket Club players to play Sunday football at many pitches around the Birmingham area, so keeping fit during the winter months. He was also an extremely good and talented table tennis player and played at the highest level, including such British Internationals, as Ann Haydon, Pam Mortimer and Johnny Leash. He started playing as a school boy before he played league table tennis in the Birmingham & Solihull District Table Tennis Association after completing his Military Service. During his career he played for a number of clubs, including Friends Hall, Joseph Lucas, Uffculme, Old Veseyans, and Springfield and joining the Royal Sutton Coldfield Table Tennis Club in 1980 and took on both the Fixture Secretaries post and later became the Club’ Secretary for more than 10 years - until 2018.
Ken was described as a defensive player, frustrating his opponents in being able to constantly return the ball back to his adversary’s side of the table. He was a regular player in Division 1, for a considerable number of years and played at a very good competitive standard well into his 70’s mainly with the Royal Sutton Coldfield Table Tennis Club to became the oldest player playing competitive league table tennis. During his time with the RSCTTC he was awarded an Honorary Member of the Club in July 2017 and attended his last Committee Meeting in August 2019.
Ken was an exceptional good administrator, as secretary of Burbury CC, and Lea Marston CC for 25 years as well as being secretary of the Aston Unity club for 12 seasons 1987-1998 and when joining the Sutton club, he took on the role of their fixture secretary. He held this post for 9 seasons 1999 – 2007, when he added the Club’s secretarial position to his administration portfolio for the next 6 seasons in 2005 to 2010.
Throughout the life of the Warwickshire Sunday League 1984 to 2016 seasons, Ken was a member of their executive taking on the role as secretary in 1985 until the end of the 2014 season; a 30-year period.
Kenneth D.W. Lancaster
1930 to 2022
He was also an active member on both the Birmingham & District Premier Cricket League and also the Midland Club Cricket Conference committees’ and was elected President of the B&DPCL in 2011 and visited all clubs during his year in office, followed by the Presidency of the MCCC in 2016, having been awarded earlier in 1999 “The Leslie Jones Secretarial Trophy” – acknowledging his administration abilities in midland cricket over some 62 years.
Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club elected Ken as a Life Member in 2017, to become the 22nd recipients to be bestowed with this honour since the club was formed in 1847.
Ken also found time to be a regular member and supporter of the Alpha One Swing Band and the Steve King Big Band and was also a member of the Highbury Theatre since 2000, rarely missed any of their performances or productions.
Unfortunately, Ken found hearing very difficult and it resulted in the loss of Ken’s ability to communicate with people. Although looking through his diary he clearly recorded in his draftsman’s trained writing both past and future events that he was involved with, and it clearly confirmed that he had no problems with his memory.
He had been a keen supporter of The National Service (Royal Air Force) Association; joining the Sutton Coldfield Branch when they held its monthly meetings at the Fox and Dogs; probably around 2013. He persuaded many ex-RAF personal into joining the Sutton Coldfield branch.
Ken passed away on the 18th May 2022 at the age of 92, during our branch’s visit to the RAF Cosford Museum. To see once again, if a Lancaster bomber was housed there, as he was proud to carry the surname “Lancaster”. His passing was a sad moment for his old Royal Air Force colleagues; but for Ken, he could not have chosen a more fitting location to spend the last few minutes of life. Ken lived on his own and had no known relative except a cousin of Vera, Margaret, who resides in France with her husband Renerey Ray. It true to say that Ken was proud to have served in the Royal Air Force and during his life acquired a mass of memorability of everything relating to “The Lancaster” and of course “Cricket”.
On the 25th May the NS(RAF)A announced that a laying down of the Standard in a Drum Head Ceremony that took place at the RAF Cosford Museum, attended by the Station Commander, Group Captain Gareth Bryant OBE, at the very spot where Ken sat down for his final rest on the 18th May 22. Prayers were said for Ken by a Roman Catholic Priest.
RAF Cosford Museum will now hold an unveiling ceremony on Sunday 11th September 22 for the Standard and Drums to permanently displayed in the area where Ken passed away.
Peter Bell 27th June 2022
Funeral Arrangements

No flowers please, but any donations we would ask that you make it in favour of the Midlands Air Ambulance Charity. Footnote.   Instead of flowers, colleagues and friend were invited, after the service at the crematorium to make a donation to the Air Ambulance. This produced a £590 response, from a congregation of just over a 100, and will be forwarded on to the RAF Cosford establishment with a comment, acknowledging our thanks for the attention, Dr Alex Wollaston gave to Ken in his final hour.
Eulogy
Kenneth David Walter Lancaster
I was first introduced to Ken Lancaster by a mutual friend in 1955 when he was playing cricket for Burbury Cricket Club in Perry Hall Park, some 67 years ago. Ken was an excellent cricketer and had joined Aston Unity Cricket Club around 1950, soon after he had completed his National Service.
During the ensuing years Ken played as an opening batsman and an off-spin bowler, in both Unity’s 1st and 2nd teams in the Birmingham & District Premier Cricket League initially at the Club’s Court Lane ground and also when the Club moved to Coppice Lane at Bassetts Pole.
Aston Unity Cricket Club did not play cricket on Sunday, so Ken set up a club with likeminded friend from the Lozells area with enthusiasm, and taking on the administration tasks in the formation and running of Burbury Cricket Club in the early 1950’s.
Initially, they played in Perry Hall Park in the Birmingham Parks leagues, before the Club decided in the 1960’s to become a nomadic team, with Ken as secretary, managing to organise a full fixture programme for two teams. However, a parcel of land was found in Blackgreaves Farm and a cricket ground was developed with the Club’s being renamed Lea Marston Cricket Club.
However, as the seasons rolled by, Ken was unable to get a regular place in the Aston Unity’s 2nd team on Saturdays, so in 2000 he moved his allegiance to playing his Saturday cricket with the Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club but continued to turn out for the Unity on Sundays. This situation lasted for 2 seasons until 2002 when Ken finally severed his connections with Aston Unity CC after 52 years.
Now at the Sutton Coldfield Club, Ken played mainly in the 3rd team, although he did captain their 2nd Xl in the 2001 season and played a number of games in the following seasons when they were short of a spin bowler. Ken continuing enjoying his cricket well into his 70’s with the Sutton Club’s 3rd team and produced many outstanding performances with his bowling, in particular, taking 6 Solihull wickets for 23 runs in the 1990 season at the age of 60, although it has been recorded that Ken was still playing at the age of 76 and had taken over 800 wickets in a playing career of over 60 years.
The Club was invited to play the MCC at Lord's, under their Club & Ground name, the Cross Arrows, as part of the Club's 150th anniversary celebrations. Ken, at the age of 68, was selected to play as the Club’s spin bowler and took a wicket and was not out when the game ended in a draw, with one run separating the two sides with Sutton 9 wickets down in a game where 545 runs were scored.
Ken was also a regular tourist with the Mayflies Cricket Club in their annual visit to clubs in the Usk Valley.
I have touched a little on Ken’s administration ability, but he was an exceptional good administrator, as secretary of Burbury CC, and Lea Marston CC for 25 years as well as being secretary of the Aston Unity club for 12 seasons in 1987. When joining the Sutton club, he took on the role of their fixture secretary, a post he held for 9 seasons before he added the Club’s general secretarial position to his administration portfolio for 6 seasons. When Ken finally stopped playing cricket, he became the 2nd Xl’s scorer for at least 6 seasons.
Throughout the life of the Warwickshire Sunday League, Ken was a member of their executive committee taking on the role as secretary in 1985, which continued until the end of the 2014 season; a 30-year period.
He was also an active committee member on both the Birmingham & District Premier Cricket League and the Midland Club Cricket Conference’
In 2011 Ken was elected President of the Birmingham & District Premier Cricket League and visited all the clubs in the league during his year in office.
This was followed by Ken being elected President of the Midland Club Cricket Conference in 2016, having been awarded earlier in 1999 “The Leslie Jones Secretarial Trophy” – acknowledging his administration abilities in midland cricket over some 62 years.
Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club elected Ken as a Life Member in 2017, to become the 22nd recipients, to be bestowed with this honour, since the club was formed in 1847.
Ken was an outstanding cricketer in all aspects of the game and would take on rolls when he considered it was the best way forward and he was generally right, but on this occasion;  “Ken”, “you have been run out at 92,Kostenlose Vektorgrafik: Zeigefinger, Hinweis, Geste - Kostenloses Bild ... by Unknown AuthorError! Hyperlink reference not valid.Kostenlose Vektorgrafik: Zeigefinger, Hinweis, Geste - Kostenloses Bild ... by Unknown AuthorError! Hyperlink reference not valid.”so it’s “good by” my old friend Rest in Peace.

Written & delivered by Peter Bell
Monday 27th June 2022
Sutton Coldfield Crematorium
Witterings or Reminiscences of the Golden Oldies,
Sometime known as the Kindergarten Group
Following the visit by Warwick Tidy on Saturday 20th August 22 to HQ. There was a considerable number of e-mail exchanges between James Ross (President), Neil Houghton (Ginge), John Robinson (Tiger), Peter Bell, (Umpire & Club Historian) and Warwick Tidy, which made interesting readings, Well the Collator of the Club’s History thought so.
From: James Ross <james.ross125@icloud.com> Date: 24 August 2022 at 21:16:01 BST
To: Neil Houghton <neil.houghton@blueyonder.co.uk, Peter Bell peter.bell@freeuk.com, John Robinson, jlr212@btinternet.com, Warwick Tidy warwick@icandothatforyou.co.uk>Subject: Sixty years of cricket
﻿
Chaps,
Been reflecting on the fact that it is sixty years since I first played at Prep School and watched live.
My father took me to see my first Test Match in June 1962 v Pakistan. I’ve got Wisden 100th Edition for 1963 which has the full match report that I’ve just been reading.England, Pullar 22, Cowdrey 159, Dexter 72, Graveney 97, Barrington9, Parfitt 101*, Millman, Lock, Trueman, Statham =544-5 dec won by an innings and 24 runs.Hanif and Mushtaq played for Pakistan.
Trueman and Statham took 10 wickets in the match between them. Lock 5.Wisden, “only on the last day did the sun shine with any warmth, but the match was free from rain and altogether 40,000 people were present, the best attendance being on Saturday when over 16,000 attended”. Father took me on the Saturday in the green Ford Zephyr, column gear change, bench front seat, no seat belts. We sat in what is now the Hollies stand (Rea Bank) I was transfixed as a small seven-year-old whilst he drank beer with business contacts all day at the bar.
Best wishes, Jamie

On 25 Aug 2022, at 10:27, Warwick Tidy <warwick@icandothatforyou.co.uk wrote >
To: "James Ross" <james.ross125@icloud.com>
Cc: Neil Houghton <neil.houghton@blueyonder.co.uk, Peter Bell <peter.bell@freeuk.com, John Robinson <jlr212@btinternet.com>,
Jamie dear boy,
My early days were living on Coneybury Farm at the back of Middleton Hall near Tamworth with brother Grahame. We cut a 'strip' in the front field and spent most Summer days playing cricket on it. Ma and Pa were both staunch WCCC supporters back in the day and thereafter.
In 1964 I went to John Wilmott Grammer School and played my first proper game of cricket against Coleshill Grammar School aged 11 and came on as change bowler and took wickets!! Ridiculous that 5 years later I was offered a contract at WCCC when 16 and was the third youngest ever to play for WCCC in the Championship at age 17!!!
Sutton Coldfield will always be dear to my heart and when I made my first team debut at 15, I think, the Captain was Peter Cadman who spoke rather nicely and always wore his wonderful blazer, a bit like the Repton blazer Jamie!! Following Peter there was Martin Bloomer and of course Mike Dixon. I was fortunate to have such wonderful Captains who understood me, as I didn't really undertsnd myself then and still don't even now!!!
Pa would take me and Grahame to WCCC from about 10 onwards to see some First Class cricket matches and I watched in awe of players who were household names, who about 7 seven years later I was in the same side as. How absurd was that. Anyway happy and not to be forgotten days and my truly best moments were to have as first class wickets including some chap called Boycott, Barry Richards, Phil Sharpe, John Hampshire, Bumble and the like. The one however that sadly got away was some bloke called Sobers who was on 10, hit a top spinner from me that really bounced and lobbed to Eddie Hemmings who promptly dropped it!! He went on to score a 100 odd and win the game. How annoying wat that!! Lovely chap though.
Not known for my batting, I did feature in a 50 partnership with Rohan Kanhai at Courtaulds, Rohan scored 49 of them and myself 1, BUT it was a very high class 1 at that!!!
Anyway there you have it, happy days indeed.
Best,
Warwick

Hi James
I don’t remember much about 1962. It was the year 1966 that I remember very well, as it was my 1ST full season with the Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club as an umpire on the lower ground with the Hawkes under the captaincy of Geoff Hatfield, a gentleman and the most pleasant man that you could wish to meet, especially compared to some of those I had meet in the Birmingham League.
On the world cup final day, the 30th July 1966, Sutton 1st team were at home with Chris Barber opened the batting only for Chris to be dismissed for no runs. if my memory is correct Chris was replaced by Neil Houghton, the gentleman I had met on the Bank Holiday in August 1965 during the tea interval who invited me to join the SCCC and I was introduced to Geoff, which resulted in me umpiring the remaining time after tea interval with the Hawkes. Neil always believed it was his biggest mistake he ever made in inviting me to join the Club, but fortunately I never believe him.
However, I have digressed, on the world cup final day at Wembley, Neil had arrived with a television so that players could watch the final, Neil in fact never saw anything of the world cup game live as he batted all afternoon. If I was to look at the fixture card for the 1966 season, I would know who the 1st team were playing, but I have sealed all the Club’s history into 7 boxes, for someone to take over as the Club’s historian and archivist duties at some future date.
It is nice to know that the youngest member of our group is starting to go down memory lane, which is a well-worn path.
Regards & Stay Safe
Peter

Happy memories from the 1960's I remember them well golden years as far as I was concerned In my twenties moving slightly quicker than I can today. In those days we did not realise that in years to come the Liverpool speed merchant would descend upon us to spice up the pace attack!
Peter claims I invited him to join the Club back in 1965 I will now take credit for that little did I realise at the time that Peter would make such a major contribution to well being and success of both the Cricket and Hockey Clubs and still contributing some 57 years later. It is time I forgave him for all the decisions that went against me.

Enjoyed reading your reminisces.
I came to Sutton Coldfield in 1970 to take up a job at Good Hope. The new clubhouse had just been opened; it was not really a good design for a clubhouse, more appropriate for a community centre!
With a pal from the hospital, I sat on a bench, sometime in May, watching a first team game; Sutton were fielding. My pal asked what I thought of the player who was bowling. I said 'well he runs-up as far as a fast bowler but he seems to be bowling leg spin'. Guess who!
When I'd been in the job a few weeks, a doctor from Rhodesia (Tony Robertson) started to come to the lab to talk about a research project he was doing. He was 'batter' in Suttons Ist team and, during our chats, it emerged that I also had some cricketing experience. Because of work commitments, 'on call' etc. I wasn't able to play regularly but he asked me If I would be interested in playing the odd game on the occasions that a Sutton side was short of players. I played in theatre kit under Dick Sperryn a number of times before I played in a mid-week Club v Hospital beer match towards the end of the season. That was when I first met Andy Beach and Roger Coombs!
We got on really well and I stayed much to late drinking with them and was signed up to join Sutton over several pints. Beachy took it upon himself to introduce me to Birmingham and the Midlands. He took me everywhere! I remember him taking me to Edgbaston, the restaurant in Scotts, the Dolche Vita, and Moore Hall on Saturday nights; he also introduced me to his sister Lindsay and got me playing hockey with Kings Heath vets; not a natural game for me but I had a whale of a time. What more could I ask for?
At the start of the next season, I began playing in the 2nd XI under Geoff Pritchett. Beachy invited me to turn up at an Imps game that was being played at Rectory Park. The Imps were batting, I was sat watching on a bench in the club's garden area, when I heard a high-pitched giggling laugh that I would recognise anywhere; who should it be but Curly Caldwell! It was great to meet someone from my old Club who remembered that I could play.
Good to hear that there is a fresh supply of Penguins.
See you Friday morning.
Tiger

Footnote.
Both Andrew Beach (Beachy) and his sister Lindsay were adopted children of Mr. & Mrs. Beach who always had a wish to get Lindsay married, but as far as Andrew was concerned and everyone who met Lindsay, thought they were like chalk and cheese.
Kings Heath were a hockey club, the other side of Birmingham. It was a club that Andrew decided to move his allegiance too when the Sutton Hockey Club’s policy was to split up their 4th team players which was more of a social side with the majority of the players being from the Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club. This resulted with Roger Coombs, James Ross, Warwick Tidy, Tony Malik and I think Chris Barber also moving to the Kings Heath club, but after a couple of seasons and the introduction of the Drink & Drive laws, they move back to the Sutton Club who had decided that the 4th Team would be more of a social side rather than in the club’s players promotion and relegation sequence.

The Passing of old Players
With the 2022 season behind us, it requires the recording of the passing of three players. Ken Lancaster on 18th May, Sid Stonehouse a month later, Martin Bloomer died on the 15th August and Andy Thomas died in late October.
A considerable amount has been written about Ken; However, Sid played his early cricket with Sutton Congs & Meths on their ground on Tamworth Road just under the bridge. In later years he became a regular supporter of the top ground teams and you would find him in the kitchen helping out with the players teas. Martin was an outstanding batsman with the 1st team during the 1960’s and the early 1970’s.
A 3rd team member, Andy Thomas passed away in late October 2022 at the age of 55 to 60. Andy had 2 daughters. He was Sunday captain of 3rd team, the Grasshoppers, in a date my son Mark advised me, was in 2004. He left the Club in 2007 or 08 when their friendly cricket became a Development League along with a number of players who became a Wishaw3rd team including Matt Taylor, Hugo Russell, Tony White and Bob Harper except my son Mark who retired from playing cricket.
Martin Bloomer
Martin Bloomer joined the Cricket Club in 1963, having been a most successful young batsman with Streetly C.C. made his debut with the 2nd team, and his ability to score 281 runs in about 4 games resulting in Martin being promotion to the 1st Xl and very quickly replaced Geoff Pritchett as their opening batsman. At the end of the season Martin had completed his thousand runs for the 1st Xl and with an aggregate of 1,300 for the club.
