Captain
1971
Captain
In late May or early June 1971, although Martin was captain, he walked away from cricket and his employer at Warwickshire Oil Storage and Roger Coombs took on the Captaincy.
I can recall a 1st Xl game that I was umpiring at the bowlers end at Rectory Park in September 1970 against Stafford C.C., Martin opening the batting, facing a, medium pace to quick, bowler who, if I remember correctly, was a Jack Harris. Martin had told me that he considered Jack was a “toffee bowler” and after the 5th over in the match Martin had had faced 3 over from Jack and was very close to a half century. Jack refused Stafford’s captains request to bowl another over as he said “I not bowling at that idiot.” However, a few runs later Martin was out having hit Jack for a series of 4’s and 6’s. Jack immediately advise his captain that he was happy to bowel again, and quickly took 5 Sutton wickets.
It was reported that Martin had died on the 15th August 2022. To me he was one of the most aggressive free scoring right-handed batsman, that I as the umpire had ever watched from behind the stumps. Martin’s aggression was from the 1st Ball of the match.
The Chairman’s Report on the 2022 Season
Thanks to Steve Russell, Treasurer for the detailed report.
As is very evident from the financial report, as a volunteer run club, for non-commercial gain, we have to work hard to stand still. We rely on our members, sponsors and benefactors to support the club to provide playing excellent facilities and coaching for all members, both junior and senior and provide opportunities for all to better themselves or just have good sporting experiences.
The fund-raising initiatives pushed by Rob Haines utilising Sport England Crowdfunding sources, have meant we have been able to buy new site screens, installed new fencing across the bottom ground, a new mower and replace the netting in the nets. We rely heavily on few volunteers who in addition to busy everyday lives commit time and effort to ensure teams, coaching and grounds are maintained. But we need more fresh engagement across the spectrum of the club, social, fundraising/commercial, playing, welfare, girls and ladies cricket.
Your club needs you; it is very simple.
We will continue to provide development opportunities for our junior players. In conjunction with the Andy Biddle Foundation, we have supported nearly 10 young players to achieve coaching qualifications to enable them to coach, develop, earn and put back into the club eco-system.
As a club we aim to be progressive and inclusive and we welcome initiatives that support our development. The club will introduce updates to membership and playing codes of conduct including anti-bullying and ECB Anti -Discrimination codes. Additionally, we will run educational development sessions prior to the commencement of the season to support players, coaches, managers to ensure all are aware of expected behaviour and standards.
The work undertaken by Mike Hepburn and his team of volunteers to get the grounds into working condition – preparing nets for use, mowing and tending to the area outside of the club etc can’t be underestimated. The turnout for leaf collection this weekend will help with both pitches being cleared before the end of November. Thanks, must also go to Jamie Ross, John Robinson, Peter Bell and Neil Houghton for the additional work in front of the club, making sure we have lovely surroundings to enjoy.
Thanks also for the paid work done by Lee Thomason and Phil Oliver to ensure both wickets were in great condition and were able to support the increased volume of cricket played this year. In particular the increased number of age group county matches supported including an Under-18 WCCC 3-day match, the high level of our grounds was a credit to the grounds team.
The club entered teams into all leagues and competitions available – 1st, 2nd, 3rd and 4th Saturday teams; Sunday 1st XI plus Sunday 2nd team and a Vets team. In addition to junior teams from U10 - U17 and Friday night All Stars…. players representing Sutton Coldfield from 9 through to 59 years old.
We have also had numerous younger players represent either district or county age group teams following in the footsteps of Felix Haines and Shailan Srikanthan who have played in the Warwickshire Emerging players teams.
Thank you for the continued support from the junior section led by Andy Neale, who’s commitment and enthusiasm has benefitted the club immensely. Again, an incredible amount of work put in on a voluntary basis to ensure our young players are supported and developed so that they become and stay as Sutton Coldfield cricketers through their life.
We were saddened by the loss of Ken Lancaster this year. A great influence in his senior years to many players and a shining example of how to keep doing something you love despite age!
We now prepare for 2023 season with indoor nets being arranged, dinner to be organised and budgets being finalised.
We are urgently recruiting for a Welfare officer required for 2023
1st XI 2022 Playing Report
Thanks to Jack for starting as skipper during the summer. He passed the armband to Aaron Thomason mid-season.
After avoiding relegation in 2021 there was a determined push for top half finish in 2022.The season started very well but unfortunately, we lost some momentum towards the end of the year.
The 1st XI finished 3rd with 316 points above close rivals Walmley but behind Attock and Coventry and North Warks, who were promoted into the Birmingham Premier League.
Lee Thomason ended the year as highest run scorer with 837 runs including 4, 50’s and 3, 100’s at an average of 52.31
Jack Smith scored 614 runs at 43.86 and 4 50’s and 1 100.
Raul Ram also contributed with 483 runs always scored at a crucial time.
With the ball Felix Haines led the way with 33 wickets at 20.09, 9th best in the league.
Jacob contributed 25 wickets @ 21.04.
2nd XI 2022 Playing Report
After surviving to remain in Division 5 in 2021, Sutton Coldfield CC 2nd Xl had an amazing turn around and hats off to all that have been involved.
The tables speak for itself, champions of Division 5 the 2nd Xl accumulated 352 points, including 15 wins. A fantastic achievement for the club which tied in with the 175th Anniversary.Certain players were exceptional however it was a squad effort - the time effort and everything that happens in the background was well worth it as a captain objective is to win silverware for the club and that was achieved this year.
Spencer GN - ended up 5th in the league with 704 runs at average of 41.41.
Nadeem Ahmed - ended up with 43 wickets with an average of 13.53.
I would like to thank certain individuals in the club who have given me this opportunity to demonstrate my leadership skills, communication and structure within the 2nd Xl.
Thanks, Nick Baker, for your ongoing support.
James Ross - for having faith and agreeing to give me the opportunity.
Geoff Gray-Nimmo - Fantastic umpire all season for us.
Martin Ball - helping me by being a vice captain.
Jack Smith - sharing and spreading knowledge from the higher leagues along with tactics and experience which has been passed down.
The aim for Division 4 is building on the skeletal, improving in certain areas and making players fight for their position to earn a place in the 2nd Xl.
We are moving in the right direction; However, we will need to plan ahead for: -1. Player recruitment
2. 2nd Xl coach
3. Structure for training batting, bowling and field
My sincere apologies I could not attend today - onwards and upwards for next year.
Nas Ali. 2nd XI Captain
2022 Season SCCC 3rd XI
An early start to a new season in Div 7, following promotion, was a shock to the system, but soon got into winning ways in week 2 with an away win at Attock 4s. The start of the season meant we were missing a few regulars, either due to players still at university, leaving the team or promoted to higher teams and this started to tell as we were starting to experience 50 over games and WL formats for the 1st time in most player cases.
With a fair bunch of our regular experienced players generally unavailable or missing we persevered with a well-earned LD away at a tricky pitch in Moseley against Pickwick P&M just before WLD came in for early summer. By this time, we had already got on the end of a few defeats, with a general failure of amassing too few runs in most cases. Come summer and mid-season we still struggled to field a consistent side with many 4th team players being drafted in to help the 3rds – congratulations and thanks to those 4th team guys for stepping up to helping out, particularly at a time when we were experiencing selection shortfalls higher up in the club.
End of season seen the 3rd team rally and beat 2 strong teams who eventually finished 2nd and 5th in the league, a statement indeed made to all around that SCCC 3rds can play at this level.
All in all, and despite the stats of: 14 Losses, 2 draws, 2 abandoned, 3 wins.
The team did their best given the short comings and should be well prepared for a season of 45 over games in Div 8 next year.
Players of note and worth mentioning:
Will Burford – Batting
Ollie Wright – all rounder
Jacob Lee - all-rounder (mostly 2nd team appearances)
Anuraag Guha – all rounder.
Asim Ashfaq – batting and vice Capt support. Jack HW – Batting.
Dave Hands 3rd XI Captain
Sutton Coldfield 4ths Div 9 North West.
Final Position: 2nd and runners up (promoted?) Won -13. Drawn - 2. Lost -2. Abandoned - 1.A fantastic season, improving from last year’s 3rd position to 2nd and runners up - potentially being promoted (subject to the 3s relegation and divisional splits in the 8th division?).We only lost to Bridge trust old boys 2nds who ended up winning the league and being unbeaten all season - and we almost beat them at home after losing the toss and batting first.
Looking back, this has been a massive achievement as, with being at the bottom of the selection chain, we mostly had a different 11 every week. However, despite this lack of continuity the personnel at disposal bought into the ethos and played some outstanding cricket.Honours were spread across the board every week, with juniors and seniors alike putting in match winning performances. In total, I think 35 players played at differing times for the 4th XI, 15 or 16 were from the junior section.
I would like to thank everybody - players, parents, captains groundsman and the committee for their support this past season. It is greatly appreciated.
Things going forward -
Clarification from the WCL on promotion and relegation status of the 3rd and 4th team.With a burgeoning junior section, the opportunity for adult cricket merged with the growth in adult membership remains in my mind an area that needs attention. A fifth team is a logistical issue due to ground availability, but a 2nd team on a Sunday would be a workable option as we have the ground availability.
Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club Adopts ECB Anti-Discrimination Code of Conduct - Action for all members.
BY NICK BAKER    29 DECEMBER 2022
To ensure Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club is aligned to ECB guidelines and to provide all our players, members and spectators with a safe and enjoyable environment it was agreed during summer 2022 and ratified at committee meeting and AGM that all members sign up to the Code of Conduct as part of their membership.
When you sign into your Membership via Pitchero you will see an Action Required for each member under your household to agree to the Code of Conduct. Please ensure you read the
Code of Conduct found in documents and tick the agree button from the drop-down menu.
Documents associated to the Code of Conduct can be found on the Website:
SCCC Documents
Sutton Coldfield Cricket ClubSutton Coldfield Cricket Club
Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club
Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club
CONSTITUTION
l. Title
The Cricket Section shall be known as Sutton Coldﬁeld Cricket Club and shall be one of
the constituent sections of Sutton Coldﬁeld Cricket and Hockey Club Limited.
(a) Sutton Coldﬁeld Cricket and Hockey Club is a company limited by guarantee and each paid-up member(s) of the Cricket Club has a liability of one pound. in the event of the Limited Company being wound up.
(b) The Limited Company is controlled by a General Purposes Committee (GPC) who have ultimate control over the Cricket Club, and its appointed officers, and all its Members. The Cricket Club is bound by the Rules of the Limited Company as specified in the Memorandum and Articles of Association.
(c) Sutton Coldﬁeld Cricket Club, hereafter referred to as ‘the Club', shall be affiliated to the England and Wales Cricket Board (ECB) through the Warwickshire County Cricket Board.
2. Aims and Objectives
(a) Mission Statement
To strive to play cricket at the highest level possible.
To provide cricketing facilities and opportunities for all members of the community, regardless of age, gender, race, nationality or ability.
(b) Aims
(i) To develop the Club through a structured Development Plan.
To foster sportsmanship and team spirit through the game of cricket.
To retain Focus Club and Clubmark status.
(ii) To encourage ladies and girls to start and maintain a place for a ladies’ section.
(iii) To encourage participation by disabled players by developing and strengthen in the whole club environment and promoting cricketing opportunities for disabled people.
(iv) Open the Club facilities to all members of the local community. Developing and promoting cricket for Black, Asian and other ethnic groups.
(v) To enable individual players to improve and develop their skills and abilities and fulfil their cricketing potential by offering good ground and training facilities and structured
coaching at the appropriate level by qualified coaching personnel.
(vi) To foster and promote the sport of cricket at all levels within the community and within
the sport. providing opportunities for recreation, coaching and
(vii) To ensure that all members, playing and non-playing. abide by the ECB Code of
Conduct which incorporates the Spirit of Cricket and the Laws of Cricket.
(viii) To ensure a duty of care to all members of the Club by adopting and implementing the
ECB ‘Safe Hands - Cricket’s Policy for Safeguarding Children' and any future versions of the Policy.
(ix) To ensure a duty of care to all members of the Club by adopting and implementing the ECB Cricket Equity Policy and any future versions of the Policy.
(x) To encourage all members to participate fully in the activities of the Club.
3. Membership
(a) Membership of the Club shall be open to anyone interested in the sport of cricket on application, regardless of sex, age, disability, ethnicity, nationality, sexual orientation, religion or other beliefs. However, limitation of membership according to available facilities is allowable on a non-discriminatory basis.
(b) The Club may have different classes of membership and subscription on a non-discriminatory and fair basis. The Club will keep subscriptions at levels that will not pose a significant obstacle to people participating.
(c) Application for membership must be in writing, proposed and seconded, by two members and be elected to Membership by the Cricket Club Committee.
Applications for Membership shall be made on a dated Application Form and when completed, details of the name of the applicant together with seconder shall be displayed in the Clubhouse for at least seven days prior to the election of the proposed Member by the Cricket Club Committee. No other personal details will be displayed.
(d) No person shall be eligible to take part in the business of the Club or eligible for selection for any club team unless the appropriate subscription has been paid by the specified date or membership has been agreed by the Committee.
(e) The Club Committee may refuse membership, or remove it, only for good cause such as conduct or character likely to bring the Club or cricket into disrepute. Appeal against refusal or removal may be made to the Committee who shall appoint an Appeals Committee to hear the appeal.
(f) All members will be subject to the regulations of the Constitution and by joining the Club will be deemed to accept these regulations and any Codes of Conduct that The Club has adopted. The Constitution shall identify those members eligible to vote at any General Meeting.
(g) A Temporary Membership may be granted to visitors who are involved with cricket
matches, or other organised functions, on the ground, or in the Clubhouse. A visitors'
book is to be present to record any necessary details.
(h) The Membership Secretary shall keep a Register of all Members.
4. Classes of Membership
The membership shall be made up of the following categories.
(a)  Members with voting rights:
Life Member
Vice President
Senior Playing Member (over 18 years of age)
Non-Playing Member (over 18 years of age)
(b) Members without voting rights:
Student Member (in full-time education)
Junior Playing Member (Under 18 years of age at the beginning of the current year)
Social Member
Family Member
Temporary Member
A list of members in each category shall be maintained by the Treasurer and Membership Secretary. Parents, guardians or carers of children who become junior members automatically become Family Members without voting rights.
5. Officers
The Officers of the Club shall be as follows:
Executive Officers:
President
Chairperson
Secretary
Treasurer
Club Welfare Officer
The affairs of the Club shall be conducted by a Management Committee comprising the Executive Officers of The Club and a number of other members elected by Full Members with voting rights of the Club e.g., Ground Chairperson, Captains, Social Secretary, Junior Representatives, Web Site Manager, Membership Secretary, Fixture Secretary, Match Secretary, 1st XI Captain, Vice-Chairperson etc.
Honorary Officers (if applicable):
President — ex offico.
The election of Life Members and Vice Presidents is agreed by the General Committee following a nomination and seconded by two members of the General Committee. subject to ratification by the President and then passed by a majority vote. The award for Life Membership is for long and meritorious service either on or off the ﬁeld of play and for Vice President is for general service to the Club.
6. Election of Officers
All Officers shall be elected at the Annual General Meeting of the Club by the members of the Club with voting rights.
All Officers shall be elected for a period of one year but shall be eligible for re- election to the same office or to another office the following year.
Where there are two or more nominations for the same position, election shall be by a secret ballot and a majority vote of those present at the meeting.
7. Management Committee
The affairs of the Club shall be conducted by a Management Committee comprising of:
The Executive Officers of the Club and a number of other members elected by Full
Members of the Club with voting rights.
Only these members of the Committee shall be entitled to vote at Committee meetings. The Management Committee will be convened by the Secretary and shall be held at agreed intervals and not less than four times per year. The quorum required for business to be agreed at Committee meetings shall be five (5) persons, to include at least two Executive Officers
The duties of the Committee shall be:
To control the affairs of the Club on behalf of the members.
a) To keep accurate accounts of the finances of the Club through the Treasurer. These should be available for reasonable inspection by members and should be audited before every Annual General Meeting. The Club shall maintain a bank current account and the following Officers shall be authorised to sign club cheques. The Treasurer and one or other of the Chairperson and Secretary.
b) To co-opt additional members onto the Committee as the Committee feel is necessary. Co-opted members shall not be entitled to a vote on the Committee and shall serve until the next Annual General Meeting.
c) To make decisions on the basis of a simple majority vote. In the case of equal votes, the Chairperson shall be entitled to the casting vote.
d) The Management Committee will appoint five members to serve on the General Purposes Committee
The Secretary shall maintain an Attendance Register, a summary of which shall be available at the Annual General Meeting.
The Committee shall have powers to appoint sub-committees as necessary and to co-opt advisers who may be non-club members invited to advise on specialist subjects.
An elected Committee member ceases to be such if they relinquish membership of the Club, resign by written notice, or is removed by the Committee for good cause after the member concerned has been given the chance of putting their case to the Committee. Appeal against removal may be made to the Appeals Committee. The Committee shall fairly decide time limits and formalities for these steps. The Appeals Committee shall comprise of three people appointed on an ad hoc basis.
The Committee has the power subject to The GPC Articles of Association to:
(a) Acquire and provide grounds, equipment, coaching, training and playing facilities, clubhouse, transport, medical and related facilities.
(b) Provide coaching, training, medical treatment, and related social and other facilities
(c) Take out any required insurance for club players.
(d) Raise funds by appeals, subscriptions, loans, charges and grants
(e) Borrow money and give security for the same and open bank accounts
(f) Set aside funds for special purposes or as reserves.
(g) Invest funds in any lawful manner
(h) Employ and engage staff and others and provide services.
(I) Co-operate with or affiliate of any bodies regulating or organising the sport of cricket or any club or body involved with cricket and with Government and related agencies.
(j) Undertake whatever is reasonably necessary to advance the aims and objectives of The Club.
None of the above powers may be used other than to advance the Aims and Objectives in a manner consistent with The Constitution and as required by Law.
8. General Meetings
(i) ANNUAL GENERAL MEETING
The Annual General Meeting of The Club shall be held not later than the end of November each year. No more than fifteen months shall expire between such meetings. Twenty-one (21) clear day written notice of the Annual General Meeting shall be given to members by circulating a copy of the notice to every member at their home address, or via email, and posting the notice on The Club notice board.
Members must advise the Secretary in writing of any other business to be moved at the Annual General Meeting at least fourteen (14) days before a meeting. The Secretary shall circulate or give notice of the agenda for the meeting to members not less than fourteen (14) days before the meeting.
The business of the Annual General Meeting shall be to:
(a) Presentation of Chairperson's and Secretary's reports.
(b) Receive and approve the accounts for the year from the Treasurer.
(c) Confirm the minutes of the previous Annual General Meeting.
(d) Elect the Officers of the Club (The President, Chairperson etc.).
e) Review the Club subscription rates and agree them for the forthcoming year.
f) Transact such other business received in writing by the Secretary from members fourteen to twenty-one (14/21) days prior to the meeting and included on the agenda.
Nominations of candidates for election of Officers shall be made in writing to the Secretary at least fourteen (14) days in advance of the Annual General Meeting date. Nominations can only be made by Full Members and seconded by Full Members with voting rights.
(ii) Extraordinary General Meetings may be convened by the Committee or on receipt by the Secretary of a request in writing from not less than ten (10) Full Members with voting rights at least twenty-one (21) days’ notice of the meeting shall be given.
At all General Meetings, the Chair will be taken by the Chairperson or, in their absence. by a deputy appointed by Full Members with voting rights attending the meeting. Decisions made at a General Meeting shall be by a simple majority vote from those Full Members with voting rights attending the meeting. In the event of equal votes. the Chairperson of the meeting shall be entitled to an additional casting vote.
A quorum for a General Meeting shall be fifteen (15) Full Members with voting rights and Officers of the Club including at least two (2) from the Chair, Secretary and Treasurer.
Only Full Members with voting rights of the Club shall be entitled to a vote at General Meetings.
9. Alterations to the Constitution
Any proposed alterations to the Club Constitution must be submitted to the GPC for ratification and then considered at an Annual or Extraordinary General Meeting, convened with the required written notice of not less than one month of the proposal. Any alteration or amendment must be proposed by a Full Member with voting rights of the Club and seconded by another Full Member with voting rights. Such alterations shall be passed it supported by not less than two-thirds of those Full Voting Members present at the meeting, assuming that a quorum has been achieved.
10. Finance
(a) All club monies shall be banked in an account in the name of the Club
(b) The Treasurer shall be responsible for all the finances of the Club and for providing a report on the financial position as required by the Committee
(c) Subject to Committee approval the Treasurer may if required delegate the management of sub-committee funds.
(d) The Treasurer shall ensure that the Club maintains adequate and appropriate insurance to cover the activities of The Club.
(e) The financial year will end on October 31st.
(f) The Treasurer will present the statement of annual accounts at the Annual General Meeting.
(g) Any cheque drawn against club funds should hold the signatures of the Treasurer and either the Chairperson or Secretary.
(h) Annual Subscriptions will be agreed at the AGM to cover all classes of membership. This can be delegated to the Management Committee.
(I) Match fees, which may include the payment of match teas, will be determined by the Management Committee.
11 Property and Funds
(a) The property comprising the buildings on both grounds is owned and maintained by the GPC.
(b) The funds of the Cricket Club cannot be used for the direct or indirect private beneﬁt of members other than as reasonably allowed by the Constitution. All surplus income or profits shall be reinvested in the Club.
(c) The Club may also in connection with the sports purposes of the Club:
Sell and supply food, drink and related sports clothing and equipment.
Employ members and remunerate them for providing goods and services, on fair terms set by the Committee without the person concerned being present.
Pay for reasonable hospitality for visiting teams and guests.
Indemnify the Committee and members acting properly in the course of the running of The Club against any liability incurred, but only to the extent of its assets.
12. Discipline and Appeals
All complaints regarding the conduct of members should be lodged in writing with the Secretary. These will be dealt with in accordance with the disciplinary procedure laid out in the Fixture Book and displayed in the Clubhouse.
13. Dissolution (Subject to the GPC Articles of Association)
(a) If at any General Meeting of The Club, a resolution be passed calling for the dissolution of the Club, the Secretary shall immediately convene an Extraordinary General Meeting of the Club to be held not less than thirty-one (31) days thereafter but within six weeks of the hearing of the resolution.
(b) If at that Extraordinary Meeting. the resolution is carried by at least two—thirds of the Full Voting Membership present at the meeting, the Committee shall thereupon, or at such a date as shall have been specified in the resolution, proceed to realise the assets of the Cricket Club and discharge all debts and liabilities of the Cricket Club.
(c) The Management Committee will then be responsible for the orderly winding up of the Club’s affairs.

Classes of Membership
Changes to Classes of Membership and Voting Rights, proposed by the committee and presented by the Secretary as follows: -
The membership shall be made up of the following categories.
(a)  Members with voting rights:
Vice-President (1) (1b)
Life Member (1) (1c)
Adult Member Playing (over 18 years of age)
Adult Member Non-Playing (over 18 years of age) (2)
Household Membership (Playing) (3) (5)
(b) Members without voting rights:
Student Member (Over 18 years of age at the beginning of the academic year and in full-time education)
Junior Member (Under 18 years of age at the beginning of the academic year)
Household Membership (Non-Playing) (4) (5)
Social Member (6)
Temporary Member (7)
(1). Nominations for (a) Life Members or (b) Vice-President must first be submitted to The President together with the name of (a) The Proposer and (b) The Seconder. Once the nominee(s) is/are ratified by The President they are then referred to the Management Committee for agreement and then passed by a majority vote.
1(b). The award for Vice President is for general service to the Club.
1(c). The award for Life Membership is for long and/or meritorious service either on or off the ﬁeld of play.
(2). An Adult Member Non-Playing is a registered Member of the Club who is over 18 years of age and actively participates in the Club's activities, including administration, coaching, management but does not participate in a game of cricket.
(3). A Family Member (Playing) is a registered Member of the Club who is over 18 years of age, and is the parent, guardian, or carer of children who are registered junior members and actively participates in the Club's activities, including administration, coaching, management etc.
(4). A Household Membership (Non-Playing) is the parent, guardian, or carer of children who are registered junior members but do not actively participate in the Club's activities.
(5). A Household Membership consists of up to two adults and up to three children under 18 years of age. Each additional child will incur an additional membership fee.
(6). A Social Member will be granted Full Voting Rights when that member actively participates in the Club's activities, including administration, coaching, or management (including as a Committee Member) etc.
(7). A Temporary Membership may be granted to visitors who are involved with cricket
matches, or other organised functions, on the ground, or in the Clubhouse. A visitors'
book is to be present to record any necessary details.
A list of members in each category shall be maintained by the Treasurer and Membership Secretary. Parents, guardians, or carers of children who become junior members will automatically be granted Household Membership without voting rights.
A vote was taken and the proposal was passed with no objections or abstentions.
Parking Charges proposed by Birmingham C.C. for Rectory Park
Birmingham City Council have for a number of months been considering car park charges for both Sutton and Rectory Parks. We have a new Councillor in Richard Parkin who’s Ward Rectory Park now falls’ within.
A couple of discussion took place between the Sutton Coldfield Cricket & Hockey Club management and Richard during the last quarter of 2022. A strongly worded letter, objecting to any parking charges being imposed in Rectory Park by BCC. The letter pointed out that Rectory Park was a sporting environment available to all living, not just in Sutton Coldfield, but the eastern conurbation of Birmingham.
The BCC met on 8th January 23 to discuss Parking Charges, and we were delighted to receive back from Richard that Cllr Majid Mahood, who has the responsibility for parks had dropped their plans to introduce parking at both Rectory Park and Sutton Park. The response was so negative on the feedback received from residents, plus the cost-of-living issues had forced a rethink of their plans. Cllr Parkin referred to our letter, stating that it will have undoubtedly contributed to the wave of negative reaction to the car parking charge
Obituary to Paul Francis McCarthy
Paul Francis McCarthy was born on 7th September 1934, the youngest of 5 children born to Harold and Jean McCarthy (nee Shaw). He was predeceased by his brothers John and David and his sisters Lorema and Jeanette.
Paul was educated at Downside where he won a scholarship to Oxford University at Hertford College to read Law. While at Hertford he represented them at cricket, hockey and table tennis. On leaving College he was articled to Herbert William Lyde of GT Smith & Lyde, and passed the Law Society finals in 1958.
Once qualified, he was called up for National Service into the Army and was posted to Hong Kong. However, he never disclosed to the military that he was a lawyer preferring to spend his time as a private rather than being commissioned to work within military law – King’s Regulations. Paul did distinguish himself by representing, the Army in Hong Kong at hockey in their 2nd Xl and in the 1st Xl. Unfortunately, while in Hong Kong, he contracted Meningitis, which was to trouble him for many years after he was demobilised from the Army in the Autumn of 1960. Once back in the UK, he re-joined Scadding, Jessop & Loffet and became a partner in April 1964 – However on the deaths of Jessop and Scadding, Paul took over the firm as a Self-Practitioner, specialising in conveyancing.
Paul retired in 1994 and took great pleasure in travelling all over the world but in particular to Italy on what became a regular event to see operas. I can recall how enthralled he was after visiting the outdoor arena in Verona, to watch a performance of Verdi’s Aida. Not just the music but the size of the cast and yet the arena never looked crowded. Paul was really captivated. He visited his family in Ireland on a regular basis over a 20 plus year period and his nieces and nephews in France. He expressed many times his fondness for Ireland and its people and took a keen interest in researching his family history. He used his visits to Ireland to try and trace his Irish ancestors, but with the burning down of the Public Record Office of Ireland at the Four Courts in 1922 many of the census records were lost. This resulted in Paul contacting numerous Parish Priests to see their records of births, deaths and marriages.
Paul was scrupulously honest, an impeccable timekeeper, totally reliable and a great conversationalist able to discuss any topic, he was extremely well read but at times shook his fist at a world full of people that didn’t share these traits. Some may have thought that he drank a little too much, but others considered Paul to be somewhat a connoisseur on red wines.
Paul was an outstanding Hockey player, first with Aldridge and then with Sutton Coldfield and represented Staffordshire many times. mainly at left back, which is regarded as one of the most difficult positions in the game. In later years he captained the Sutton Coldfield Sunday side between 1970-71 season and 1978-79 season. He was also regular tourist with the Sutton Club to the Weymouth Easter Festival under the name of “The Strollers”.
Paul also supported the Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club and was a scorer for the 1st and 2nd Xls for a number of seasons, but I don’t recall that he ever played cricket for the Sutton Club. When the Sutton Coldfield Cricket & Hockey Clubs amalgamated to form a limited company in 1970 this coincided in the construction of a new pavilion, the one you see today, replacing some 5 buildings including an outside toilet. Paul was the Company Solicitor who drew up the Articles of Association.
Paul moved from Aldridge to reside in Thornhill Road while looking after his sick mother for many years and then his sister, Lorema, who was diagnosed with MS shortly after his mother died. This took a toll on Paul, but he took care of them both until the end, before moving to live in Coles Lane after Lorema, passed away, so as to be near the town centre as he had given up driving.
Sad to say in the last few years he became very reserved and isolated especially when he moved to Churchill Retirement Living on Church Road, Boldmere, but Paul loved living on his own and was very content in his own company. His Irish family epitaph on Paul was that he had a deep belief in honesty and a high degree of respect for the truth. He applied these principles to himself and those around him. This sometimes meant that he came into conflict with people he believed were not honest or truthful, but he was always true to himself and his own sense of integrity.
He passed away on the 30th November 2022, age 88 leaving 6 nieces and nephews. They were David’s children, Astrid, Eric and Patrick who live in France and John’s children Fergus, Kathryn and Judith who lives in Donegal in North West Ireland and who organised the funeral arrangements.
As they say in Ireland –Ní fheicfimid a leithéid arís. Ar dheis Dé go raibh a anam. Translated: We will never see the like again. May his soul be at the right hand of God.
Peter Bell & Judith McCarthy2nd February 2023
Annual Cricket Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club Annual Dinner
This was the first occasion that the annual dinner was held in April on the 6th at Moor Hall Golf Club, rather than the last Friday in January or the first Friday in February.
This was due to the concern members had about Covid, which was the reason than no annual dinner was held in the 2020 and 21 seasons.
SUTTON COLDFIELD CRICKET CLUB
Lovers of cricket, we’re back once more
To reflect on last season in the game we adore
In fact, in the year since I last did this grace,
The sport has developed at a dizzying pace.
The last time we met, England were down on their knees,
Beaten at home and then twice overseas.
Since then, New Captain, Coach, style and all
The answer to the problems, they call it Baz Ball.
Back in our day, you played yourself in,
Now that load of old cobblers has gone in the bin.
Our President, I saw him, had a pretty defence,
Hitting out straight away would have made him all tense.
But despite the misgivings, it’s worked like a charm,
Chasing down huge totals with enormous calm.
Bairstow was amazing, Brook looks like a star,
And the old buggers still bowling are the best by far.
Our guest for this evening has been part of that team,
For a decade, the Brummie Botham’s been living our dream.
However, as a youngster, against Sutton he played,
A local derby at Walmley and tempers were frayed.
We won’t hold it against him cos’ he’s done a fine job,
Even if on that day, I think I called him a knob!
So, I’m sorry for that Chris, and have a great year,
Season started today and league cricket now near.
Two things on our wish list for 2023,
Number 1, to see Sutton at the top of the tree.
The second sight for which we all yearn,
Is to see Stokesey with a smile and holding the urn.
Lord, we thank you, for good food, good wine and lively chat
And keep us playing with a nice straight bat.
Amen

He moved to Walmley, on the suggestion of the WCCC, so that he would play in a stronger level of cricket. Once he joined the County and playing for a living everything changed. It was in to not that Chris is only 1 of 8 cricketers whose name appear on both the Lords Honour Boards, scoring a century and taking 10 wickets in a game. A marvelous achievement.

The Club hosted the first Inter County LGBTQ+ Cricket Tournament.
The Country’s LGBTQ+ tournament in Birmingham is hailed
a success – and sends a powerful sporting inclusion message
Warwickshire celebrated Pride month in style by hosting the first ever LGBTQ, cricket tournament, with Middlesex crowned inaugural champions.
Sides representing five county clubs Warwickshire. Worcestershire, Gloucestershire, Middlesex and Northamptonshire - competed in the event at Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club on Sunday 11th June 23.
Around 200 spectators were present, contributing to a festival feel for the eight-a-side T10 tournament. Middlesex lifted the Inaugural trophy after progressing through the round-robin format undefeated with their players coming from Graces CC. the world's first LGBTQ+ cricket club, which was founded in 1996.
A late downpour brought a premature end to the event - washing out the final round of games - and at one stage the West Midlands Air Ambulance stopped play as it landed on the outfield to collect paramedics from the nearby Good Hope Hospital.
But nothing could detract from the success of the event, which was organised by Warwickshire Cricket in conjunction with Birmingham Unicorns, one of only two LGBTQ+ cricket clubs in the UK.
Unicorns chair Lachlan Smith said: "This is a big achievement: a great crowd turned up, there was some good quality cricket on show, and it demonstrates there is a big appetite in the LGBTQ+ community to play and support cricket”.
"Warwickshire came up with the Idea. It's been hard work pulling it together, but so worthwhile. We're highlighting that cricket is a game for all. We want to encourage more LGBTQ+ people to come and watch, umpire, score and play”.
"If we've opened just a few eyes in the LGBTQ+ community that cricket is a sport for them then it's been a success”.
"Cricket has its challenges, some are well documented, but LGBTQ+ inclusion has come on leaps and bounds in the last few years. The direction of travel is very positive."
International umpire and former England player Sue Redfern took charge of one game having hot-footed over from New Road, Worcester, where she had been officiating in the Charlotte Edwards Cup final. A week earlier she had become the Vitality Blast's first female umpire.
Redfern added: it was important for me to be here today. It's another milestone in LGBTQ+ sport and cricket's journey to becoming Inclusive for all.
"LGBTQ+ people may have thought cricket isn't for them because they don't see it, they don't see participation in the network. That's why this is fabulous for the game, the visibility It brings and that will encourage others to get involved.
"There is still work to do, no doubt, but by doing this it sends a message that we won't tolerate non-Inclusive behaviour. It's been a long time coming but we're here and we can build on this."
Warwickshire CCC's high performance manager Paul Greetham, the newly appointed vice-chair of the ECB's LGBTQ+ Committee, said: "it's great to see so many people here from the LGBTQ+ community involved, playing a sport, they love and being their authentic selves. This is my home club, I’ve played here for 20 years, so this is a proud moment for me.
"This is a community that's felt under-represented in sport. But Warwickshire and Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club have thrown open their arms, saying it doesn't matter who you are, how you identify, what your background is, or who you hold hands with on the boundary rope - this is a sport for you."

Presidents Day
Our President, James Ross had invited past players and Life Members to join him for a Fork & Finger Buffet at 3 pm and to share wine with him on the 8th July, while the 1st team Xl were entertaining Solihull Municipal. This is the second consecutive season that rain has interfered with the cricket.
The First Win in sight was Defeated by a Horrendous Rain Storm.
The 1st team have not been having the best of season, due to a number of players seeking other opportunities. However, the game on Saturday 8th July 23, the tide appeared to have turned. Sutton batting first, scored 220 all out in 44.5 overs; Dhruv Malhi 47, Yuvrajsinh Shinol 46 and Aaron Thomason 64. Solihull Municipal in reply were 54 for 5 wickets after 29 overs (Aaron Thomason 2 for 23 runs in 11 overs and Smir Shabir 2 for 10 in 8 overs)
The heaviest rain storm arrived flooding the ground in less than 15 minutes. Sutton had a winning draw with 16 points and Municipal 6 point in a losing draw. The storm had robbed us of a potential first win in the season, but if that was not bad enough the league penalized the Club 10 points for playing an ineligible player.
“They say it never rains but it pours.”

Mid-Season - Selection Update by our Chairperson, Nick Baker on 14th July 23
A message regarding selection to all players at Sutton Coldfield CC.
This has been a difficult season with three of our adult teams languishing near the bottom of their respective league tables. During winter we lost several players from the club through a variety of reasons, and several of them from the 1st XI. This has obviously had an impact on results across all the teams, with several players having to play at a higher standard than they may have envisaged at the start of the season.
We have managed to recruit some talented young players and with our own very successful youth squads at the club the future is definitely bright!
However, we have had several players who this year who when asked to step up to a higher-level team and have declined the opportunity and have therefore not supported the club in its overall aims to be successful across all of the respective divisions.
For those who are unsure of the priorities within the club, our main aim is to keep the first XI in the Warwickshire Premier league, followed by aiming to keep the second Xl in the current division, followed by the third X1 and the fourth Xl.
With this in mind the decision has been taken from the chairman, the cricket manager and the first team captain to take responsibility for the selection of all four Saturday teams with the input of each of the captains, with the clear aim to get the strongest possible teams out on a Saturday with the priority being the 1st Xl.
This approach although drastic will help to give our teams the best chance of staying up in their respective decisions.
Based on this, ALL players need to make sure that they have completed their availability on Pitchero by Sunday evening, enabling the captains and myself to start to prepare selection for the following Saturday.
Based on availability teams will be selected, if you are suddenly unavailable to play for the team you have been selected in, you will not be considered for selection at all that weekend (including Sundays).
There will always be some exceptional circumstances players may consider making them unavailable be these need to be communicated to myself (Rob Haines) ASAP. Based on this we will endeavour to produce solutions that enable us to field as strong as teams as possible.
I hope you are all able to support this approach to give us the best chance of success.
Thanks for your co-operation.
RegardsRob, Nick and Aaron

The Warwickshire Cricket League v The Warwickshire County Club
The Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club hosted a 20-twenty over match between the County’s 1st team (The Bears) and a represented side of our league with Lee Thomason being in the leagues team on Tuesday 1st August 2023.
Prior to the game that commenced at 5 pm a soft ball competition was held on the lower ground for youngsters of both genders with a number of coloured gazebos erected on the ground.
The Bears were invited to bat first and scored 230 runs with some mighty hitting with at least 6 sixes, with the ball being hit out of the ground. The league side in their reply struggled a little against real first team bowlers, but they performed admirably to finish with 150 runs on the board. The Bears were generous in the last two overs by bowling slow, (probably spin bowlers) allowing the WCCL side to show what they could do with the occasional bowlers. This was much to the delight of the spectators and the youngsters with a number of sixes being achieved. After the game the youngsters mingled with the players collecting autographs.
The event ran smoothly, using both grounds, showed our facilities and what is available at SCCC and to the local community. A lot of planning must have gone into organising the event.  I don’t normally enter my personal view on what is the history of SCCC, but on this occasion I attended the event with my old colleague of the 1970’s - 80’s, Bob Strachan and we both enjoyed sitting on the bench, thought the afternoon, talking about the past and what we missed when you compare cricket of today. A great day’s cricket.
The attendance had been, as an estimate, around 300 to 400 adult present and well over 150 youngsters watching the game, with the nets being used as a Bar-b-que production unit. I don’t normally mention bar financial figures but over £4,000 were taken at the event.
A quick financial overview of £920 from entrance which was voluntary and hat sales plus £965 from the Bar-b-que less costs for meat, bread rolls and gas estimated at £430 and the cost of catering for players and guests £350.

What Warwickshire County Cricket Club had to Say!
Bears Roadshow enjoyed another successful outing as hundreds of people flocked to Sutton Coldfield for an evening of cricket and community fun.
A strong Bears side – including Club captain Will Rhodes, Jake Lintott and Rob Yates – took on a Best of Warwickshire League XI in a T20 clash at the Rectory Park ground on Tuesday (1 Aug).
They came out on top by 66 runs after Ed Barnard (89 off 42 balls) and Hamza Shaikh (50 off 26) powered the Bears set a target of 230. Solihull Blossomfield CC all-rounder Chris Kenny scored a half century in just 14 balls but the hosts’ chase fell short on 164/7.
But there was also plenty of off-field fun as the event started with an under-10s softball competition before Bears players spent time coaching youngsters, signing autographs and posing for photographs.
Sutton Coldfield CC Chair Nick Baker said: “It was a great occasion and a fantastic advert for cricket. The players joined our softball game and spent time coaching the youngsters, taking selfies with fans and signing autographs. “There was a big crowd, several hundred people, and a lovely atmosphere. Everyone I spoke to had a great time and it would be brilliant to see the Bears back at our club again in the near future.” There was also a welcome sight for Bears fans as Liam Norwell opened the bowling as he continues his recovery from a back injury. Norwell took the first wicket while 17-year-old Theo Wylie – who’s signed a rookie contract with Warwickshire for Metro Bank One Day Cup – took 2/15 and fellow newcomer Michael Booth also claimed a wicket.
Bears First Team Coach Mark Robinson, said: “We want to maintain strong links with our local sides, inspiring future generations of cricketers, so taking the Club out into the community with events like this are really important.”
What the WCCL had to Say!
The Warwickshire League representative side will feature leading players from clubs including Aston Manor, Bedworth, Handsworth, Olton and West Warwickshire, Rugby, Stratford-upon-Avon, Streetly, Sutton Coldfield and Walmley.
Warwickshire Cricket Board’s Schools Participation Manager Chris Kenny – who plays for Solihull Blossomfield – is also set to take on his Bears colleagues.
David Woakes, Cricket & Grounds Chairman for the Warwickshire League, said: “We are really looking forward to another great fixture against the Bears as last year’s fixture was played in great spirit and allowed so many of our players to show their talent and ability.
“This year we have tried to ensure that we have an event split of the top players in our league. Our options for selection have been great, giving some real selection headaches and we are confident in the team being able to put in another strong performance.
“Sutton Coldfield is a fantastic community club, who do a lot within their local area. They have a great youth section and it will be fantastic to see the Bears players provide coaching at such a great venue.”
Notice
There is very limited car parking at Sutton Coldfield CC and spectators are encourage to use public transport. Sutton railway station is a 12-minute walk from the ground, plus there is more parking in the town centre.
The game is free to attend but there is a suggested £5 donation for adults.

A Vandals Night Attack
The Damage
The Repair

Obituary to Barrymore James Stokes
Barrymore James Stokes was simply known as Barry who was born on the 25th February 1930, and served 2-year National Service in the Royal Air Force, where his administration talent was recognised. He started his career in the motor industry with British Leyland at Longbridge and at Cowley, in Oxford working in their advertising and publicity business before becoming their manager until he left in 1966 to join James Beattie department store in Wolverhampton. There he worked his way to become the stores Assistant General Manager in the Mander Centre before being transferred in 1975 to open the new Beattie’s store in the Gracechurch Centre in Sutton Coldfield as their General Manager.
In 1953 Barry married Peggy and had a son David and a daughter Sue, but they were divorced soon after the family arrived to live in Walmley, Sutton Coldfield. Barry was the most well-turned-out gentleman, very smart and always in a suit and would hold conversation with any person regardless of their appearance. He was a gentleman who simply gather friends.
As soon as Barry came to reside in Sutton Coldfield, he moved from Beacon Cricket Club to join the Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club playing in their 2nd and 3rd Xl on Sundays. But the domestic arrangements with his ex-wife caused Barry to move his allegiance to Walmley Cricket Club, where his administration abilities were a great asset to his new club. In 1987 Barry became their Chair and in 1992 he was Chair again for 5 years, 1992 to 1996 inclusive, to be elected their President in 1997 until 2012, a period of 16 years.
Barry was also a member of the Management Board of Birmingham & District Premier Cricket League, from its inception in 1998 until 2009. Throughout that period, he was a member of the Ground & Facility Audit Committee. In 2008, Barry was appointed President of BDPCL.
He was also Chair and, on the Ground, & Facility Audit Committee of the Warwickshire Sunday League, until it was wound up in 2019. Barry was also a social member of Water Orton Cricket Club and would often been seen there most Sunday morning throughout the year.
In 2003 Barry married Christine and went to live in the outskirts of Tamworth, before moving back to reside in Sutton in June 2019. He passed away on the 29th June 2023 at the age of 93.
Peter Bell
19 August 2023.
Obituary to Peter Yates
Peter Joseph Yates was born on the 9th September 1930 in Sutton Coldfield and marred Pat on 29th October 1955 and had a son, Paul. Peter was educated at Bishop Vesey Gramar School and became a regular player in the Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club 1st team in 1953 as a left arm medium to fast swing bowler who moved the ball both ways and was a very useful batsman and his record speaks for itself.
Looking through the club’s historical records, Peter’s name first appears in the first Xl in 1953 playing on both Saturdays and Sundays for 14 seasons until the end of the 1962 season.
He played mainly in the 2nd team in 1963, before returning to the 1st team for 1964 to 67 seasons. He again returned to the 2nd team as their captain in 1968 and remained with that side until the end of the 1970 season. Peter continued playing infrequently for the 3rd Xl until the end of the 1978 season.
Peter’s bowling record from the club’s records book based on the season averages which covered the top 3 or 4 players averages. Peter was the top bowler in 1956 and 57, although his highest number of wickets in a season was 39 and 37 respectively at a cost of 13.7 and 16.3 runs per wicket.
Peter Yates
Peter, while in the 1st team took a minimum total of 370 wickets at an average 17.1 from over 2,500 overs outstanding achievement when you consider that the club had the bowling of Olsen and Pellow, who each took around 100 wicket per season with an average of around 15, but more important they took a wicket every 4 overs and 3 balls. Nevertheless, Peter playing in the 1st & 2nd teams taking over 500 wickets, bowling well over 3,100 overs at around 16 runs per wicket and at the rate of one wicket every 6 over and 3 balls. He was one of the elite bowlers, the 13th in the club’s history, to take 8 wickets in a 1st Xl game against the unfortunate Aston Unity CC, bowling 10.3 overs, 4 maidens, 27 runs at an average of 3.4. Peter also performed an excellent "hat trick" removing Burton's first three batsmen
Peter batting opportunities were limited, batting at No. 8, however, in 1955 season he scored 343 runs that season with an average of 17.1 and a highest score of 50 not out, which was probably his best season with the bat.
In the 1971 and 72 seasons Peter was the club’s secretary
In 1977 the Alpine Soft Drink Company launched an under 16 youth competition with Peter being the match manager and organizer of the first Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club’s junior side. He got together a very talented squad of some 18 young cricketers who went on to win the first competition for young cricketers in the Midlands to become the Alpine Under 16 Champion, led by captain, David Stokes, who went on to play in the Club’s 1st team as did a number of others players who went on to play in the club’s 2nd Xl team.
Peter’s sporting activities continued at Moor Hall Golf Club playing golf well into his 90’s although he played on a 9- hole section with likeminded players.
Peter held the Sutton Cricket Club’s longest paid-up membership of over 70 years, and ran a very successful business in Walsall in the leather trade, manufacturing mainly high-quality wallets and purses, which were on sale in Harrods.
Peter passed away on the 2nd August 2023, aged 92, leaving a wife, Pat a son, Paul and two grandchildren.
Peter Bell – 31st August 2023

Treasurer’s Report
Financial Report – Season 2023
A year of both significant levels of both income and expenditure with an overall loss on the year of £6080.00. There remains however £5000.00 in outstanding invoices - £4500.00 Sponsorship from Lanes and £500 ECB Contribution to LGBTQIA+ festival. Both have been chased but as at the date of this AGM remain outstanding. If paid the loss will reduce to £1080, slightly less than the £1264.00 budgeted at the beginning of the year.
To look at items in more detail starting with our income:
Membership & Subs: Membership overall was nearly £1500 below budget with Senior membership £1646.00 down (approx. 13 members) and Social down £350 (14 members). Junior membership was however above plan (£511.00). Membership is a key area and the loss and non- replacement of the Membership Secretary was a significant issue this year and one the new committee needs to address urgently.
A poor year weather wise resulted in lower match income at senior levels. Junior match fees however were significantly higher (£1245.00). Winter nets were well supported by both junior and senior sections, with the junior section organising activities pre-Xmas which made a useful start on this area of income. This resulted in excess of £828.00 above budget. All of the figures taken together for playing cricket meant we were broadly in line with budget.
Donations & Sponsorship: Once again the club is extremely grateful to all of our donators. Some of our donations are small monthly amounts which mount up through the year and others are generous amounts of single donations from Life & Vice Presidents but also friends of the club. This is something we should never take for granted.
Sponsorship has however been a problem this year. Even with the anticipated £4500.00 from Lanes we are £5,500 below budget. It should also be noted that as the Lanes sponsorship was not agreed until late in the season, the payment to be made will cover most of next season as well so this is an area that will require significant focus from the new committee.
Other Income
Income from the BBQ provided excellent extra income and we showed a profit of over £1840.00, so well done to all those who cooked and sold on Friday nights plus at the LGBTQA & WCL games.
Misc Income comes from a number of sources, £3347.00 was from transfers from Number 2 account for a new mower and work on the nets. These therefore are also shown on the other side of the accounts under Ground supplies (£950.00 for the mower) and Miscellaneous Expenditure (£2397.00 for the Nets). The other major sources of income here are £900 Entrance Fees for the WCL v WCCC game plus £650.00 contribution from WCB. If received the ECB contribution will be shown here.
Dinner/Social – Dinner showed an income of £3330.00. Additional revenue in this area came from the Players lunch/Presidents Day. Socials made a profit of just over £1000.00.
Turning to Expenditure
Budgets were well managed this year with the end of year figure broadly in line with budget. Many of the areas are known costs going into the season which is why most are at or close to the budgeted figure. Coaching costs in 2022 were particularly high hence the budget figure. These have been well manged by Andy Neal this year with the poor weather reducing
Senior coaching costs.
Player costs are lower than budget due to the weather. This clearly is an area that will not be incurred next season due to our relegation, which will help overall expenditure.
Ground Labour – the 3-year contract with TOP Grounds Management has come to an end and will need to be re-negotiated for next season.
Miscellaneous Expenditure: As noted earlier £2397.00 of this figure is a contra entry covering the costs of the nets paid for out of account 2. The other 2 biggest items were £1280.00 for mobile toilets. £500 of this should be offset by a contribution from the ECB and £650 from WCB.
Food and Fuel for the BBQ makes up a further £1132.00 with other major costs (over £100.00) being £475.00 for banners, £238.00 for cleaning, £190.00 for the WCB dinner, £150.00 donation to Dave Thornton memorial, and £110.00 Raffle money to Birmingham Unicorns
Looking ahead
To provide the standard of cricket and cricket facilities we all want at this club will continue to cost money. Costs will certainly increase. Given the challenging economic times we also need to be aware of the costs of playing cricket for our members. With this in mind raising money will be key whether through grants, sponsorship, or donations and this will be an important early discussion for the new committee.
After 5 years as Treasurer and 12 years as either Secretary, Chairman or Treasurer (on 2 occasions holding 2 roles at the same time), I have decided to take a break and will not be seeking re-election as Treasurer. Thank you to everyone who has supported me during this time and I hope the playing fortunes of the club will once again be on an upwards trajectory.
Club Chairman’s Report
2023 Season
Chairmans Report
Thanks to Steve Russell, Treasurer for the detailed report.
On behalf of the club, I must pass on my immense gratitude to Steve Russell, after completing 4 years as Chairman he has continued a further 4 years as treasurer, this is amazing service and as wonderful servant to SCCC, his involvement, calm steady hand and commitment must be lauded. We look forward to Kirsty as his successor picking up the reigns soon.
As is very evident from the financial report, as a volunteer run club, for non-commercial gain, we have to work hard to stand still. We rely on our members, sponsors, and benefactors to support the club to provide playing excellent facilities and coaching for all members, both junior and senior and provide opportunities for all to better themselves or just have good sporting experiences. Where there are shortfalls against projected budgets we have to work hard and ensure we cut our cloth accordingly especially in light of tough global and local economic conditions.
We rely heavily on few volunteers who in addition to busy everyday lives commit time and effort to ensure teams, coaching and grounds are maintained.
But we need continued fresh engagement across the spectrum of the club, social, fundraising/commercial, playing, welfare, girls’ and ladies’ cricket. We have support to drive girls and ladies’ cricket with Warwickshire committing specific coaching support in 2024 utilising donations from beneficiaries which should be supplemented by the club. We want to continue to ensure that SCCC is a welcoming club for both members and our guests, on and off the field.
Disappointingly, isolated off the field events have led to time consuming meetings and reviews drawing focus and attention away from the playing field and has led to the club embracing and adopting new policies - The ECB Use of social media Policy and the adoption of the ECB General Conduct Regulations to bring the club disciplinary policies up to date. Standards of which and expectations of SCCC high standards will be revisited pre-season 2024 with all players, parents and members.
On a more positive note, which is where we all want the focus of the club to be we have had some fantastic engagement and positive advocacy of the club in the local community.
The opportunity for SCCC to host a couple of headline events during the summer emphasised how the club can come together to be a beacon and focus for the local community. Through the contacts driven by Rob Haines recognizing the opportunity to strengthen ties with Warwickshire.
The first ever globally LGQBT County tournament was held on June 11th at Rectory Park. 6 teams representing Warwickshire, Middlesex, Leicester, and others played in a round robin format. The BBC and Sky Sports attended with both running pieces on the event. With well over 250 attending the SCCC volunteers provided first class cricketing facilities, hospitality, and a welcoming environment. The day was interrupted by the local air ambulance using the outfield for and emergency staff transfer from Good Hope Hospital and finally curtailed by tropical downpours at the end of the day. The feedback from Warwickshire and from the LGQBT community was resoundingly positive with a request for us to host again in 2024.
Sutton Coldfield CC also hosted the Warwickshire CC vs Birmingham Cricket League T20 match in July. As with the majority of the summer the wet weather loomed over the event. However, we managed to pick a date with a gap between downpours.
The junior engagement event prior to the main match brought Warwickshire coaches along with Hugh Bear the WCCC mascot to provide a brilliantly supported junior tournament – the main event provided the large crowd of up to 500 people close hand experience of first-class cricket at Rectory Park, thankfully we avoided any incidents with the ball flying to all parts! The WCCC staff and players thanked the club for a first-class event and again we have set the benchmark for future events.
The group of SCCC volunteers – players, juniors and parents helped set up, run the carpark – no small feat, run the BBQ (taking nearly £1k during the day) collect donations and keep the crowds entertained, thanks to Rob Haines again for his musical contributions! Although incredibly difficult to gauge the turnout for these events as they were the first of their kind run locally, we learnt and adapted as we went along and hopefully the template in place for future events. Contributions over the bar to the GPC will have been supplemented by these events and hopefully any surpluses that the GPC recognises this year can be re-invested into the clubhouse.
The work undertaken by Mike Hepburn and his team of volunteers to get the grounds into working condition – preparing nets for use, mowing and tending to the area outside of the club etc. can’t be underestimated. The turnout for leaf collection this weekend will help with both pitches being cleared before the end of November. Thanks, must also go to Jamie Ross, John Robinson, Peter Bell and Neil Houghton for the additional work in front of the club, making sure we have lovely surroundings to enjoy.
Thanks also for the paid work done by Lee Thomason and Phil Oliver to ensure both wickets were in great condition and were able to support the increased volume of cricket played this year. In particular the increased number of age group county matches supported including the festivals and multiple WCCC age group matches, the high level of our grounds was a credit to the grounds team, particularly in light of the torrid weather we have experienced this summer.
The club entered teams into all leagues and competitions available – 1st, 2nd,3rd and 4th Saturday teams, Sunday 1st XI plus Sunday 2nd team and a Vets team. In addition to junior teams from U10-U17 and Friday night All Stars…. players representing Sutton Coldfield from 9 through to 59 years old.
We have also had numerous younger players represent either district or county age group teams.
The junior section and club as a whole were rocked by the untimely death of Dave Thornton, the manager of the u15 team and parent to Marco a keen batsman. The junior players and friends of Marco rallied around him for the final few games and where a shining example as to how a team and club can help in adversity. Thanks, must also go to Martin Jones and Paul Wright to standing in to support the lads during this very difficult period.
Thank you for the continued support from the junior section led by Andy Neale, wholehearted dedication and enthusiasm has benefitted the club immensely. Again, an incredible amount of work put in on a voluntary basis to ensure our young players are supported and developed so that they become and stay as Sutton Coldfield cricketers through their life.
We now prepare for 2023 season with indoor nets being arranged and budgets being finalised.
1st XI Team Report
Obviously, it was quite a difficult and disappointing season on the field for the club. After losing five players preseason to various reasons, the 1XI were always going to be up against it! Add to that, we would have to take a fair few of the newly promoted 2XI, we would also hinder their season.
Despite the tough season there definitely were a couple of positives! Jacob Lee scoring a 1xi 50 and more than holding his own, as well as lots of youngsters earning a lot of experience- which can only help them in the future!
It may be a difficult period on the field for the club but there’s no doubt, with the strength of the club’s youth system, and the volume of volunteers at the club that the club will eventually get back to where it belongs.
Lee Thomason (1st XI team Captain)
2nd XI Team Report
From being champions of Division of 5, to being relegated from Division 4. It was two sides of the spectrum to experience to say the least.
We simply were not good enough, a team or a squad is based on a core of 6-8 players who play week in a week out.
Having an altered team each week didn’t allow any movement for momentum or positive results.
We gave it out our all with the players this year.
Selection was a nightmare, taking up all week to find a change in the team above or in my team.
Recruitment is a must for us to prosper throughout the club.
A few youngsters who have done well has been the positives with Jacob Lee scoring runs & Ewan Hay leading wicket taker for the 2nd Xl.
Results speak for themselves and I hope we improve next year.
Nas Ali (2nd XI Team Captain)
3rd XI Team Report
Finished 5th:  186 Points  5 wins  2 winning draws  1 Losing Draw  5 Losses
An interesting season for the 3rd XI this year, with the new post covid divisions, with the aim being to consolidate from last year and stay in the division.
It was an incredibly tight and competitive division, and on the last day 3 teams were still in danger of being relegated- the 3rd XI being one of them! Thankfully we took all 24 points against a good Ward End Unity side to finish 5th!
It has been an incredibly tough season for the 3rd XI, as like all the other teams we struggled at times due to the player vacuum at the top of the club which obviously had an effect on team selection.
However, despite often fielding young inexperienced sides we managed to show real fight and a competitive spirit whether we won or lost, and I’m sure everybody has learnt a lot from this season.
As ever, some really promising performances from some of our younger contingent, backed up with some quality from our more senior members of the group!
