4th XI Team Report - Summer of 2023
Warwick County Cricket League - Division 9 West & Friendlies
A challenging season
Dark Clouds:
Most players, which played for the 4s last year went up to the 3s.
The team was playing a division higher than last year so the waters were deeper.
There were more than 35 players representing the 4s this year.
App. 10 debuts this year, most of them u/13s.
Nucleus of the team never got formed,
Unprecedented churn at the top made team selection a nightmare with selection open till Friday eve on most weekends.
6 division games abandoned due to weather, which is 1/3rd of the schedule.
Silver Lining:
Despite an unsettled look, and all of the above, the team did not get relegated.
In fact, another win would have seen the team finish at 6, rather than an 8.
Many seniors put their hands up and contributed to the team's success like Jim, Nick, Hem Patel and Darren.
Many youngsters impressed with their skills and showed they belonged like Nirek, Harry B, Sam L, Anuraag.
Some new talent coming through in Charlie Dale Harris, Rich Madden, Pranav Rajesh, Tom D, Ben D & Hassan K.
Shining Stars:
Top 3 scorers - Nirek (277) Rohit (228) Jim L. (174)
Top 3 bowlers - Rohit (25) Harr B (13) Hem P (10)
Top 3 fielders - Rohit (12) Nirek (6) Jim L. (5)
Top 5 performances Bat - Anuraag (86) Joe B. (82) Jim L. (72*) Darren F. (71) Hassan K (68)
Top 5 performances Bowl - Charlie Dean H (5/29), Rohit (5/58), (4/23) Harry B. (4/23), Ben D (4/26)
The team having started shabbily and shakily found its feet nicely mid-season, and finished quite strong with 6 wins, 2 defeats, 2 abandoned, in the last 10 games of the season (friendlies included). At one point, it looked like the team would not get on the score board at any time during the season and many a times it looked that we won’t have a team to take the park. However, great encouragement from the club leadership, a helping hand by the senior players and the junior coaching staff kept fuelling the team with talent and hope, which helped us sail through torrid times.
Better times to come for sure.
Rohit Chopra (4th XI Team Captain)
Junior Section Chairman’s Report
A junior season to again be proud of with the club organising cricket for players aged 5 to 17.  If rain had not impacted so heavily then we had around 100 fixtures to play at all junior levels including a trip to Edgbaston for the girls’ softball team.
Our 5-8 year All Stars numbered around 60 this year and we continue to introduce new players and families to the game and club on Friday nights.
During COVID we struggled to define separate teams at ages 8-11. Thanks to hard work and support from Laura Spence, Ian Rivers, and Nick Thompson we now have soft and hardball teams that cater for ages 8-11.
The under 11s finished second in their group and are largely supported by players who are under 10s so good prospects for next season.
Laura has also helped ensure we have a girls softball team competing in softball festivals which again has provided a great way of developing girls new to the game. Laura has also ensured we have indoor teams running this Autumn at ages under 11 and 14.
The under 12s competed in the under 13 Burton League and came second despite playing against older teams and in some cases under 15 girls. Thank you to Richard Seager for taking on the challenge with a relatively inexperienced team.
The under 13s have had a fantastic season winning the Warwickshire u13 championship, winning the mid-week league and only coming runners up in the play offs by 1 wicket – the final being held here at Sutton Coldfield. Thank you to Simon Matthews and Kam Fazal for their enthusiasm and competitiveness in bringing so many players into being part of such a successful season.
The under 14s lead by John Crawshaw and Jim Little played Thursday nights in the Burton Under 15 league and in the mid-week Warwickshire league.  In the Burton league the team came fourth beating league winners Rolleston in the process and second in the mid-week league. Can I particularly record the clubs thanks to Jon as he arranged and organised most of the games and practice throughout the season despite his own child not being involved this season. This follows on from his hard work and support to the club over the past five seasons, his presence and support will be missed moving forward.
I cannot mention the Under 15s season without first acknowledging and recording the loss of our Team Manager David Thornton. David passed away completely unexpectedly a few days before the last under 15 game of the season. David was a father to player Marco, husband to Amanda, friend to many, West Ham fan and general lover of sport.  The clubs’ condolences continue to go out to Dave’s family and friends.
In that context the under 15 team, which included his son Marco, should be congratulated for taking the field on the Sunday morning after Dave’s passing to play Kings Heath. Thank you to Martyn Jones and the parents of the team for their support in enabling the game to go ahead.
The under 15s finished fourth in a tight Warwickshire Championship league, three cancellations in our rain hit season probably depriving the team of second place.
Our under17 team struggled against the weather and some capable teams in Walmley and Handsworth in their league, finishing third out of four. Credit to Jacob Lee for captaining the side and the support of Mark Burford and Steve Walcott with running the team.
In terms of Sutton Junior Players representing Warwickshire:
Katie Spence and Manasvi Jaglan at Under 11 girls, Aadya Gella at Under 13 girls – Aadya should be congratulated on her award winning 5.4 overs-1 maiden-6 for 19 versus Northamptonshire in late July.
Gus Lea and Nirek Jaglan at Under 13 boys
Ben Dawson at District under 13
Will Burford at District under16
I feel that this year particular mention should made this year of senior captains’ willingness to integrate many junior players into Saturday and Sunday adult teams. It has been a challenging year in terms of results on a Saturday but the volume of juniors playing across the four teams is a real positive that should bring huge optimism for the near future. So, thank you to Rohit, Ian, Nas and Ben on a Sunday for getting so many juniors involved and seeing the rewards of junior players delivering for adult teams.  Particular mention to Nirek Jaglan for making his debut aged 13 in the Saturday first team.
Ensuring we have teams and facilities for those 100 junior games can I register my thanks to the following people:
Team managers and organisers
Coaches – Ellie Philips, George Dyche, Luke Bishop, Sam Harry, Harry Smith, Felix Haines, Jack Hurrell-Woodward, Rob Haines, Dom Lee and Parth Patel. Many of these developed and funded thanks to contributions from the Andy Biddle Foundation
Volunteer coach helpers – Ollie Claydon, Will Burford, Nick Birch, Ewan Hay.
Special thanks must go to volunteer Mike Hepburn for his work on the ground and to Lee Thomason for his flexibility and patience in arranging some of those 100 games and his apprentice Harry Lucas.
We have also hosted several junior Warwickshire games and it is to everyone’s credit that we are sought out as hosts for such matches.
The support of the main committee is greatly appreciated, Asim as Club Welfare Officer, Roy as Club Secretary, Nick as Club Chairman and Steve Russell as Treasurer. Jamie for your gardening and most importantly the occasional quiet understated compliment that is very much appreciated.
Lastly and by no means least our positive Junior parents always deserve credit for just getting players to the games and organising hectic schedules through the diary that is Pitchero. Particular thanks to those who have helped volunteer for ground works days/leaf clearances and supporting the two Warwickshire events. I must also highlight those who have helped with Friday night BBQs – we do have a budding BBQ squad, trials in the new year.
For me recent news in my family has made me review my volume of commitment to the role of Junior Chair, in many ways I would love to carry on but 2024 will probably be somewhat different and now seems the right time to encourage others to step forward to take the role if not tonight in the very near future. I plan to still be heavily involved with Friday nights ‘All Stars’ and certainly envisage being on the junior committee if asked ready to lend support and admin help as Mike Mackey, Rob Haines and Nick have done for me.
We are lucky and fortunate at this Club to have such a committed and enthusiastic group of largely volunteers keen to see our junior cricket being enjoyable and successful.  Maybe this AGM feels like a time of transition given several of us are stepping back somewhat but we should rightly be proud of the sheer volume of cricket played across the year at junior level and its potential to provide a foundation for adult cricket development at the club in the coming years.
Andy Neal
Junior Section Chairman

8.The Role of Ladies & Girls Cricket Within the Club In 2024 presenter by Peter Bell.
Why do we spend money in coaching girls if we are not prepared to offer them cricket. We have two cricket grounds including two pavilions, whereas all the other local cricket clubs who run girls’ teams have to make their facilities cater for female cricketer and they do.
Friday summer evening cricket in Rectory Park draws big crowds – parents, that in Moms and Dads, grandparents, and older siblings to watch their under-13s.
This raises a number of questions: Do we approach Moms and Dads, grandparents, and older male siblings about helping – in running female cricket sides who perhaps have been watching their daughters and granddaughters develop cricketing skills for 3 to 4 years?
We share these premises with SC Hockey Club and they run 15 very successful female hockey sides. who currently play in a higher level of hockey than the men’ team, in the National League North, and won the European Club Championship in 1990.
I don’t believe finance is a problem. Grants are readable available. To name just one; We are under the pathway of flights from Birmingham Airport and as such we can apply for grants of up to £3,000 twice a year. Provided we run cricket for both sexes. Applications are now invited for the next round of grants, which will be made in April 24 with a deadline for applications to be in by Friday 23rd February 24 Grants are not awarded for recurring expenses, such as coaching fees, salaries, rents, and utilities. However, I believe playing equipment for women and girls would qualify.
Have we ever approached our membership, or our VP, or Life Members for financial assistance. Why not?
Sports for Girls and Woman had taken off over the last 2 years, you name any sport and they are starting to cater for a female side, who would have thought Rugby and Rugby League
would have female sides, even boxing.
We need to do like-wise – immediately starting in 2024. With two grounds we would have no problem getting teams to visit Rectory Park on selected Sundays. Looking to the future there will even be an Olympic Cricket competition in 2028.
We should advertise in Supermarkets – ASDA; TESCO, Sainsburys with a different advert every month for Women and Girls to join the SCCC.  Also, we should be contacting all neighbourhood schools to advertise the Club’s activities.
(NB) There are supplementary grants available from the ECB and we need to get on the back of this. We do, however, have barriers, that may affect ladies and girls’ cricket.
(AN) There have been discussions about this at the WCB. Girls’ cricket has had problems for quite some time – there’s a lot of competition from football which has become more profile. There’s also difficulty in growing girls’ clubs – we have tried to recruit from the Hockey Club but we also do not wish to pressurise people.
(PB) The Hockey Club has 15 girls’ teams. – If they play hockey then surely, they can play cricket.
MEH The lower ground is suitable for Ladies and Girls’ cricket.
Any Other Business
(JR) There being no further business the President thanked everyone for attending and asked people to think positively and we will sort out the vacant Chairperson’s positions as soon as possible.
There being no further business the meeting closed at 8:25pm.

A Flood & a Visit from the Local Authority
On Friday 5th July one of the rooms in the Pavilion that supplied the transfer the hot water between the central heating and the kitchen burst and flooded a storage room. The water system was turned off and Butco were appointed to replace the valve. This however, could not be replaced immediately. Unfortunately, we received a visit from the local authority the follow day and our President was asked to circulate the following.
The Club was asked by Mat and Beth, Clubhouse Managers, to circulate this message regarding the closure of the Clubhouse kitchen. I’d be grateful if representatives from both clubs would also circulate this message to respective membership. I’ll forward on this message to Cricket Club Committees:
It’s with disappointment that the kitchen will be closed with immediate effect until 17th July after consultation with the local authority and the Food Standards Agency. At present we have no hot running water in the kitchen or bar area. The law is “Every sink or other such facility provided for the washing of food is to have an adequate supply of hot and/or cold potable water and must be connected to an effective drainage system.” After speaking to the local authority who had called us yesterday to advise that they are coming to undertake an inspection unannounced within the next 3 weeks gave this advice “It is not permissible to have the kitchen open if your kitchen staff cannot wash their hands or dishes in hot water.
The water must be running hot and must be of drinking quality” there’s no one more disappointed than MAB at having to close the kitchen £££ but as a catering company first and foremost we cannot have a poor food hygiene rating registered against us as this has the potential to have massive detrimental effect on the running of our business. I’d also like to apologise for the late announcement of the kitchen closure. It was hoped that our contract with Butco heating would have resulted in a repair as they attended yesterday and were hoping to come back this morning to get us some hot water to the kitchen and bar. Unfortunately, this hasn’t happened and we are now awaiting a more permanent repair. As Bar and Clubhouse Managers we are working with Matt Jones and Nick Baker to get the kitchen back up and running asap. Apologies and thanks for your support.
James Ross
President SCCC
1st Xl v Coventry & North Warwick CC 2nd Xl
Saturday 6th July was sponsorship day by the “Golden Oldies” or referred to by Gill Houghton as “The Kindergarten Group”. Unfortunately, there were no afternoon tea available to the sponsors or their guests due to the closure of the kitchen, especially as our visitors were Coventry & NW CC.
They were a Club, which the Sutton Club had played cricket against for over 100 years and were a very difficult side beat. However, today’s game was a 110-run win for Sutton, who batted first and scored 272 runs for 7 wickets when their 50 overs were reduced to 48 overs because of a delayed start. The visitors were bowled out for 167 in 35 overs, via the DL over and runs adjustment.
Looking back through Sutton’s history, there was a match played in 1984 where Sutton were the wines by 6 wickets and our president of today played a big part in Sutton’s win as did Landy Luckhurst. (see Footnote below)
Midland Club Championship- 1984
Sutton Coldfield v Coventry & NW
A superb performance from Sutton's Andrew Luckhurst, who took 6 wickets for 28 runs off only 21 overs saw the champions and league leaders Coventry collapse to 116 all out.
Luckhurst's bowling was well supported by keen fielding which maintained the pressure on Coventry throughout.
They never recovered from the earl. dismissal of their run-machine. Graham Davies, who was brilliantly run-out by Sutton's Nick Longman, with a direct throw from mid-wicket as Davies went for a most unlikely quick single.
Needing 117 to win, a fine controlled innings from Sutton's Jamie Ross, scoring 64 not out saw Sutton to a tremendous six wicket victory, giving them the same number of wins, six, as the leaders.

Footnote
Sadley, Andrew Luckhurst in September 2023 received a 17-year custodial sentence in Birmingham Crown Court after being found guilty of 4 offences of fraud and 8 of theft, involving over £20 million. Within the judge’s comments in his sentencing described “Andrew as a deeply dishonest man who quite deliberately conned (to use the vernacular) many victims out of their monies, in some cases life savings”.
A number of victims – particularly those concerned in the theft counts - were elderly and vulnerable.  Many of them spoke of you as a loyal friend, they invited you into their lives and families, and regarded you as someone in whom they could place their absolute trust.” Some were ex-players in the SCCC & SCHC.  The investment was basically a Ponzi Scheme.
Annual Visit to Lords
Our President, James Ross, on his annual visit to Lords, found the Leader of the Reform Party, Nigel Farage. MP. waiting to greet him on the 1st day of the England test match against the West Indies on Wednesday the10th of July 2024 at Grace Gate. James was wearing the Club’s jumper so that Nigel would be able to identify him, rather than wearing the MCC traditional retailer of Custard & Cream.
Nigel Farage is a British politician and broadcaster who is the MP. for Clacton and the leader of Reform UK since 2024.
He had previously been its leader from 2019 to 2021 when it was called the Brexit Party. He also was the leader of the UKIP) from 2006 to 2009.
Nigel became an MEP for South East England when he served between 2010 to 2016.
He was In the European Parliament until the UK voted to withdrawal from the European Union in 2020.
Congratulations to Captain, Jacob Squires Wood in scoring a124 runs, out of a total of 203 for 9 in the last game of the season against Harborne at Rectory Park, only to lose by 7 wickets.
The Annual Dinner -27th September 2024
This year’s annual dinner of Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club was held on Friday 27th September 2024 in the Club’s Pavilion.
This was the first occasion that the dinner was ever held at the very end of the season, two weeks after the last game rather than the last Friday in January or 1st Friday in February.
Also, the annual dinner return, since Friday 27th January 1987 to its own Cricket Pavilion as on Friday 26th January 1988 the Club move the event to Penns Hall, now a home for illegal immigrants. Since then, the Club has been nomadic about its annual dinner by holding the occasion at virtually every large venue in Sutton for nearly 38 years.
The evening was attended by 73 members, of which over 50% were active players. Also, something in the order of a dozen ladies attended, but I was too polite to count them.
The formal evening commenced with the President, James Ross saying grace: -
Dear Lord, as we gather to dine tonight
We welcome you all and believe that it is right
To thank you for cricket, the friendship and sport
And the “Spirit of Cricket” we firmly support
At grounds like Rectory Park, Stratford, Leamington and Leicester Ivanhoe
Our cricket is often a story of woe
But most of the time we have great fun
And occasionally we even manage a run
We’re lucky to be here in such a great place
To have good food and wine to indulge after grace
And please bless our Club and those that we play
and next year, if possible, keep the rain away
Taken from a quotation by C.B. Fry
The catering was run by our resident function manager, Mat Scott and his company MAB Catering and dinner commenced with Soup with Crotons with a 30 minute for awards to be made.

Chapter XXlll – 2025 Season
Disable Parking & Emergency Entrance to Playing Area
BCC – Parks Department have after much persuasion, marked out in the car parking area behind the Pavilion with two disable parking bays, and the emergence entrance to the playing areas.
Pre-Season Activity
The new season started with the Lower Ground having a new artificial playing surface. £7,200 has been spent on the lower ground with a new astroturf pitch. This replacing one that was laid down in 1980’s. Also, extensive work has been carried out refurbishing the nets at a cost of £10,200.
We have been fortunate in securing grants towards these two facilities. Further improvement to the ground costing around £1,000, in pruning back some of the trees with overhanging branches and hedges. Mostly to the lower ground playing area
The Top Ground Covers
On the 1st May new covers arrived at a cost of £4,000 for the top ground & paid by the Club.
The Pavilion
Members and visitors alike, will have noticed that our Pavilion-Clubhouse has and is still being spruce up, both externally and internally thanks to the joint efforts of the clubs. (The S.C. Cricket and Hockey Club Limited). Every effort will be made to get all apocopate history back on the wall where applicable.

Saturday 31st May 2025. All 4 Teams Won.
It’s many seasons since all four of the Club’s Saturday matches have all recorded outstanding wins. We have to look back as far as the 1980’s and 90’s to find four winning performances on the same day, but no official record was noted.
The 1st team (WCL Div 1) at home entertained Solihull Municipal 1st lost the toss and were put into bat on a very good pitch and scored 269 for 7 off their allotted 50 overs. Felix Haines and Oliver Gray were both joint top scorers with 44 runs.
Municipal in their reply were all out in 41.1 overs for 165 runs with Felix Haines taking 3 for 16 runs in 6.1 overs and Sutton had won by 104 runs and claimed the 20 match points
The fielding was good but the Sutton wicket keeper, Nirek Jaglan had an outstanding game, only conceding 1 bye and taking 2 catches. His second catch, was a very fast run towards square leg to get under a skied ball from Municipal top scorer, when he had accumulated 67 runs, including 6 fours and 1 six.
The 2nd Team (WCL Div 6 West) away to Solihull Municipal at Brick Kiln Lane, who won the toss and elected to bat, but were bowled out for 20 runs in 12 overs. Jack Allen with 6 for 11 in 6 overs and Ewan Hay 4 for 9 also in 6 overs.
Note: - 20 runs is one of the very lowest scores, if not the lowest by an opposition team.
Sutton in their reply scored 22 runs for the loss of 1 wicket in 4.5 overs, leaving Sutton as the winners by 9 wickets and gaining 20 points.
The 3rd Team (WCL Div 8 West) at home to Handsworth 3rd who won the toss and invited Sutton to bat first with Jim Little opening the batting and scored 108 runs and not out from the team’s total of 215 for 5 wickets in their allotted 45 overs.
Handsworth in their reply were all out for 166 runs in 40 over giving the 3th a 49-run victory and 20 points. 5 bowlers each took 2 wickets with Daniel Wright finished with 2 for 23 runs and an economic rate of 2.6 followed by Ken Dunnington with 2 for 28 in 7 overs and an economic rate of 4.0.
The 4th Team (WCL Div 10 West) was away to the local rivals Four Oaks Saints 4th. Sutton Won the toss and they invited the home team to bat first, who were all out for 154 runs in 41.4 overs. Two of the 5 successful bowlers were led by Shahrukh Atif, taking 3 wickets for 8 runs in 5 overs and an economic rate of 1.6 followed by Charlie Dale-Harris with 3 for 21 runs in 4 overs at an economic rate of 3.5.
Sutton in their replay quickly knocked the runs off in 24 overs without losing a wicket in an opening partnership of 158. With Ian Hay not out on 88 and his colleague Qasim Arif not out on 53.  Winning a local derby by 10 wickets is most pleasing as well as collecting the 20 points. The 4th team, originally the Owls, have created a new record opening partnership.

As someone who studied maths – many years ago, I thought I would give a statistic overview of Saturday 31st May 2025 quadruple victories. I can’t find any record of all four Saturday sides winning their games on the same day.
I believe and do recall that it did occur in the 1980’s or 90’s, when the playing strength of the club was exceptionally very strong and with many high-quality players within the club. Nevertheless. Saturday’s results are now established in the club’s history, in detail and with a mathematical average valuation: 51.1 to 12.6, a difference of 38.5 between Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club and our Opponents, or as a Ratio of 4 : 1.
Please to also record that the Chairman and President were proud to acknowledge a notable achievement in SCCC’s long and proud history by buying four jugs of beer between them.
Purchase the Freehold Information Note ? : -
The Cricket Club should consider purchasing the Freehold of our Ground Lease from BCC of 8.33 acres. The contact within BCC is, according to Councillor David Pears, an Eddie Curry. BCC is selling off all their assets in Sutton Coldfield. Now is the time to buy the property we have leased the area we play on since 1847 and we own with the SCHC the Pavilion, but we own all other buildings on the ground.
Eddie Curry is the Interim Head of Parks at Birmingham City Council. He has a long history in public parks and green spaces, with over 25 years of experience in the field. He also holds an MBA in Strategic Management. In addition to his practical experience, he is a qualified landscape professional with an OND in Landscape Practice and an HND in Horticulture. He is also an International and National Green Flag Judge and Chair of the Core Cities Parks and Green Space Group

Presidents Day 2025
Saturday 9th August 25 was Presidents Day, and what a successful day it was, the sun shown from when the cock crowed to when the stars came out. We were greeted by the President, James Ross with a hand shake and a refreshing drink. Sutton’s 1st Xl won the toss and decided to bat against a strong Harborne side but made a disastrous start when the captain, Jacob was caught off the 1st ball in the second over for 1 run. However, Phil Dunnett joined Felix Haines and the score slowly moved to 62 when Felix was bowled for 23 runs in the 13th over. After a couple of brief partnerships, the score slowly moved to 102 runs in the 22nd over when the 4th wicket fell. This resulted in the floodgates opening as batters came and went in quick succession in 6 overs and the scoreboard had slowly crept to 120 runs with Phil Dunnett; our No. 3 batter was on 42. Our last man, James Dalton strode to the none striker’s end after having been briefed on what to expect. It was probably about running between the two wickets.
This is where Phil took complete charge as every ball he received went for 4 or 6, like hailstorms on to the pavilion roof and into the spectators’ area in front of the pavilion. This is when James became an Olympic sprinter over 22 yards when the 5th or last ball in an over occurred. 4 time he showed his speed and 3 time he faced the 1st ball in the over and again it was a sprint to the other end. Meanwhile, Phil completed his century and was caught out to a skier just short of the boundary with 116 runs to his name in just under 2 and ½ hours and with the score a healthy 194.  The partnership of 74 runs between Phil Dunnett and James Dalton, who score 3 runs and faced 7 balls, is a Club last wicket partnership record.
Harborne in their reply made a cautious start, but then they lost wickets to have 75 runs on the scoreboard when the 5th wicket fell. Wickets continued to fall as Jacob switch his bowlers around and with score of 128 the 8th wicket fell. From there on the spectators became very anxious as Harborne moved towards the winning line. However, Felix Haines came back to bowel and took the 9th wicket with a bail trimmer with the score at 179.  Ewan Hay was immediately reintroduced into the attack at the Hospital end; his first ball in his new spell sent the batters off stump cartwheeling back with the score at 179 and Sutton were home with a 15-run victory in the 48th over and 20 points.
The members, guests and spectators on this President’s Day game had everything a game of cricket can offer, concern, danger, excitement and success, as well as hospitality on a very hot summers’ day.
Peter Bell
Match Reporter

2nds End the Season in 3rd Position!
Nick Baker writes on the 15 Sep - 15:20
Well done the 2's !
Well done to the 2nd XI led by Jon Miller this year.Played 20, Won 9, Winning Draw 1, Losing Draw 1, Lost 6, Abandoned 3, a total of 259 Points.
Great batting performances from Rayyan, with an average of 58 followed by Toby Baker av49 and Regan Beamish ave 36.
With the ball the young bowling set up of Dan Wright 21 wkts, Sam Little 19 wkts and Harry Baker 18 wkts all did well and Jack Allen's 6-11 against Solihull Municiple being a highlight.
Sutton 3rd Xl Win Promotion from Division 8 West
With one match in this cricket season to go, in the 8 West League, Sutton have gained automatic promotion as runner up in their league with a resounding win by 189 runs over Moseley 4th in Rectory Park.
Winning the toss and batting first, Sutton scored 313 runs for 4 in their allotted 45 overs. Jim Little opened the batting and carried his bat; not out with 142 runs to his credit. He was helped in a 3rd wicket partnership with Darren Fry of 218 whose contribution was 94.
The 218 runs partnership is a record for the 3rd wicket by the 3rd XL and Jim’s score is one of the highest scores for an opening batsman who was undefeated.
Moseley were all bowled out for 124 runs in 37.3 overs with the wickets being shared with Charlie Dale-Harris taking 3 wickets for 21 runs.
However, in the last match of the season the 3rd Xl were away to Shenley Fields 2nd XL, and winning the toss decided to bat and were all out for 171 runs in 43.2 overs.
In reply the home side were 18 for 7 wickets in 7 overs when heavy rain caused the game to be abandoned. Sutton gained 10 points to Shenley Fields 8 points.
Congratulations to Sutton’s 3rds winning the Division 8 West league with 300 points and the runner up were Kings Heath 3rds on 284 points.

Sutton 4th Xl Win Promotion from Division 10 West
Congratulations also to the 4th Xl for promotion as runners-up in the 10th Division West League.
Sutton 4th are runners up in Division 10 West to Bridge Trust OB 2nd Xl. The seasons results were based on a percentage points system as two grounds were unfit or not available witch effect four teams including Sutton game away to Pickwick. The points were 17.11 to 15.77 and the points were 308 to 268, if Sutton’s match against Pickwick had been played and won by Sutton the league positions would have remained unchanged.
However, the last game of the season for the 4th was against Bridge Trust OB’s and was an outstanding win for Sutton by 9 wickets, having bowled the OB’s out for 89 runs in Rectory Park in 20.3 over. Sutton in their reply scored 91 runs for the loss of one wicket in 9.2 overs gaining 20 points with the OB’s receiving nil points.

U17s Warwickshire T20 Semi Final
Nick Baker15 Sep - 15:37
Sutton lose out to Moseley in a wet and windy encounter
On a very wet and windy morning at Rectory Park, following the end of season celebrations, the U17 team took on Moseley in the Warwickshire T20 Blast Semi Final.Sutton put a Moseley team with several Warwickshire Under-18 players into bat.
An early wicket from skipper Ewan Hay, bowing the county opener set the tone, excellent in the field Sutton pressed on with 2 more quick wickets from Harry Baker, who nearly competed a hattrick.
In the field, led by Gus Lea behind the stumps, Marco Thornton, Harry Matthews, Henry Eustace, Tom Dudley and Dan Wright all committed 100%Moseley kept the scoreboard ticking over very well and recovered from 40-3 to a final position of 170-6.
With the bat the pressure of the inbound rain storm meant SCCC had to achieve a minimum strike rate of 8.6, falling a fraction short despite some great hitting from Dylan Jones, Sam Little and Ewan Hay.SCCC made it to 124-8 before the heavens opened and closed the 2025 season.
Well done to a great team of promising players.
Thanks also to the team of volunteers who helped out in the morning and to DJ Haines who kept us all entertained
The Annual Dinner -7th November 2025
For the second year the Club’s Annual Dinner was held on Friday 7th November 2024 in the Club’s Pavilion.
The event was attended by some 80 members, which included active, past players and Ladies. MAB Catering, the SCC&HC’s resident Pavilion Manager produced a three-course meal, which includer a hot and slightly spice soup, a chicken course and a cheese board.
The evening commenced with the Chairman Nick Baker, giving a brief welcome and a rundown on the successes of all the teams – The 2025 season was probably the most successful playing record across all teams the Club has ever had in its 178 years
The formal evening commenced with the President, James Ross saying grace: - A repeat of the grace of last year’s Annual Dinner
The Chairman highlighted the success of the Clubs teams and thanked a number of members for their efforts in the voluntary performance behind the Club’s success: -
Iin particular Friday evenings when over 100 youngsters attended the various age groups coaching sessions.
Over 25 games were played by Under-13’s and Under-14’s on Sunday mornings
The Under 15’s  Played 8 games; won 5; lost 2;& 1 no result because of rain.
The Under 17’s- getting into the Warwickshire County T20 Cup semifinal.
The Chairman also thanked the following members for their efforts; -
Andy Neal   –     Junior Coach
Mike Hepburn – Ground Chairman
Peter Bell    –     Girls Cricket
Nick concluded by informing his audience that the awards would be made in between the dinner courses with the 4th and 3rd teams first and the second interval would be the 2nd and 1st teams.
4th Team Awards
This was made by the captain, Martin Jones who gave a few details about the success of the team in gaining promotion from the 10 West WCL, in particular that 9 players made their debuts in the 4th team of which 7 were under 17 years of age.
Commenting on the batting, Martin referred to the team having 14 significant partnerships over 50 runs out of 17 matches, with 5 personal 50’s and 1 player; Toby Baker with 104 runs to his name as well as scoring 70 runs in another game. Martin went on to say that: - Ian Hay had scores of 96 and 88 both not out, Arslaan Raza 77, Dylan Jones 68 no, Jim Spencer 67 no, Harry Matthews 57 and Marcus Smith 56 runs.
In the bowling, three players took 5 wickets; Harry Matthews for 23, Dan Thacker for 31 and Henry Eustace for 39 runs.

In the fielding department he was one of the best fielders in the Club. His run out throw-in from cow-corner boundary was magnificent, which will be remember for a long time
Ksitij was an outstanding player in all three departments of the 3rd team game this 2025 season and gets my award.

The Sunday League Awards.
When the dinner’s main course was completed, the Sunday team, who play in the Arden Premier Sunday League was presented by Ben Dunnington. However, Ian Hay intervened Ben to explain that many of the teams in this league have high quality players. He went on to say, that many of the clubs who only play Saturday Cricket in the senior midland cricket leagues, plus overseas players and don’t play Sunday Cricket competed for clubs in the Arden Premier Sunday League for financial reward. He then gave an example; that one of these players was paid £100 and a £1.00 for every run scored in a game, so the standard of cricket is very high. As the Club’s reporter I thought Ian’s interjection was very informative.
The Captain mentioned that the team finished 4th out of the 8 team in the Premier Division a very creditable efforts by all players who were available for Sunday Cricket. Proposed Constitutional Amendments — raised by Peter Bell
Title and Management Body (Clause 1. Title)
Amendment: Replace the words The Limited Company is controlled by a General-Purpose Committee (GPC)" with The Limited Company is controlled by 'The Council or Council of Management (CoM)
Membership Application Display (Clause 3. Membership)
Issue: The constitution states that application details (name and seconder) shall be displayed in the "Clubhouse's.
Context: The Cricket Club uses a Pavilion and a Lower Ground Pavilion, not a Clubhouse, as the boxing clubhouse is located elsewhere'. The lease with BCC also refers to the SCC&HC headquarters as the Pavilion
Amendment: Change the wording to remove the requirement for application/seconder details to be displayed. Any other reference to Clubhouse can be changed to Pavilion.
Management Committee Voting (Clause 7. Management Committee)
Issue: The constitution states: "Only these members of the Committee shall be entitled to vote at Committee Meetings".
Implication: It must be assumed that those not on the committee, but in attendance (e.g., Life Members and perhaps V.P.'s), cannot vote. They should still be advised of such meetings
8.   Any Other Business
Womens and Girls Cricket
Memorandum of Association: All SCCC members need to be made aware that they are governed by the Memorandum of Association with SCC&HC Limited, dated 6th December 1974. This document needs to be updated, and the Cricket Club must have an input in the replacement.
Action: NB involved in this; noted to AGM attendees.
Membership Liability: Membership numbers need to be reported to the SCC&HC Limited due toa £1.00 members liability clause'.
Action: NB to advise SCCHC.
Meeting Minutes Circulation: There is a concern that the Cricket Club has become a "secret society" because minutes are not circulated
Heritage Display: Why have the club's heritage items not been returned to their original place in the Pavilion after decoration, rather than being placed in the corridors? Many members find the corridors an inappropriate position
Freehold Acquisition: Acquiring the freehold of the 8.23 acres from BCCC

Peter Bell proposed that the treasurers report be accepted.
Nothing positive resulted regarding the item raised by Peter Bell in the latter part of the agenda although he did point out that his knowledge on young girls’ cricket was supplied in writing by Paul Long a parent. As there were insufficient Girls to make a team. This appeared to be the case in the regarding the WCCC.
The next item was regarding the circulating of minutes as Peter described the Committee Meetings were being run by a secret society, as minutes had not been circulated for two years. Nothing positive came out of that topic.
John Robinson raised an issue concerning the Cricket Club’s photographs & its heritage items had been removed from the Pavilion’s internal walls to be painted and were now displayed in the corridors leading to the toilets and the players’ entrance which John considered it was an inappropriate resighting. Nothing positive resulted, other than the asbestos in the walling was raised by Ian Hay who obviously nothing about the construction of the building in 1974. However, the retiring chairman, Nick said that was a matter that should be addressed to the SCC&HC Limited.
The issue of acquiring the Freehold of the 8.23 acres from BCC was never raised other than the issue of a car had driven through the roadway fence into our leased playing area a vehicle went through the fence, and travelers had been seen in the park so Peter Bell informed the members that he had paid £350 to have the fence repaired with the post being set in concrete. Mr. Haines enquired, why had we not involved our insurers. Peter informed the meeting that the last Park’s manager John Porter had informed the SCCC that the fence would not be replaced by the BCC and that is why the FoRP and some from the SCCC planted 1,000 whips along the roadway fence only for someone to spray them with a week killer and for the mowers to removing them altogether. Peter also mentioned that he had met up with a lady who works for a French company in the planting of 500 whips in the Newdigate Road Park and he would follow up.
Absent from the meeting was a Ground Chairman report and no Committee members were elected or 1st team captain, and Peter criticized that our website was out of date in particular the 10 documents; (such as the 2021 AGM & the revised AGM of 2021, the New Year 2022 and the minutes of last AGM of 26th October 2023).
Peter Bell wrote to the president the next day to express his disappointment in the outcome of the meeting and that he would see that an EGM took place before the 2026 season commences.
That evening, John Robinson rang Peter on behalf of the President to ask him not to say or do anything regarding the AGM or EGM as it was important the new committee settle in to their new posts. Peter reluctantly agreed.
John W. Ratledge
John Ratledge was Born on 13th January 1932 and Died on 17th November 2025, aged 93.
According to the Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club annual fixture cards, John joined the club as a player in 1960 and continued playing until the end of the 1967 season, before he was recorded in the none playing list in the 1968 fixture card.
in 1962 John was elected captain of the Owls, (4th team on Saturdays). There is a note in the club’s history that the Owl’s played 20 games - won 12, lost 2, with 8 games either, not being played because of the weather, or abandoned because of rain and the history qualified this by stating no drawn games. In 1963 John was elected Hawkes captain (3th team on Saturdays) which he did in the 1964 and 1965 seasons.
In 1964 John was elected on to the General Committee and remained on that committee for 4 years 1964 – 67 seasons. However, in 1962 John assisted A. D. Whittaker, the Social Secretary in the organising the Annual Dance in the Town Hall; A. D. Whittaker and John Ratledge made £100 profit. In the 1994 at the Annual Dance the same two gentlemen achieved a profit of £131. These two events were recorded by Sharp in the club’s history. As it was an annual dance; I assume he same two gentlemen also organised in 1993 dance but it may not have been as profitable and therefore not recorded. My personal diary said; I also attended the event in 1963.
I can remember John was a very stronger supporter of the “Festival Shield” and was present for virtually every game for what is now called in world-wide cricket; “20 twenty”
John Ratledge
John Ratledge was very much involved with the replacement of the thatched roof pavilion according to the writings of Norman Sharp in 1964 “The Pavilion was considered to have a limited life left and that the time was ripe to go ahead with plans for a more modern building. Accordingly, a sub-committee was appointed to review the prospects. This committee was formed to consider a new Pavilion consisting of the Hon Secretary, G.B. Thompson, D. Alford (Quantity Surveyor), L.O. Tippins (Architect), D.P. Locker (Solicitor), W.N. Houghton (Captain), and J. W. Ratledge. The latter gentleman brought a business man’s approach and expertise to this committee. However, I wonder in days world (2025) what did Norman have in his mind with to words “the time is ripe.” Looking back at the fixture cards of 1960 to 1965, the total membership associated with the cricket club totaled 351 in the 1962 fixture card. See the table below. Norman probably considered that with such a large membership, loans, gifs and grants would cover a typical cost of a new pavilion of £9,000 based on lots of £10. In 1974 when the Pavilion opened the risen to figure between £12,00 to £15,000.

Looking Back on the Early Formation of Cricket Clubs
When you start to look back into the historical past, all sorts of information come to light. No more so, with the number of players who have played over the 178 years since the Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club was formed in 1847. That was before the Warwickshire County Cricket Club was officially formed on 8th April 1882, at a meeting in The Regent Hotel, Leamington Spa, by William Ansell to serve Birmingham's growing population, with the club establishing its home at Edgbaston in 1885. The Sutton Coldfield Cricket Club was believed to be the fourth Cricket Club to be formed in the Birmingham. Further research produced the following overview on Midlands Cricket
Several local cricket clubs were established in the Midlands well before the formal county clubs were founded in the mid-to-late 19th century.
Earliest Recorded Midlands Cricket Clubs
Walsall Cricket Club: The club claims a history dating back to 1812 and celebrated its bicentenary in 2012. According to records at Lord's, the official start date is noted as 1833.
Enville Cricket Club: Cricket has been played at the Enville Hall ground in Staffordshire since at least 1821, the year of the first recorded game held to mark King George IV's coronation. The club was officially formed by the local landowner at that time. The ground was once described by the London Illustrated News as the "finest cricket ground in the world" in 1857. On the 23 Jul 2021 the Enville Club, celebrates its 200th birthday. Officially the oldest cricket club in the Midlands.
Birmingham Cricket Club: This club was started in 1819, with members from the town's young elite playing at Ladywood.
West Bromwich Dartmouth: This club was officially recorded as starting in 1833, the same year as Walsall according to Lord's records.
Wolverhampton: This club was formed in 1835.
Birchfield Cricket Club: This club was established in 1840.
Harborne Cricket Club: Founded in 1868 by a group of young men from St Peter's and the Baptist Church, it is now one of the largest in the Midlands.
Shifnal Cricket Club: Formed by James Leake in 1849. In 1851 an agreement was entered into between a James Leake and Thomas Clarke for the local cricket ground in Haughton.
The Pickwick Club, now located in Wake Green Road was founded in 1858, and had its own ground in St Paul's Road, as well as a fine reputation.
Coventry & North Warwickshire Cricket Club: Formed in 1851 at the club's ground in Binley Road, Coventry.
Early Mentions of Cricket
The various county cricket clubs in the Midlands were formed much later.
Leicestershire County Cricket Club created in 1820 (reformed in 1879 into its modern county structure.
Nottinghamshire County Cricket Club: While cricket was played at the Trent Bridge Inn site in the 1830s, the ground was officially laid out in 1841, and the county club was founded that same year.
Yorkshire County Cricket Club was formed on 8 January 1863 at a meeting of the Match Fund Committee which had been established two years earlier
